## Cancel Credential - Voluntary

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Cancel_Credential.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Cancel Credential -
Voluntary
Cancel Credential - Voluntary
The cancellation of a license is without prejudice. An individual whose license is canceled may apply for
a new license at any time after the cancellation. The expiration of a person's license during the
cancellation does not invalidate or terminate the cancellation. The cancellation of a license does not
affect a pending action against the person.
For a customer to voluntarily cancel a credential (either for themselves or as legal guardian or agent
action on behalf of another) they must complete the Driver License / ID Cancellation Request, form #96-
0384.
All Customers complete the top section of the form (Name, DOB, Address, City, State, Zip,
License Type, Number, Issue Date and Expiration Date)
Individuals acting on their own complete the Licensee Use section for themselves and
have their signature notarized
Agents acting on behalf of others complete the Licensee Use section, have their signature
notarized and must also have Power of Attorney authorization if not signed by the licensee
Legal Guardians acting on behalf of their minor children complete the Legal Guardian
Section and have their signature notarized
An individual whose DL is suspended and requests to cancel their DL and be issued an
extended ID is not required to complete the Driver License / ID Cancellation Request, form #96-
0384. The request for an ID on a properly completed application serves as written consent to
cancel their DL.
Cancellation Identification upon the Issuance of a Driver License
An individual may not possess an identification card and a Non-Travel Driver License (DL). Upon the
issuance of a Non-Travel DL, the Division shall cancel the Arizona ID. This does not apply to IP or
Travel ID's.
An applicant may possess both an Arizona ID and an IP
An applicant may possess a Non-Travel DL and a Travel ID
Cancellation Withdrawal of Consent

A parent or guardian may withdraw their consent at any time, resulting in the cancellation of the minor's
DL or IP. The DL or IP cannot be expired. A properly completed Driver License ID Cancellation Request
form or a written request for cancellation must be submitted at the time of the request. The written
request for cancellation must be notarized and include the minor's:
Name
Date of Birth
License Number
When the parent or guardian requests for a cancellation is submitted after the minor has
reached the age of 18, the DL will not be canceled. The following is not applicable once the
minor turns 18 years of age. Upon receipt of satisfactory evidence of the death of the person or
persons who signed the minor's application, the Division shall cancel the DL or IP and shall not
issue a new DL or IP until a new application is made, duly signed, and verified.
Cancel Credential in MAX
Policies

3.8.1 Cancellations

## CDL Downgrades

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/CDL_Downgrades.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL)
Issuance > CDL Downgrades
CDL Downgrades
Voluntary Downgrade
An applicant, whose Commercial Driver License (CDL) driving privilege has been disqualified,
suspended, or revoked, can choose to voluntarily downgrade from a CDL to a class D or M license.
Downgrading from a CDL to a lower class license will be processed as an original. An age appropriate
application fee will be charged.
If a customer is requesting a downgrade from a CDL to a Class D license they may choose
retain a motorcycle endorsement at no charge.
If the CDL applicant has any outstanding withdrawal actions that would prevent the issuance of a Class
D or M license (i.e., outstanding tickets, TSS, DUI, etc.), the applicant must reinstate at the time of the
downgrade, and pay any reinstatement fees. If the applicant is coming in from out-of-state with a CDL,
all endorsements must be entered from the prior CDL.
An applicant may choose the downgrade because:
They have a CDL medical suspension or revocation
MRP did not receive a Examination Report Form (MCSA-5875 long form)
They have no intention of driving a CDL vehicle again
DACH Downgrade
A driver with a drug and alcohol program violation, per the Federal Motor Carrier Safety Administration
(FMCSA) Drug and Alcohol Clearinghouse (DACH), is prohibited from performing safety-sensitive
functions, including operating commercial vehicles (CMVs), for any Department of Transportation
(DOT)-regulated employer until the Return to Duty process is complete.
Therefore, beginning November 12, 2024, the Division shall verify through the FMCSA DACH before
issuing, upgrading, renewing, or transferring a CDL or CLP. This verification is performed automatically
in MAX while processing the Credential Issuance service.
Example of DACH Alert - Prohibited

If the Clearinghouse reports a Prohibited status of the CDL/CLP driver's privileges, a required
downgrade will be initiated for the CDL/CLP privileges only. The Withdrawal for the privilege downgrade
will go into effect on the 30th day from receipt of the Prohibited status from the Clearinghouse.
The driver is required to complete a Return to Duty process with the Clearinghouse who will report to
the Division a 'Not Prohibited' status when complete.
The CDL/CLP drivers will be able to continue to drive on their current credential after
becoming commercially prohibited only for their personal vehicles.
For additional information:
FMCSA Drug & Alcohol Testing Regulations brochure
DACH website
Completing Return to Duty
The driver has 12 months to complete the Return to Duty process with the Clearinghouse or a
Withdrawal will be recorded on the Driver Record in MAX.
If more than 12 months has passed, the applicant must reapply by still completing the Return to Duty
process and for MVD they will be required to pay reinstatement fees and by completing any required
tests (knowledge test scores are valid for 12 months).
Prohibited Status Reported Incorrectly
If a Prohibited status is reported incorrectly to a CDL/CLP driver, FMCSA will send an updated status to
the Division as an Erroneous reporting. Upon receipt of an Erroneous reporting from FMCSA, the
Division will rescind all associated action immediately.
If a Prohibited status comes back and the applicant states they should be clear, see your
upline approver, as they will need to contact MVD Support Services.
Downgrade due to Authorized Presence
For a Non-Domiciled CLP or CDL, if the SAVE final response does not confirm the I-94 or I-94A
category of H-2A, H-2B, or E-2, the Division must not issue (including duplicates), transfer, renew, or
upgrade a Non-Domicile CLP or CDL, a required downgrade must be initiated for the CLP/CDL
privileges only.
For additional information, see the Commercial Credential topic > CDL Documentation Requirements
section.
Processing a Downgrade
Use the Credential Issuance service
On Credential tab, select Class D

If applicant has a Motorcycle endorsement, select Class M under Non-Commercial
If applicant has corrective lenses, the restriction will automatically transfer
Though it should automatically transfer, verify when processing
Continue process as normal

## Duplicate Commercial Learner Permit or Commercial Driver License

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/CDL_Duplicate.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL) Issuance >
Duplicate Commercial Learner Permit or Commercial Driver License
Duplicate Commercial Learner Permit or Commercial Driver License
A Credential Issuance service is used for a replacement Commercial Learner Permit (CLP) or Commercial Driver
License (CDL). The duplicate CLP or CDL may be issued at any time, with proper identification; as long as the license is
valid and has no open withdrawal actions.
A duplicate CLP or CDL may be issued for:
Lost, stolen, mutilated
Change of address
Name change
New photo
A duplicate CLP or CDL shall retain the same:
Class
Endorsement(s), when already present on the license
Customer will need to visit a MVD or ATP office in-person to have endorsement(s) removed
Expiration date
Customers that have both Non Travel and an Arizona Travel credential are required to have both credentials
updated at the time of renewing or applying for a duplicate license and are required to pay for all applicable
fees associated with the issuance of both credentials. This will ensure the issue dates and photos are the
same on both credentials.
Duplicate CLPs or CDLs may be requested through:
An MVD Office or Authorized Third Party Provider
CDLs only: Online at AZ MVD Now (limited to duplicate with no changes and no One Time Mailing Address)
Duplicates for Limited Term and Non-Domicile CDLs
Customers with a Limited Term and Non-Domicile Authorized Presence are required to request a duplicate credential in-
person at a MVD or ATP office.
Documents are required to be presented in person for every Non-Domicile CDL and CLP issuance (including
duplicates).
Any new SAVE results while processing the duplicate credential will need to be scanned into the customer's documents.

For a Non-Domiciled CLP or CDL, if the SAVE final response does not confirm the I-94 or I-94A category of
H-2A, H-2B, or E-2, the Division must not issue (including duplicates), transfer, renew, or upgrade a Non-
Domicile CLP or CDL, a required downgrade must be initiated for the CLP/CDL privileges only.
Required Information or Documentation
The following are required when requesting a duplicate CLP or CDL:
Travel License/Identification Application, form #40-5122
Identity must be proven and credential must be valid
"Not for Federal Identification" will be printed on the face of the credential
A new photo must be taken following the Digital Image Standards
The Social Security Number is not required, unless a name change is requested
A male applicant (under the age of 26 years) will consent to be registered with the US Selective Service System
Additional documents for name changes
Alien Registration number not required for a duplicate
Possible Additional Required Information

## CDL Entry Level Driver Training

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/CDL_Entry_Level_Driver_Training.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL)
Issuance > CDL Entry Level Driver Training
CDL Entry Level Driver Training
The new Entry-Level Driver Training (ELDT) regulations require that all entry-level drivers of commercial
motor vehicles (CMVs) receive training from a provider listed on FMCSA's Training Provider Registry
before administering the applicable CDL skills test or the hazardous materials endorsement knowledge
test.
CSRs are not required to scan the ELDT or CSTIMS results into MAX as part of the service.
CDL Applicants Required to have ELDT 
Beginning February 7, 2022, the following are required to complete ELDT:
Obtain a Class A or B CDL for the first time
Upgrade an existing Class B CDL to a Class A CDL
Obtain a school bus, passenger, or hazardous materials endorsement for the first time
If a CDL credential has ever had a Hazmat endorsement and the CDL is being renewed or is
expired, ELDT is not required.
View the ELDT Applicability Factsheet for more information on scenarios and links to the registry. View
the ELDT Applicability Flowchart for more information on applicants that are required to have ELDT for
first time CDLs and endorsements, also for those that are exempt from ELDT. View the FMCSA website
for more information on ELDT.
Required ELDT
The type of training required depends on the type of license or endorsement for which the driver is
applying:

## Renewal Commercial Learner Permit (CLP) and Commercial Driver License (CDL)

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/CDL_Renewal.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL) Issuance >
Renewal Commercial Learner Permit (CLP) and Commercial Driver License (CDL)
Renewal Commercial Learner Permit (CLP) and Commercial Driver License
(CDL)
A Credential Renewal service is provided for customers whose Commercial Leaner Permit (CLP) or Commercial Driver
License (CDL) have either expired or will expire.
Commercial Learners Permit (CLP)
A CLP is valid up to one year. If a CLP is renewed, the applicant must retake all segments of the knowledge tests.
For the CDL's Vehicle Inspection and Basic Control Skills Test, passing scores are valid only for the period
remaining on the CLP initially issued to the applicant
Commercial Driver License (CDL)
Arizona issues a eight year CDL, except in certain cases (i.e., Limited Term (Permanent Residents) or Non-Domicile
(Temporary INS) and Sex Offender customers). A CDL holder may renew up to six months prior to the expiration of the
CDL.
The eight year CDL includes CDL holders over the age of 65.
Commercial Driver License (CDL) applicants or applicants whose CDL has been expired or withdrawn for more than
one year shall re-take all applicable tests.
CDL holders with Hazmat endorsement need a valid HME Security Threat Assessment (STA) to renew the HME. View
the Hazmat Security Threat Assessment (STA) topic for more information on Hazmat information.
At the discretion of the Division, an applicant may be required to retest for a Class M, A, B, or C CDL or CLP.
Customers that have both an Arizona Travel and Non Travel credential are required to have both updated at
the time of renewal. This ensures the issue dates and photos are the same on both credentials. Customers
are required to pay for all applicable fees associated with the issuance of both credentials.
Credential Renewal Requirements
Requirements for a license renewal may include:
Either a Travel License/Identification Application, form #40-5122 or Travel License/Identification Application
(Spanish), form #40-5122s
The Social Security Number (SSN) is required
The customer will retain the same class and endorsements, and is issued a new expiration date

Endorsements cannot be added or removed during a renewal service
The applicant must a Primary document (proof of U.S. citizenship) to issue an original, renewal, or upgraded
CLP/CDL (does not apply to foreign applicants):
The CSR may use the scanned artifact (Primary document) on file (MAX/Documents, or OnBase)
If the Primary document is on file, the document does not need to be physically provided
U.S. passports on file that are currently expired, but were not expired at the time of original issuance,
may only be used for non-TID credentials and for the renewal of a non-TID CDL
Expired U.S. passports cannot be used for the issuance of an original CDL or for upgrades
Passports on file that are currently not expired, may be used to issue TID credentials, not including
CDLs
All source documents must be scanned or uploaded to the new credential issuance service, view
Document Management > Adding Documents section for information on how to add documents to a
service
If foreign customer, must provide original Authorize Presence documentation. Pulling documents from
MAX and OnBase is not acceptable
Proof of address is required, if the customer's current credential does not reflect the customer's current physical
address
If CDL has been expired or withdrawn for more than one year shall re-take all applicable tests
No Withdrawal actions on the record
A CDL renewal can be completed within six months prior to expiration.
If a customer establishes their identity for an original credential, of any type, with a valid passport, that same
U.S> passport (valid or expired) can be used to renew or obtain an original non-TID CDL.
If a customer uses a valid U.S. passport to establish authorized presence, the CSR may use the scanned
artifact on file to issue an original, renewal, or upgraded non-TID CLP/CDL.
Renewals for Limited Term and Non-Domicile CDLs
Customers with a Limited Term and Non-Domicile Authorized Presence are required to request a renewal in-person at a
MVD or ATP office.
Documents are required to be presented in person for every Non-Domicile CDL and CLP issuance (including
duplicates).
Any new SAVE results while processing the renewal credential will need to be scanned into the customer's documents.
For a Non-Domiciled CLP or CDL, if the SAVE final response does not confirm the I-94 or I-94A category of
H-2A, H-2B, or E-2, the Division must not issue (including duplicates), transfer, renew, or upgrade a Non-
Domicile CLP or CDL, a required downgrade must be initiated for the CLP/CDL privileges only.
Commercial Driver License Renewal (CDL) Fees
A fee is required for each renewal application when a CDL knowledge test is required.

Renewing a CLP or CDL
If the customer is presenting an expired CDL, due to the extension for eight years effective September 29th,
2021, they may request a duplicate credential for a $12 fee to reflect their extended expiration date.
View the Credential Renewal topic > Renewing Credential section for steps on how to renew a CLP or CDL.
When an initial or renewal issuance of a CDL, or new endorsement has been added to a CDL, a
CDL Maintenance Card needs to be provided to the customer
If processing a commercial credential and the applicant has issues with the Federal Motor Carrier Safety
Administration (FMCSA) Drug and Alcohol Clearinghouse (DACH), refer them to DACH.

## Commercial Driver License Restrictions

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/CDL_Restrictions.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL) Issuance > Commercial Driver License Restrictions
Commercial Driver License Restrictions
Commercial Driver License (CDL) restrictions are limits placed upon a customer's driver privileges. Restrictions are triggered by a number of factors,
including health issues or types of vehicles the customer is eligible to drive.
Some restrictions may be in combination of endorsements. View the Commercial Driver License (CDL) Endorsements for more information on
endorsements
Some restrictions may require a CDL knowledge test or CDL skills test to be removed. View the Removing Restrictions and Testing section for more
information on testing requirements
During processing:
When in the Taxes and Fees screen, you will need to manually collect the proper fees for the requested service, which is $12 for this scenario,
but will be processed as an original.
CDL Restriction Examples
Restricted to non-air brake vehicles
Restricted to 'No Class A Passenger Vehicle'
Restricted to no tractor trailer
Restricted to wearing corrective lenses while driving
View the Driver License Operational Restrictions Chart for an all-inclusive list.
An over-the-counter MVD office vision test is not acceptable.
If a driver is stating they don't require a B restriction, the Division will require current medical examination documentation.
CDL Restrictions
During issuance of an initial CLP or CDL, the Restriction should automatically be added
When adding a restriction or endorsement to a CDL, you will have to ensure all endorsements and restrictions are being re-added, as applicable
If adding Credential History (out-of-state CDL), you will have to manually add them
Restriction Code is shown only during issuance or on the physical credential

## Commercial Driver License Vision Standards

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/CDL_Vision_Standards.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL)
Issuance > Commercial Driver License Vision Standards
Commercial Driver License Vision Standards
CDL applicants must have binocular vision and meet the following CDL vision standards: using each
eye independently:
Visual acuity (clarity of vision) must be 20/40
Visual field (temporal and nasal peripheral vision) must be 70º or greater temporal and 35º or
great nasal
Recognition, must be able to distinguish the colors of a traffic signal (or device) showing standard
red, green and amber
View the Vision Screening topic > Binocular and Monocular Vision section for more information on what
binocular vision is.
Identifying Corrective Lens or Hearing Aid Restrictions
In order to determine if a CDL driver is required to have a corrective lens or hearing aid restriction, the
INTERstate or INTRAstate CLP or CDL applicant/holder must have a FMCSA-qualified medical
professional electronically submit current medical examination documentation.
For an INTRAstate CDL applicant/holder with a medical variance (waiver or exemption), they must
submit the paper medical examination documentation to a MVD field or ATP office, through mail, or
email. See the Submitting Medical Examination Documentation section for mailing and email details.
An over-the-counter MVD office vision test is not acceptable.
If a driver is stating they don't require a B restriction, the Division will require current medical
examination documentation.
Example of MEC & MER
"B" Corrective Lens
If the driver needs corrective lenses to meet this requirement, a "B" restriction will be placed on the
CDL.

When adding a 'B' Restriction, ensure all endorsements and restrictions are being re-added,
as applicable.
A customer uses a Bioptic Telescopic Lens System (BLS) is not eligible for a CDL, therefore,
an Arizona Intrastate Medical Waiver, form #70-0106 will not be issued.
Intrastate Driver Waiver
Interstate Driver Waiver

## Commercial Knowledge Test

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/Commercial_Knowledge_Test.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL)
Issuance > Commercial Knowledge Test
Commercial Knowledge Test
The CDL knowledge test is conducted in order to determine whether the applicant possesses sufficient
knowledge of both state and federal traffic laws and driving practices for the safe operation of a
Commercial Motor Vehicle (CMV). The knowledge tests provide no assurance of technical skill to drive
safely on the highway.
When an applicant is requesting a Commercial Knowledge Test for a HAZMAT endorsement,
the driver must complete ELDT prior to the test being administered.
View policy 3.6.2 Commercial Driver License (CDL) Issuance and the CDL Entry Level Driver
Training topic > Entry Level Driver Training (ELDT) Check section to complete the
ELDT Check.
The CDL Knowledge tests (including endorsement tests) are offered by the Division in both the English
and Spanish languages.
Depending on the type of CDL test, a certain amount of questions need to be answered correctly. A
score of 80% or higher is needed to successfully pass.

Type of CDL Test
Total # of
Questions
# of
Questions
Allowed
to be
Missed
CDL General
Knowledge
50
10
CDL Combination
20
4
CDL Air Brakes
25
5
CDL Passenger
20
4

Materials
30
6
CDL Tanker
20
4
CDL Doubles and
Triples
20
4
School Bus
20
4
The CSR must question the applicant to determine the appropriate CDL knowledge test
applicable to the request. The applicant must indicate:
The class of license requesting
The type of vehicle to be operated
The type of cargo to be transported
The gross vehicle weight rating (GVWR) of the vehicle(s)
Commercial Knowledge Test
The following customers are required to pass a CDL Knowledge Test prior to the issuance of a Class A,
B, or C CDL or CLP:
A customer whose Arizona CLP has expired
A customer who is applying for a first time CDL or CLP
A customer who is applying for a higher class CDL or CLP
A customer whose Arizona or out-of-state CDL is expired, suspended, canceled, or revoked for
more than 12 months
A customer who holds an out-of-country CDL
A customer who holds a CDL issued by a U.S. Military agency
A customer who is adding a new endorsement to the Arizona CDL
A customer who is renewing or transferring a Hazardous Materials Endorsements (HME)
A customer who does not have a valid CDL in their possession or is unable to provide proof of a
previous license

A customer who previously passed the knowledge test but did not have the endorsement issued
in the previous 12 months
A customer who is under the age of 18 is not eligible for a CDL/CLP and will not be given any
CDL Knowledge Test.
A customer who is eligible for a CDL/CLP, but is under the age of 21 is not eligible for a HME
and will not be given a HME Knowledge Test.

## Commercial Medical Requirements

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/Commercial_Medical_Requirements.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL)
Issuance > Commercial Medical Requirements
Commercial Medical Requirements
Beginning June 23, 2025, MVD will no longer accept paper medical examination
documentation for INTERstate CLP or CDL applicants/holders that were completed on or
after June 23, 2025. INTERstate documentation must be submitted electronically.
INTRAstate CLP or CDL applicants/holders may submit paper documentation through
Create Work Task or the customer may email the documentation to
MRP (medicalreview@azdot.gov (for customer)).
The Division adheres to the physical qualifications set by 49 Code of Federal Regulations (CFR) per
Arizona Administrative Code (A.A.C) R17-4-508, R17-5-202, and R17-5-204, which must be met by
every Arizona licensed driver operating a Commercial Motor Vehicle (CMV). This means, in the interest
of public safety, commercial motor vehicle drivers are generally held to higher physical, mental, and
emotional standards than passenger car drivers.
No person will be issued a Class A, B, C license, or CLP until all physical qualifications are
met.
Customer Required to have Medical Examination
The following CDL customers, on initial application or renewal, must have medical examination
documentation certifying they are physically qualified to operate a CMV.
Any person who has not been medically examined and certified as physically qualified to operate
a CMV
Any driver who has not been medically examined and certified as qualified to operate a CMV
during the preceding 24 months
Any driver whose ability to operate a CMV has been impaired by a physical or mental injury or
disease
Certain medical conditions will require the commercial driver to submit the medical
examination on a more frequent basis.

Persons Qualified to Conduct Medical Examination
The medical examination must have been performed by a professional licensed to practice by the
federal government, in any U.S. state or territory and is registered with the Federal Motor Carrier Safety
Administration National Registry of Certified Medical Examiners (FMCSA) Federal Motor Carrier Safety
Administration regulations with one of the following credentials:
Medical Doctor
Doctor of Osteopathy
Doctor of Chiropractic
Nurse Practitioner
Physician Assistant
Licensed Optometrist (may perform medical examinations as pertains to visual acuity, field of
vision and the ability to recognize colors)
Required Medical Documentation for CDL
A commercial driver who wants to obtain, retain, or reinstate a commercial driver license (CDL) is
required to have a medical professional complete the medical examination documentation to
determineif a person is medically qualified to operate a commercial motor vehicle safely.
The medical examination documentation is renewed every 24 months or sooner, as determined by the
medical professional or by regulation.
The documentation completed by the medical professional could be one or all of the following:
Medical Exam Certificate (MEC) (MCSA-5876 short form)
MEC is renewed every 24 months or sooner, as determined by the medical examiner or by
regulation
Medical Examination Report Form (MCSA-5875 long form)
Medical Variance forms
Medical Variance Forms
Submitting Medical Examination Documentation
Verifying Documentation for Accuracy
The non-excepted INTRAstate CLP or CDL applicant/holder may provide their paper medical
examination documentation to MVD Medical Review Program (MRP).
The documentation should be reviewed for accuracy prior to using the Create Work Task. However,
MRP will have the final say to determine the form's complete accuracy.
For additional information, see the Submitting Medical Examination Documentation section.
Medical Examiner's Certificate
Medical Examination Report
Incomplete Medical Examination Documentation

Verifying Medical Examination Documentation on File
There are a few ways a CSR or a customer can determine if the medical examination documentation is
on file.
1. CSR.
Credential Issuance > Requirements Tab
2. Customer.
Individual - AZ MVD Now: Either on their dashboard or by using the CDL Medical Exam
Documents service
For additional information, see Driver License & ID Menu topic > CDL Medical Exam
Documents section
For additional information, see AZ MVD Now: Individuals topic > AZ MVD Now
Dashboard section
Organization - AZ MVD Now: Using the CDL Medical Exam Documents service
For additional information, see Medical Examination Certificates for Orgs on
AZ MVD Now topic > Check Status of CDL Medical Exam Documents section
Verifying in Credential Issuance > Requirements Tab
No Medical Examination Documentation on File Talk Track

## Commercial Skills Test

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/Commercial_Skill_Test.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL)
Issuance > Commercial Skills Test
Commercial Skills Test
The Division administers Commercial Driver License (CDL) testing based on minimum federal
standards for the safe operation of different types of trucks and buses to provide assurance of
knowledge and technical skill to inspect and safely operate a Commercial Motor Vehicle (CMV) on the
highway.
A Commercial Learners Permit (CLP) holder is not eligible to take the CDL test within 14 days
of the initial issuance of the CLP and must wait:
Field office: Come back the following day to retest
Any testing, non commercial or commercial knowledge or skills test may be
administered only once per day
ATP school: Wait two days to retest
CDL skills tests are administered only by a qualified CDL Examiner, in English only, after the successful
completion of all applicable CDL knowledge tests. Upon successful completion of the entire
CDL examination process, a CDL may be issued with any necessary and applicable restrictions and/or
endorsements.
CDL skills tests will only be offered in the English language, the use of an interpreter is prohibited
Per AAMVA: Arizona CDL Examiners can conduct a CDL skills test in a vehicle with a 'check
engine' light on. This does not affect the pre-test
If an appointment needs to be scheduled for a CDL skills test, view the Phone Services >
Scheduling CDL Skills Test Appointments for more information.

been issued.

## Credential Issuance - CDL Scenarios

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Commercial_Driver_License/Credential_Issuance_CDL_Scenarios.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL) Issuance >
Credential Issuance - CDL Scenarios
Credential Issuance - CDL Scenarios
Beginning June 23, 2025, MVD will no longer accept paper medical examination documentation for
INTERstate CLP or CDL applicants/holders that were completed on or after June 23, 2025. INTERstate
documentation must be submitted electronically.
INTRAstate CLP or CDL applicants/holders may submit paper documentation through Create Work Task or
the customer may email the documentation to MRP (medicalreview@azdot.gov (for customer)).
You cannot waive the Medical Exam Certificate Requirement. You must wait for MRP to enter the details.
However, the applicant can still test until the requirement has been satisfied.
Issuing a CDL typically takes at least two trips to MVD for a customer if everything goes as expected. Here's a summary
of generally how the flow works.
Visit 1: Apply for a Commercial Learner's Permit (CLP) and Take Knowledge Tests
Verify eligibility
Verify medical examination documentation is on file
if no medical documentation on file, see the No Medical Examination Documentation on File Talk Track
Pay fees for test(s)
Take applicable knowledge test(s)
Satisfy Requirements
Request transfer to CDL Queue
Visit 2: Take CDL Skills/ Road Test and Issue CDL and Applicable Endorsements
Verify eligibility - Applicant must have held CLP for 14 days before they can be road tested and issued a CDL
Verify the customer has passed applicable skills/road test(s) by verifying CSTIMS, see upline approver for results
Update application for CDL issuance
Pay fees for test(s)
Request transfer to CDL Queue

If processing a commercial credential and the applicant has issues with the Federal Motor Carrier Safety
Administration (FMCSA) Drug and Alcohol Clearinghouse (DACH), refer them to DACH.
Issuing CDL Applicant a Commercial Learner's Permit (CLP) (No Endorsements)
Issuing CDL Applicant a Commercial Learner's Permit (CLP) (With Endorsements)
Issuing Class A, B, or C CDL (Arizona Original)
Issuing Out of State CDL Transfer (with Hazmat)
Issuing Out of State CDL (Not Transferring Hazmat Endorsement)

## Credential Adjustments

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Adjustments.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Credential
Adjustments
Credential Adjustments
Credential adjustments (also referred to as upgrades or downgrades) are credential issuance services
where you add or remove credentials or endorsements. For example:
Adding: When a customer who already has a Class D Driver License and wants to upgrade to a
Travel ID or add a Class M Motorcycle endorsement.
Adding: When a customer who already has a Class A Commercial Driver License for Passenger
Vehicles and wishes to add School Bus.
Removing: When a customer who already has a Class D Driver License and a Class A
Commercial Driver License and wishes to downgrade to the Class D Driver License only.

## Commercial Credential

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Commercial.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL)
Issuance > Commercial Credential
Commercial Credential
CDL Fees: A Quick Glance
The Commercial Motor Vehicle Safety Act (CMVSA) of 1986 required the development of minimum
standards for all commercial drivers nationwide. However, it was the Motor Carrier Safety Improvement
Act (MCSIA) of 1999 that required the development of an endorsement for school bus operators.
Prior to the issuance of a CDL or CLP, the applicant must meet all applicable eligibility, identity, and
authorized presence requirements.
The Division shall only issue a Class A, B, or C license to an individual who demonstrates proficiency in
the English language. Proficiency is determined by the successful completion of all applicable skills
examinations. Once proficiency has been completed, a valid Arizona CDL or CLP will display
'Commercial Driver License', the appropriate DL class and, when applicable, CDL endorsements.
CDL Authorized Presence Eligibility Requirements
There are certain types of authorized presence that are eligible to apply for a CLP or CDL and must
meet the requirements for identification, age, and provide proof of authorized presence.
1. U.S. Citizen or Naturalized Citizen.
2. Permanent Residence (also referred to as Limited Term).
3. Temporary INS with I-94 (also referred to as Non-Domicile).
Additional Guidelines for Eligibility
Applying for a CLP or CDL
Once an applicant has met the authorized presence requirements and guidelines for obtaining a CLP or
CDL, further requirements are required.
Commercial Learners Permit (CLP)
Commercial Driver License (CDL)
CDL Documentation Requirements
Additional documentation will be required depending on the type of authorized presence and CLP or
CDL credential is being issued.

## Credential Duplicate

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Duplicate.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Credential
Duplicate
Credential Duplicate
There is a Credential Duplicate Service in MAX, however it is recommended you use the
Credential Issuance service instead and select Duplicate as the Action. The process
documented below demonstrates the Credential Issuance service being used to issue the
duplicate.
A Credential Duplicate is a replacement for a Driver License (DL), Instruction Permit (IP), Identification
License (ID) or Commercial Driver License (CDL) and may be issued at any time, with proper
identification; as long as the license is valid and has no open withdrawal actions. This service is also
used when the customer needs a 12 Year Photo Update.
A Duplicate Credential may be issued for:
Lost, stolen, mutilated
Change of address
Name change
New photo
Restriction that may need to be removed (non-commercial)
A Duplicate Credential shall retain the same:
Class
Unless the customer is changing their credential from a Class G to a Class D
Endorsement(s), when already present on the license
Expiration date
Customers that have both Non Travel and an Arizona Travel credential are required to have
both credentials updated at the time of renewing or applying for a duplicate license and are
required to pay for all applicable fees associated with the issuance of both credentials. This
will ensure the issue dates and photos are the same on both credentials.
Duplicate credentials may be requested through:
An MVD Office or Authorized Third Party Provider
By mail

Online at AZ MVD Now (limited to duplicate with no changes and no One Time Mailing Address)
A customer who is required to register as a sex offender, or a customer who needs a 12 Year
Photo Update, must apply in person and have a new photo taken.
Required Information or Documentation
The following are required when requesting a Duplicate Credential:
Travel License/Identification Application, form #40-5122
Identity must be proven and credential must be valid
The photo on file may be used as proof of identity
"Not for Federal Identification" will be printed on the face of the non-TID credential
A new photo must be taken following the Digital Image Standards
The Social Security Number is not required, unless a name change is requested
A male applicant (under the age of 26 years) will consent to be registered with the US Selective
Service System
Additional documents for name changes
Alien Registration number not required for a duplicate
The Legal Guardian Certificate is waived when the minor applicant is applying for a ID,
duplicate DL or IP, or reinstatement of a DL or IP, per policy 3.3.5 Licensing Variations Specific
to Minors.
The certificate is required for a IP renewal
Duplicate Credential Fees

Credential
Fee
Information
Instruction
Permit
$2.00
Legal Guardian Affidavit is not required
Identification
$12.00
Travel or Non-
Travel
For Non-Travel ID only No Fee for recipient of Social Security
Income (SSI) (with proper documentation, or Characteristic) or 65
years of age or older
Driver License
 
$12.00
Travel or Non-
Travel
Social Security Numbers is not required, unless requesting a name
change, but the credential must be valid
No Fee Credential Issuance:
Fees may be waived when the customer states they have not received the credential, and it
has been more than 10 days and less than 90 days from the credential print date and the
address has not been changed from the original application date.
View policy 3.3.6 License and Identification Fees for more information on credential fees.
Prior to the issuing of a no-fee, review the customer's record and original application to determine
whether the fee may be waived.
Authorized Third Parties, see your upline approver prior to issuing a No Fee
MVD CSRs will document the reason for the No-Fee and scan to the customer record

If a customer has a name change, view the Name Change topic for more information on required
documentation. You will also use the Credential Issuance service.

## Credential Endorsements

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Endorsements.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Credential Endorsements
Credential Endorsements
Endorsements allow applicants to operate certain specialized types of vehicles. Credential Endorsements are
indications on a customer's driver license credential that they are legally qualified and permitted to drive
specific vehicle types within their Class. Driver Licenses (DL) are issued by “Class”: G for graduated, D for
operator, M for motorcycle, and A, B, or C for commercial. If the Class M license is combined with any other
class of license, it will be added as an endorsement the current license.
For commercial Class A, B, or C licenses, endorsements indicate the types of vehicles the customer may
drive. View the Commercial Driver Endorsements topic for more information on commercial endorsements.
Motorcycle, Motor-driven Cycle, and Moped Credential Endorsement
Motorcycle Endorsement for Military Members that are Currently Out-of-State or
Out-of-Country
Adding an Endorsement
Adding an Endorsement with Three Wheel Cycle Restriction

## Credential Issuance - CDL Scenarios

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Issuance_CDL_Scenarios.htm`

Credential Issuance - CDL Scenarios
Issuing a CDL typically takes at least two trips to MVD for a customer if everything goes as expected.
Here's a summary of generally how the flow works.
Visit 1: Apply for CDL Permit and take Knowledge Tests
Verify eligibility
Submit Medical Exam Certificate (MEC) to Medical Review (Create Work Task)
Pay fees for test(s)
Take applicable knowledge test(s)
Satisfy Requirements and Issue CDL Permit
Visit 2: Take CDL Skills/Road Test and Issue CDL and applicable endorsements
Verify eligibility - Applicant must have held CDL permit for 14 days before they can be road tested
and issued a CDL
Ensure MEC reviewed and input by Medical Review (Requirement will be satisfied)
Take applicable Skills/Road Test(s) and/or enter skills tests results in Requirements
Pay fees for test(s) and application
Issue CDL
If an appointment needs to be scheduled for a CDL skills test, view the Phone Service Process
> Standard Work topic > Scheduling Appointments > Commercial Driver Licensing
Appointments section for more information.
The following scenarios reflect detailed steps for those shown above:
Issuing CDL Applicant a Commercial Permit (No Endorsements)
Issuing CDL Applicant a Commercial Permit (With Endorsements)
Issue Class A, B or C
Issuing Out of State CDL Transfer (With Hazmat)

Out of State CDL Transfer (without Hazmat)
When completing a Credential Issuance service, don't forget to print the Credential Application
from MAX. See Printing the Credential Application for more information.

## Credential Questionnaire

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Questionnaire.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Credential
Questionnaire
Credential Questionnaire
The Credential Issuance Questionnaire is a tab that lists a series of questions and affirmations for which
you will capture the applicant's responses. This is a dynamic-content tab. MAX automatically prompts
you to capture responses to appropriate questions based on several factors, including the applicant's
age, medical history already captured in MAX, and the type(s) of credential(s) the customer is applying
for. Because the Questionnaire tab dynamically generates questions based on multiple factors, the
questions asked by MAX may not be the same from customer to customer or even from service to
service for the same customer. For example, a customer with a class D driver license will be prompted
to answer different or additional questions if they later apply for a Class A, B, or C commercial driver
license.
Find examples below of many of the questions you may be prompted to capture responses for. For
some questions you are merely capturing their response. For others, the responses you capture will
automatically trigger additional steps you need to take before you can issue the credential (e.g. answer
additional questions, or satisfy additional requirements).

## Credential Renewal

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Renewal.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Credential Renewal
Credential Renewal
A credential renewal is provided for customers whose Instruction Permits (IP), Driver Licenses (DL), or
Identification (ID) have either expired or will expire.
Customers that have both an Arizona Travel and Non Travel credential are required to have
both updated at the time of renewal. This ensures the issue dates and photos are the same on
both credentials. Customers are required to pay for all applicable fees associated with the
issuance of both credentials.

## Credential Restrictions

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Restrictions.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Credential
Restrictions
Credential Restrictions
Credential restrictions are limits placed upon a customer's driver privileges. Restrictions are triggered by
a number of factors, including health issues, age, type of credential, and criminal or civil judgments.
There are dozens of restriction possibilities.
Credential Restriction Examples
Age:
Aged 15 1/2 to 16 - restricted to instruction permit
Under age 15 1/2 - Farm Waiver - restricted to farm vehicles on farm property
Health/Vision:
Restricted to wearing corrective lenses
Restricted to daylight only
Restricted to wearing a hearing aid
Restricted to driving vehicles with mechanical or prosthetic aid
Medical:
Restricted to any applicable adaptive equipment
Class M-Motorcycle Credential:
Restricted to moped or motorized cycle
Restricted to 100cc or less motorcycle
Restricted to three wheel cycle
Legal:
Restricted to drive to or from employment only
View the Driver License Operational Restrictions Chart for an all-inclusive list.
View the Commercial Driver License Restrictions topic for more information on CDL restrictions.
Prior to August 10, 2015, non-CDL Arizona credentials had differently coded restrictions.  To conform
with AAMVA standards, these restrictions were changed, view the Restriction Conversion Chart to see
the differences.
Restriction Conversion Chart

## Credential Types

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Credential_Types.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Credential Types
Credential Types
No person may be licensed by more than one state at any one time. The person should be
licensed in the state where he or she spends most of their time. If a customer divides his or
her time in more than one state, they must choose one state when applying for and obtaining
a DL and / or IP.
The exception is Arizona allows the customer to have an ID card in more than one state.
This does not apply to Travel Identification cards
It is the customer's responsibility to know if the other state allows for an ID in more than
one state

## Department of Corrections Released Offender Identification or Affidavit of Identification

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Department_of_Corrections_ID_DL.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Department of
Corrections Released Offender Identification or Affidavit of Identification
Department of Corrections Released Offender Identification or
Affidavit of Identification
A Released Offender Identification Card or Affidavit of Identification issued by the Arizona Department of
Corrections (ADC) may be accepted as proof of authorized presence if:
The card contains a photo of the customer
The Released Offender Identification card must display a digitized photo image and specific
identification information
The Citizen field must have the abbreviations "U. S." and will not be accepted if left blank or
contain any abbreviations
Upon the issuance of a credential the card or affidavit will be returned to the customer with any
documentation presented. The CSR will not punch a hole punch on any portion of the card or affidavit.
A Released Offender Identification card that displays an "SO" indicator signifies a customer
who is required by law to register as a sex offender.
Released Offender & Authorized Presence
The Arizona Released Offender Card is used to provide proof of identity. In MAX, the card is not an
option when adding Authorized Presence. For now, use the U.S. Birth Certificate option until MAX is
programmed with the proper option.

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Extension,
Temporary, or Restricted Credentials
Extension, Temporary, or Restricted Credentials
These types of credentials are eligible for a specific time frame and issued due to issues with the driving
record, or when a customer is out-of-state at the time of the DL expiration date.
The expiration date is adjusted by a CSR and in some cases may require approval.
To keep track of the issued credentials, an expiration adjustment reason must be selected by the
CSR to keep track of the type of temporary credential issued
Out-of-State (OOS) Six Month Extensions and Hardship Licenses are managed by the Driver
License Central Production Unit and require an override, or approval, to complete the service
Fees for temporary credentials may need to be manually adjusted

## Name Change

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Name_Change.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Name Change
Name Change
In order to establish a name other than the name listed on the primary identification document, a
customer must first change their name with the Social Security Administration (SSA), which may take up
to 48 hours or more to verify through the SSOLV system. The documentation required to establish a
customer's name or a name change is the same for a Standard or a Voluntary Travel ID Credential.
When a customer's name changes, the customer shall notify the Division within ten days after
the change. A name change may be reported either in-person or in-writing. The Division is
required to update the customer record when a notice of name change is received.
The name will be cross-referenced and the new name will be placed on the Customer
Record.
To change the customer's full legal name, the customer must present one primary identification
document from the Acceptable Documentation for a Non-Commercial Credentials topic > Acceptable
Primary Documents section.
In order to establish a name other than the name listed on the primary identification document, the
customer must also submit a certified copy of one of the following to authorize the name change:
Marriage License or Domestic Partnership Certificate (used to change last name and the option to
change middle name to maiden name); if an electronic marriage certificate is submitted, view the
Electronic Marriage Certificate section for more information
Marriage certificates are not required to be recorded with their respective agencies before
submitting it for a name change
Divorce Decree (cannot be used to change first name)
It is not required that the Divorce Decree states 'restore last name'
Adoption Decree
Certificate of Citizenship or Naturalization
A court order changing the name
Or two forms of permissible name usages listed below (used to change last name and the option
to change middle name to maiden name)
Driver License, ID, or Instruction Permit issued by any state or US Territory
Social Security Card

US Military Active Duty/ Dependent ID Card
US Armed Forces Driver License
US Department of Veterans Affairs Card
Medical Insurance ID Card (including AHCCCS)
Federal Bureau of Prisons Release Identification Card
Unexpired or Expired U.S. passport
To change a customer's middle name to their maiden name, the customer must provide two forms of
permissible name change documents, view Arizona DL/ID Requirements, form #40-5144 for a list of
acceptable documents.
Electronic Marriage Certificate
When the primary identification document is an I-94, the name shall be obtained from the
customer’s foreign passport, Visa, or Arizona Refugee Medical Assistance Card.
A customer may also need to supply two forms of any of the following permissible name usage

## Knowledge Testing in MAX

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Non-Commercial Testing/Knowledge Testing in MAX - NEW.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Non-Commercial
Testing > Knowledge Testing in MAX
Knowledge Testing in MAX
Knowledge testing (all test types) is now available directly in MAX and no longer Idemia's AutoTest
software.
The basic flow of this process is:
1. Start the credential issuance service, collect fees and complete everything you would normally
complete up to the Knowledge Test.
2. In the Requirements tab send the Knowledge Test to the test station (or print a paper test).
3. Go to the customer's record and add an Assisted Self-Service Invite (ASI) and print out the invite
code and PIN to give to the customer.
4. In the test area, the customer will log into AZ MVD Now as a Guest, using the ASI you provided
them.
5. The test will be the only option available for them to take from the testing computers.
6. The test will automatically stop when the tester has hit the threshold to either pass or fail the test.
7. Test will have the option to see results and review test answers.
8. Once the test is completed the results will be automatically updated in MAX.
Offered Languages for the Knowledge Test
English
Arabic
French
German
Italian
Korean
Mandarin
Russian
Spanish
Tagalog
Vietnamese

Important Details:
The customer can only take the test at MVD or ATP test computers. The ASI will not work on any
other device
Permit Test at Home is the only exception, and it is also now using the new test format in
MAX
AZ MVD Now will automatically log out after 10 minutes of inactivity on the testing system. Make
sure the customer knows this
CSRs are responsible for monitoring the testing area. This is done by visually scanning the
testing area periodically.
If a customer is observed using inappropriate assistance, the CSR will politely and privately
terminate the testing and inform the customer they were using inappropriate assistance in
completing the test.
Inform the applicant they are eligible to retest the following business day.
For CDL Knowledge Tests ONLY: View the Commercial Knowledge Test topic > Applicant is
Observed Cheating section on how to handle.
View the Non Commercial Operator Knowledge Test topic > Verbal Knowledge Testing section for more
information on how to handle a customer who requests a verbal knowledge test.

## Motorcycle Knowledge Test

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Non-Commercial Testing/Motorcycle_Knowledge_Test.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Non-Commercial Testing > Motorcycle
Knowledge Test
Motorcycle Knowledge Test
Testing consists of 25 questions, of which 20 questions must be answered correctly. A score of 80% or higher is needed to
successfully pass.
All knowledge test results related to the credential issuance process must be entered into MAX where they will be associated
to the customer record to satisfy the requirements for those services when they are started.
A Class M IP is valid for seven months (unless authorized presence requires a shorter period of time) and no more than two
Class M IPs may be issued to the same person within a 24 month period.

We will honor the fees and test results.

Must have Class M IP in their immediate possession
Cannot operate a motorcycle on a controlled access highway or on a public highway from sunset to sunrise or when
there is insufficient light to clearly discern a person and a vehicle on the highway at a distance of 500 feet
Only valid in Arizona

## Motorcycle Skill Test

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Non-Commercial Testing/Motorcycle_Skill_Test.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Non-Commercial
Testing > Motorcycle Skills Test
Motorcycle Skill Test
The Division administers ALMOST, which incorporates testing methods and standards developed by
Motorcycle Safety Foundation (MSF). View Motorcycle Skill Test Examiner Training in the MVD One
Source New Hire Training website for more information on what is required to become a certified
motorcycle skill test examiner.
View the Non Commercial Skills Test topic for more information on:
Requirements Prior to Issuance
Vehicle or Medical Restrictions While Driving
Additional restrictions specific to motorcycles: restrictions may be imposed with respect to
areas, locations, highways within this state, or the time of day or night the customer will be
permitted to operate a motor vehicle. Medical or disability restrictions must be noted on a
license application after the skills test has been completed. Restrictions must be entered on
the Customer Record
View the Motorcycle Knowledge Test topic for more information on:
Types of Tests Required for a Credential
Satisfying Motorcycle Knowledge Testing
View the Credential Endorsements topic for more information on endorsements.
Fees paid by the customer are valid until the original credential has been issued, after testing
has been completed.
Test results that are from the Division are valid for life and do not expire.
Protective Gear for Motorcycle Skills Test
Alternative Motorcycle Operator Skill Test (ALMOST)
Special Motorcycle Skill Test (SMST)

When the customer performs the driving examination with a motorcycle, motor driven cycle,
or moped with a maximum piston displacement of 100cc or less; the Division shall restrict the
license to the operation of a motorcycle, motor driven cycle, or moped with a maximum piston
displacement of 100cc or less, view the Driver License Operational Restrictions Chart.
Accordingly, when a customer presents a MSF card that reads '100cc<' stamped in red, a
restriction must be added to the customer's driving record.
When the customer successfully completes the ALMOST or SMST using a three-wheel
motorcycle or a motorcycle with a sidecar or ATV (three or four wheel), the processor shall
place a 'Three Wheel Cycle' restriction on the driver license and customer record.
The 'Three Wheel Cycle' restriction may be removed when a customer successfully
completes the ALMOST or SMST using a two-wheeled motorcycle or by removing the M
endorsement.

## Non Commercial Operator Knowledge Test

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Non-Commercial Testing/Non_Commercial_Knowledge_Test.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Non-Commercial
Testing > Non Commercial Operator Knowledge Test
Non Commercial Operator Knowledge Test
A knowledge test determines the customer's ability to read and understand official traffic control devices,
knowledge of safe driving practices and the traffic laws of Arizona.
Prior to administering any knowledge test the applicant must meet all eligibility, identity, and authorized
presence requirements. Testing consists of 30 questions, of which 24 questions must be answered
correctly. A score of 80% or higher is needed to successfully pass.
All knowledge test results related to the credential issuance process must be entered into MAX where
they will be associated to the customer record to satisfy the requirements for those services when they
are started.
The Division offers American Sign Language (ASL) Interpreter for the noncommercial tests,
view the American Sign Language (ASL) Interpreter Request topic for the process to request
an interpreter.
Fees paid by the customer are valid until the original credential has been issued, after testing
has been completed.
Test results that are from the Division are valid for life and do not expire.
The customer paid for and passed their permit test on July, 3rd 2020. They are here today to
get their permit issued. We will honor the fees and test results.
Class G Permit Restrictions Flyer, form #15-1037P has been created to hand to customers
when issuing their Class G Instruction Permit.

## Photo First Work Around

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Photo_First_Work_Around.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Photo First Work
Around
Photo First Work Around
Photo First is an MVD policy requiring an updated photo for all customers requesting new, duplicate, or
renewal credentials. MAX requires a new photo be taken and restricts your ability to complete a
credential service until a new photo is in place. However, since MAX is still in development, and we do
not have access to cameras to take pictures, we have created this temporary workaround that allows us
to upload a portrait photo and a signature image from your local computer.
This work around is only needed while MAX is under development. When MAX goes live, Photo
First requirements will be satisfied using a camera in the office.

## Photo Management & Verification of Signatures

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Photo Management

Photo Management & Verification of Signatures
MAX requires a new photo be taken for any credential service. MAX will restrict your ability to complete
a credential service until a new photo is in place.
The Photo First Photo is valid for one day, expiring at midnight the day it was taken.
If credential is issued, then endorsements are added that same day, you will receive the
message, "Photo First Photo is still Valid, no photo required." You can continue without taking
a new photo.
If the service is deleted, the photo will not be valid and will need to be retaken.
A new photo will need to be taken if any correction is done on the same day, due to new facial
recognition process.

## Power of Attorney

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Power of Attorney.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Power of Attorney
Power of Attorney
A Power of Attorney (POA) allows another to act as an agent or attorney-in-fact for the grantor. The
CSR is responsible for reading and reviewing each POA to determine the limitations.
Credential Power of Attorneys
Custodial Care POA - a parent or guardian may delegate authority and power to another person
or persons, thus allowing the person or persons to act on his/her behalf regarding the care,
custody or property of his/her minor child,except consent to adoption or marriage
The custodial care POA is valid for six months, except in the case of a military member
POA, which is valid for one year
Military Member POA allows the designee to conduct business for the military member
A valid Power of Attorney (POA) may be used to sign the Legal Guardian Certificate and/or
Driving Practice Certificate see Policy 8.3.4 Power of Attorney. The follow is required for a valid
POA:
Properly notarized
Is not expired
The grantor is not deceased or has not revoked the powers of the POA
View the Forms Required for T&R topic > Power of Attorney section under Title & Registration
topics for more information on POAs and requirements.
Using a Power of Attorney to Issue a Non-Travel Identification Card
A valid Power of Attorney (POA) may be used to allow the designee to request a Non-Travel
Identification Card for a customer who has previously met all applicable eligibility, identity, authorized
presence requirements and has an existing photo on file. The following applies:
12-year photo rule does not apply
POA cannot be used to apply for a driver license or a travel credential (including duplicates)
For extenuating circumstances reach out to MVD Constituent Services
POA cannot be used to get an ID for a customer with the Sex Offender characteristic
Application must be properly signed by the designee (e.g., John Smith by Shirley Jones POA)

Driver License/ ID Cancellation Request, form #96-0384 must be completed when the customer
has a current driver license on record
View Photo Management & Verification of Signatures > Use Existing Photo and/or Signature section for
information on how to issue a credential using the existing photo.
The POA is required to be scanned with the Identification Application.
Policy
8.3.4 Power of Attorney

## Processing Department of Child Safety (DCS) Credentials

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/Processing_DCS_Credentials.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Processing Department
of Child Safety (DCS) Credentials
Processing Department of Child Safety (DCS) Credentials
The Department of Child Safety allows a DCS Representative to upload a child’s documents into MAX prior to
coming into an MVD Office to receive an Arizona credential. Documents to be uploaded:
Birth Certificate
The Office of Vital Record DCS Data Verification form will be accepted. There is an agreement
between Vital Statistics, DCS , and MVD that we can accept this document as Authorized
Presence/ Birth Certificate for a non-travel credential, see below example of the Office of Vital
Records form.
Notice to Provider
If minor is applying for a Class G IP: The only person that is eligible to sign the Legal Guardian
Certification must be listed in the Out-of-Home Care Provider's Name field on the Notice to
Provider, form #CSRO-1035A (see example below; provided by DCS)
An organization's name is not permitted to be is listed in the Out-of-Home Care Provider's
Name field on the Notice to Provider AND sign for the Legal Guardian Certificate. If an
organization's name is listed, a new Notice to Provider form will need to be submitted with an
individual's name listed
The Legal Guardian Certificate is waived when the minor applicant is applying for a ID, duplicate
DL or IP. or reinstatement of a DL or IP, per policy 3.3.5 Licensing Variations Specific to Minors
The certificate is required for a IP renewal
Application; required for an ID, IP, and DL
Printed application is required to obtain signatures for IP and DL credentials
Consent for a Restricted Instruction Permit for a Minor, form #96-0781 when minor is at least 15 years
of age and under 18 and applying for a Restricted IP. View the Credential Types topic > Instruction
Permit section > Class G Restricted IP section
Example of Notice to Provider Form
Example of Office of Vital Records Form
Credential Fees
No charge for an Identification Card
$7.00 for an Instruction Permit
$3.00 for a Restricted Instruction Permit
$25.00 for a Driver License

Prior to Office Visit
1. In AZ MVD Now (AMN) , the DCS rep will search or create a record for the child.
2. The DCS rep will upload the child's documents into AMN.
During Office Visit
1. To search in MAX, click Advanced Search.
2. Type the child's name and DOB in the in the appropriate fields.
3. Click Find.
4. In the customer record, click the Documents icon to review and verify the child's uploaded
documents.

5. Click the link or the download icon for each document to review and verify.
The Notice to Provide this will let the CSR know that the minor is under DCS care and to waive the
ID fees
6. Click Close to return to the Customer Record.
The documents should NOT be printed (e.g. birth cert, Notice to Provide). Uploaded Application
may only be printed for IP and DL services. Do NOT print or provide the documents to either the
caregiver or the child.
7. Credential Type:
Identification card: The Child is not required to sign the Travel License/Identification Application,
form #40-5122 or Travel License/Identification Application (Spanish), form #40-5122s as the
customer already completed the online legal disclaimer.
Instruction Permit (restricted or non-restricted) or Driver License: The Travel License/Identification
Application, form #40-5122 or Travel License/Identification Application (Spanish), form #40-5122s
will need to be printed and signed. If the minor is applying for a restricted IP, the instructor must

complete the Consent for a Restricted Instruction Permit for a Minor, form #96-0781. View the
Credential Types topic > Instruction Permit section >Class G IP section or Class
G Restricted IP section
8. If the credential application is not uploaded into the Customer's Documents, have the customer complete
the application and upload into MAX.
9. Take the child's photo.
10. Process the customer's credential as normal.
11. If applying for an Arizona Identification card, for a minor in DCS care, there is no fee. In the Taxes and
Fees screen, click Add Reason.
Select Department of Child Safety from the Adjustment Reason drop down.
Type the ID card fee in the Adjustment Amount box.
Click Add.
12. Continue with process as normal.
Contact Maddie Carbajal at 602-712-6240 if you can't locate the customer record or not able to
complete the service.

## Reactivate Credential

Credential
Reactivate Credential
When an active credential is canceled in error this is the process to follow to have it reactivated. If the
credential status is not active due to an open withdrawal or because it is expired, it cannot be
reactivated.
All MVD field offices/ATP’s that cancel a customer’s Driver License in error can now reach out to
Tech Support (602) 712-4900 to request the re-activation of that credential.
The CSR will have to provide a brief description of how the credential was canceled in error to
theTech Support agent.
As long as the credential is not expired, Tech Support will move forward with reactivating the
canceled credential.
If the credential was canceled erroneously by an out of state jurisdiction, the CSR will also reach out to
Tech Support to research the out of state issuance.

These instructions are designed for the CSRs who will be finishing the TID issuance for customer's who
have started the process on AZ MVD Now.
Eligible customers can process their upgrade to an Arizona Travel ID (TID) in their AZ MVD Now
account.
What makes a customer eligible:
Photo on record is less than 8 years old
Customercurrently holds a non-commercial or commercial credential without HazMat
The Primary document that is uploaded by the customer will need to be something we have seen

Customer adds funds to the account

proof of address
We will not do initial name changes during this process. We can keep names if they can
provide name change document or use permissible documents.(Example: Customer
uploaded birth certificate with maiden name, but we already have the name changed and
documents to support the name change in MAX or OnBase. The name will be retained even

document number is not found on the front of the card, see Foreign Authorized Presence

## Hazmat Security Threat Assessment (STA)

`Driver/Credential_Issuance/TSA_Hazmat.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Commercial (CDL)
Issuance > Hazmat Security Threat Assessment (STA)
Hazmat Security Threat Assessment (STA)
Hazardous Materials (hazmat) pose an inherent risk when being transported on highways. Any
accidental spill can impact the health of people, animals, and the environment, sometimes for months,
or even years after the incident. Hazmat employers and employees are required under USDOT to
complete in-depth security and safety training. Additionally, due to this potentially very high risk, a driver
cannot qualify to transport hazardous materials if they pose a threat to the security of the nation.
Background Checks
The U. S. Patriot Act requires the Transportation Security Administration (TSA) to complete a Hazmat
(HME) Security Threat Assessment (STA) for a background and fingerprint-based check before a
HazMat endorsement is issued.
When a customer applies for a Hazardous Materials Endorsement (HME), the customer must first
complete a HME Security Threat Assessment (STA). They will apply in person or via Department of
Homeland Security/Transportation Security Administration website (universalenroll.dhs.gov).
Once approved, the background check is valid for up to five years, regardless of how long the
actual CDL credential is valid.
Background Checks on Renewal HME Applicants
If the customer's background check is expired and they are renewing it they will apply again with the
Transportation Security Administration.  They will need to return when the background check is
completed and clears, the Transportation Security Administration reports the Security Threat Response
information to ADOT and the approved response is manually added by MVD Support Services. View the
TSA Responses and Meanings chart for more information on the approved responses.
The customer can go to the Department of Homeland Security/Transportation Security Administration
website to check status (universalenroll.dhs.gov).
Out-of-State CDL Holders with HME
When an out-of-state driver applies for a commercial license with a Hazmat endorsement, and they
currently have that endorsement, the HME Security Threat Assessment (STA) needs to be updated in
MAX with the current TSA Security Threat Response. When you send a customer away to complete

their CDL knowledge test(s), have an upline approver submit a Service Portal ticket to Tech Support to
have the CDL Helpdesk make an inquiry with STA/Hazmat.
View the Credential Issuance - CDL Scenarios topic > Issuing Out of State CDL Transfer (with Hazmat)
section for full steps.
TSA Responses and Meanings
Response
Message
Okay to
Issue
Hazardous
Materials
Endorsement
(Yes/No)

threat,
approved

No
Eligibility Requirements
The eligibility qualifications for a HME are:
Valid CDL or applying for a CDL
Must be 21 years of age or older
Application for CDL with Hazardous Materials Endorsement (HME)
Apply to Transportation Security Administration for a HME Security Threat Assessment (STA)

Must pass appropriate CDL Entry Level Driver Training requirements
Pass and complete all appropriate CDL written and skills tests, if applicable
After completion of the CDL skills test, the applicant may choose to receive a CDL without the
HME until the Transportation Security Administration approval is received to which the customer
will return to the MVD for issuance
View the Commercial Driver License (CDL) Endorsements topic > Hazardous Materials Endorsement
(HME) section for more information on testing requirements with HME endorsements.
Issuing a CDL With a TSA Hazmat Endorsement

## Abandoned Vehicle Fees

`Driver/Event_Management/Abandoned_Vehicle_Fees.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Abandoned Vehicle
Fees
Abandoned Vehicle Fees
When a vehicle has been abandoned, the owner of record will have an Abandoned Vehicle status
added to their record after the vehicle has been towed away from the place of abandonment.
View the Abandoned Vehicle Overview topic for more information on Abandoned Vehicles.
You can print the Abandoned Vehicle Fee Flyer and post it in your office.
Abandoned Vehicle Fees
The Abandoned Vehicle fee becomes an additional licensing or registration fee from the owner of record
at the time of application for a service. View the Services Available with an Abandoned
Vehicle Withdrawal section for a list of service exceptions.
The Abandoned Vehicle fee due when a vehicle is abandoned on:
$500: private property, public land, or within the right-of-way of a street or highway
$600: national forest, state park, bureau of land management, or state trust land located outside
the boundaries of an incorporated city or town
When a vehicle has multiple owners, only one Abandoned Vehicle fee will need to be paid.
Services Available with an Abandoned Vehicle Withdrawal
The Abandoned Vehicle fee becomes an additional licensing or registration fee, and the Division shall
collect the Abandoned Vehicle fee from the owner of record at the time of application, with the exception
of the following transactions:
Issuance of a disability placard
Original or temporary ID card
Duplicate DL, CDL, CLP, IP, or ID
Duplicate registration (for any vehicle registered to the customer)
Duplicate title (for any vehicle currently titled in the customer's name)

## Adding an Incident

`Driver/Event_Management/Adding_an_Incident.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Adding an Incident
Adding an Incident
This is the standard process on how to add an incident on a customer's record in MAX. If the violation is a
DUI, follow your office standard work to contact technical support. CSRs should NEVER add DUI incidents to
driver records.
Steps for Adding a New Incident to a Customer Record
1. Open MAX, click on the menu.
2. Open the Driver Record.
3. Type customer's first name, middle initial (if applicable), last name, and year of birth. Click Search.
It is recommend that you search by the customer's first, middle initial (if applicable), and last name
so any duplicate customer records can be found and a Combine Records Request, form #96-0698
can be submitted.
View the Finding a Customer topic > Combine Records Requests section for more information.

4. Scroll down and click on the Display Full History (includes Voided, etc.) box if applicable.
5. In the filter field, type in the complaint number to verify the citation is not already on the record.
6. Click on the Manage Incidents button.
7. Select Court Paper Abstracts from the Work Group dropdown menu, the Incident Type box will appear.
8. Type or select the customer’s violation code in the Incident Type field.
9. Click on Add New Incident.

10. Type the Occurrence Date. 
Occurrence Date Chart
11. Type the Received Date (the date MVD received the citation).
12. Type the Court Docket Number.
13. Type the Vehicle Plate Number.
14. Type the Vehicle Plate Jurisdiction.
15. Type the Vehicle Make.

16. Type the Court Number or click on dropdown menu to search for court number or name (use the Tab
button to move your cursor down the menu options). Once the Court Number is selected MAX will auto
populate the remaining fields in the Court Information section.
17. Select in the Citing Agency - who issued the citation: DPS, City PD, Sheriff Office, etc.
18. Type in Speed Details if indicated on the document.
19. Select Yes or No for the Accident Indicator - if not indicated on the citation select No.

20. Type the Complaint Number- if multiple charges add an 'A' after the first complaint number and so on.
21. Type the Complaint Sequence - '1' for the original charge, and '2' for an amended charge.
22. Type the Disposition Date.
23. Select the Court Disposition Code.
24. Select the appropriate answer in the Drugs Involved box.
25. If applicable, check the appropriate box(es) in Violations Circumstances.

26. If applicable, complete the appropriate box(es) in Court Ordered Information.
27. Click Save.

28. Return to the Driver Record and review the citation you just entered.
