## Commercial Withdrawal Incidents

`Driver/Event_Management/Commercial_Withdrawal_Incidents.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Commercial Withdrawal
Incidents
Commercial Withdrawal Incidents
An Arizona Commercial Driver License holder convicted of violating a state law or local ordinance relating to a
motor vehicle traffic in any state or a federal, provincial, territorial or municipal law of another country, shall
notify the Division and their employer within 30 days after the date of conviction. The only exception being not
required to report parking violations.
A CDL Class A, B or C will not be issued to an applicant who is under disqualification due to loss of driving
privilege, cancellation, denial, suspension, revocation or withdrawal of an operator license, permit or privilege
until properly restored.
When the operator license is withdrawn for any reason, any CLP issued to the applicant becomes
invalid
The applicant must reapply by paying all required reinstatement fees and completing any required
tests. In the interest of Public Safety, when a CDL is withdrawn (suspended, revoked, canceled or
disqualified) or otherwise expired one year or more, all knowledge and skills tests must be re-
administered.
When a CDL is withdrawn (suspended, revoked, canceled or disqualified) or otherwise expired one year or
more, all knowledge and skills tests must be re-administered. If a Medical Examiner's Certificate (MEC) has
expired, the customer must submit a current MEC and pay any reinstatement fees.
If a Medical Examiner's Certificate (MEC) has expired, the CDL applicant/holder must have a FMCSA-
qualified medical professional complete the required medical examination documentation:
For the INTERstate CLP or CDL applicant/holder, they must have a FMCSA-qualified medical
professional electronically submit current medical examination documentation
For the INTRAstate CLP or CDL applicant/holder, the paper medical examination documentation
may be submitted to a MVD field or ATP office or through email (medicalreview@azdot.gov (for
customer))
CDL Disqualifications
There are two type of CDL disqualifications:
Lifetime Disqualification - the individual is disqualified for life from operating a Commercial Motor
Vehicle (CMV)
A commercial driver with a lifetime disqualification may reinstate their commercial driving
privilege after ten years if they meet all eligibility requirements, see Policy 3.8.2 Disqualifications
for a complete list of requirements.
Permanently Disqualified - the individual will never be able to obtain a CDL

Lifetime Disqualification - Reapplying for a CDL After Ten Years
CDL Medical Suspension and Revocation
Failed to Submit USDOT Medical Exam Reports Incident
When a CDL holder's medical examination documentation has expired and they fail to submit valid
documentation, a 'Failed to Submit USDOT Medical Exam Reports' Incident will be placed on the customer
record.
Incapable of Operating a Motor Vehicle Incident
When a FMCSA-qualified medical professional electronically submits the medical examination documentation
indicating the CLP or CDL applicant/holder is medically unqualified, MAX will automatically add the 'Incapable
of Operating a Motor Vehicle' Incident to the record.
For additional information, see policies 3.1.5 CDL Medical Requirements and 3.6.5 CDL Actions/Convictions.
Medical Incidents with Suspension and Revocations
Upon summarily suspending the licensee's privilege to drive a CMV for failure to maintain physical
qualifications or for failure to submit a complete medical examiner’s certificate, the Division shall
simultaneously mail two notices within 15 days after a medical examiner’s certificate is due or actual
submission date to the licensee's address of record that:
Suspends the licensee's CDL 10 days after the notice's date, and
Revokes the licensee's CDL 15 days after the date of the suspension notice
A CDL will expire if revoked for one year or more. The holder of an expired CDL may obtain a new CDL by
successfully completing all CDL original-application knowledge, vision, and skills testing, and by submitting a
current medical examiner’s certificate.
For additional information, see policy 3.6.5 CDL Actions/Convictions.
For the INTERstate CLP or CDL applicant/holder, they must have a FMCSA-qualified medical
professional electronically submit current medical examination documentation.
For the INTRAstate CLP or CDL applicant/holder, the paper medical examination documentation
may be submitted to a MVD field or ATP office or through email (medicalreview@azdot.gov (for
customer)).
Revocations Specific to CDL
The driving privilege of a person who holds a CDL is revoked, the Division shall update its records to reflect
the revocation action and shall notify the Commercial Driver License Information System (CDLIS) of the
action taken.
Failure to Pass Security Threat Assessment
Federal Regulations require that all CDL applicants or holders, requesting a Hazardous Materials
Endorsement (HME), undergo and successfully complete a Transportation Security Administration
(TSA) HME Security Threat Assessment (STA) prior to issuance of the HME. If notification is received that a

HME application is not approved, the applicant is mailed a CDL cancellation notice. If the licensee is an
unexpired CDL holder with HME, the HME is no longer valid. If the CDL holder:
Surrenders the HME to the Division within days of the mailed CDL cancellation notice and is otherwise
qualified to hold the CDL, the H or X endorsement will be removed and a no fee duplicate CDL will be
issued
Does not surrender the HME to the Division within 15 days of the mailed CDL cancellation notice, the
Arizona CDL will be canceled
CLP or CDL Fraudulent Testing
If the State received credible information and the individual has not been convicted, the State will require re-
testing of the Knowledge and or Skills Testing. Re-Testing must be completed within 30 days of the State
receiving notice. Failure to do so will result in the disqualification of the CLP or CDL. Any failure of the
Knowledge or Skills Testing will result in a disqualification of the CLP or CDL. When a disqualification occurs
the CLP or CDL holder will reapply under State procedures applicable to all CLP or CDL applicants.
Identity Theft
It shall be presumed that information from the driver history belongs to the holder of a CLP or CDL. The
individual may overcome this presumption if they are able to present evidence of mistaken identity or identity
theft. A hearing may be allowed when the Division receives driver history information resulting in a case of
mistaken identity or identity theft.
The individual will submit a written hearing request
The hearing request shall be submitted within 20 days from the date of the mailed notice of action
The hearing request shall indicate if the request is based on a case of mistaken identity or identity theft
Hearing and Appeals
The customer may request a hearing with the Executive Hearing Office (EHO) within 15 days of the licensing
action date of notice.
View Hearings Overview topic for information and description.
CDL Voluntary Downgrade
An applicant, whose Commercial Driver License (CDL) driving privilege has been disqualified, suspended, or
revoked, can choose to voluntarily downgrade from a CDL to a class D or M license.
If an applicant whose CDL driving privilege has been reported as Prohibited by Federal Motor Carrier Safety
Administration (FMCSA) Drug and Alcohol Clearinghouse (DACH), they will only be eligible to driving non-
commercial vehicles.
For additional information, see CDL Downgrades.
Example of Incident and Withdrawal

Example of AZ Privilege Status
Will display as ELG in the CDL field

Compliance Fees

## Compliance Management

`Driver/Event_Management/Compliance_Management.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Compliance
Management
Compliance Management
Compliance Management is part of the larger topic of Event Management. When a customer
experiences a withdrawal (stop, cancellation, suspension, revocation, or disqualification on their driver's
privileges or on their vehicle's status), they will need to satisfy compliance requirements to reinstate or
conduct MVD services. When an incident is recorded, MAX automatically evaluates the Incident and,
based on business rules, adds any appropriate withdrawals and compliance.
Compliance refers to the actions a customer must take to satisfy requirements to remove a withdrawal,
such as paying a fee, providing documentation, waiting a period of time, or attending traffic school, etc.
Almost without exception, when a customer and/or vehicle has a withdrawal(s), you will only
need to satisfy the Compliance. Once all Compliance is satisfied, MAX will automatically update
the Withdrawal status and make the customer eligible for reinstatement or remove any stops.

Compliance Window Explained
Satisfy Compliance: Pay Fees
Satisfy Compliance: Customer Provides Required Documentation

## Compliance Within a Service

`Driver/Event_Management/Compliance_Within_a_Service.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Compliance Within
a Service
Compliance Within a Service
The MAX Withdrawal and Compliance engine automatically applies stops, cancellations, suspensions
and revocations whenever incidents are recorded. MAX also automatically adds all compliance
requirements the customer must satisfy in order to clear their path to completing services at the MVD. In
many cases, the compliance the customer must accomplish is out of your control. For example, the
customer must contact the court, or, the customer must wait a certain amount of time, etc. In these
cases MAX will automatically remove the withdrawals once the court transmits the compliance data, or
once the waiting period expires. View the Event Management topic for more information.
However, there will be times when you will be able to work with the customer to satisfy their compliance
requirements immediately. If the customer is required to pay a fee, for example, MAX will prompt you
with the option to collect the fee(s) before allowing you to proceed with the service. Similarly, if the
customer must provide documentation, you may be able to receive those documents, and satisfy the
compliance requirement.
This tutorial assumes you have a working knowledge of how to start a service in MAX. If you need
clarification, view the Starting a Service topic.

Customer Pointer Management
Pointers in MAX are a reflection of the types of credentials have been issued, and where.
Customer Pointer Management in MAX provides for manual updating of pointers only in MAX in the event a
batch process failed. This functionality is used very rarely, and only by the MVD Support Services team.

the pointer with AAMVA. This function should only be used in the event a batch update failed.

2. Search for the Customer.
3. Use the dropdown(s) to select any pointer updates needed.

## Disposition Codes & Incident/Withdrawal Statuses

`Driver/Event_Management/Disposition_Codes_&_Statuses.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Disposition Codes
& Incident/Withdrawal Statuses
Disposition Codes & Incident/Withdrawal Statuses
Disposition Codes are what the courts send with convictions/dismissals to indicate the courts' status on
the conviction. These are most often sent to MAX electronically through a batch job, but on occasion
may be provided to MVD as a Court Paper Abstract that can be manually added/updated in MAX.
Disposition Codes are only updated in the Incident and will automatically update the Withdrawal
status if impacted.  For example, if the court voids a conviction, the Disposition Code for the Incident
will be changed to 00 (Void.) This will automatically change the status of the Incident and Withdrawal to
"Voided."
Statuses are how MAX indicates the status of an Incident/Withdrawal on the Driver Record. These are
found and are most often managed in the Incident. In most cases, an update in a Disposition Code (in
the Incident) will automatically update the MAX status, but on occasion must be changed manually.

## Driver MVR

`Driver/Event_Management/Driver_MVR.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Credential Issuance > Driver MVR
Driver (MVR)
A Driver MVR is the printout of a driving record. The Division offers two basic types of records, restricted
(displays limited information) and non-restricted. Information printed on the MVR will depend upon the
type of MVR requested.
A MVR may display the following:
Personal information (name, DOB, residential/ mailing address, and physicals)
Current license information (class license, issue date, and expiration date)
Applicable restrictions and endorsements
Prior License history information
Outstanding Compliance Requirements
In-state and out-of-state violations
Driving history points (related to the time frame requested)
Withdrawals
Compliance
Violations and or Incidents
View Driver Record > Point Totals section for more information on how points are assessed.
Anyone knowingly obtaining, disclosing, or using personal information from a MVR for a use not
permitted by law and anyone who misrepresents their identity or makes a false statement with the intent
to obtain such information in a manner not authorized by law, is subject to a civil and or criminal
penalties.
Customer Service Representatives (CSRs) may process MVR requests for the following record types
only:
Driver
Vehicle
Mobile Home
Lienholder
Support documents, except microfilm (not issued by Third Party office)
Driver MVRs may be processed on AZ MVD Now.

The Central Issuance Mailing Dashboard > Processing Dates tab provides the current date
mailed in MVRs are being worked.

## Driver Reinstatement

`Driver/Event_Management/Driver_Reinstatement.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Driver
Reinstatement
Driver Reinstatement
Driver Reinstatement is not a separate service in MAX. If a customer has active withdrawals (stops,
cancellations, revocations, etc,) they will need to meet all compliance requirements before their driving
privilege can be restored.
If a customer's driving credential was not canceled or revoked due to a withdrawal, as soon as
they satisfy their compliance requirements their non-expired existing credential is still good and
services can continue
If a customer's credential expired while the withdrawal was in place, and they have since satisfied
all their compliance requirements, provide a Credential Issuance service
If a customer's credential was canceled or revoked due to a withdrawal, and their Arizona
Privilege Status is ELG has been reinstated, provide a Credential Issuance service
Reinstatement Fees
A customer's driving privileges will remain canceled, disqualified, suspended and or revoked until
application for reinstatement is made. Personal Checks are not accepted for the payment of any
reinstatement fee.
The following fees are required at the time of reinstatement:
Abandoned Vehicle Fees (ABV)
Non-Sufficient Funds (NSF)
Travel Driver License, when applicable
For reinstatement of driving privileges after:
A suspension or disqualification
A suspension or disqualification extension
A revocation
A revocation extension
An administration suspension (Admin Per Se)

Type of Reinstatement
Fee
Suspension or disqualification
$10
Suspension or disqualification extension
$10
Revocation
$20
Revocation extension
$20
Administration (Admin Per Se) suspension
$50
The total reinstatement amount is dependent upon the type and combination of withdrawal actions
recorded in the Customer Record. Certain reinstatement fees may be combined.
If at the time of reinstatement, Andrew's record displays two Administration (Admin Per Se)
Suspensions, two Revocations, one Driver Improvement Suspension and one Disqualification.
Andrew's reinstatement fees would total $80.
One $50. fee for both Administration (Admin Per Se) suspension reinstatement
One $20. fee for both Revocations
One $10. fee for both the Disqualification and Driver Improvement Suspension
Admin Per Se
When as a result of an administrative hearing the Admin Per Se is:
Expunged, reinstatement fees are required
Rescinded, no reinstatement fees are required
Suspension
The suspension for:
Admin Per Se
Implied Consent
Child Support Arrearage
Failure to Appear/ Failure to Pay a Civil Penalty
Minors and Juveniles
Traffic Violations

Underage Drinking
Will stay in effect until the person submits:
Implied Consent reinstatement fee
Any other applicable fees
For suspension on or after August 9, 2017, a customer will no longer remain suspended until
reinstatement fees are paid but will be required to pay the fees when renewing, obtaining a
replacement or updated credential.
Reinstatement fees may be required prior to the issuance of a SIIRDL. Whenever the customer record
contains an open withdrawal action and all requirements applicable to the open withdrawal action are
met, the processor shall collect all fees due. When multiple withdrawal actions are present on the
Customer Record, additional fees may apply.
Revocation
The initial step in the revocation reinstatement process is the submitall of a properly completed
Revocation Application.

## Event History

`Driver/Event_Management/Event_History.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Event History
Event History
The Event History will allow Driver Improvement, Interlock Unit, Record Unit, Special Projects IT, MVD
Support Services, and Tech Support to view the history of changes made to an Incident, Withdrawal, or
Compliance. The data that displays for an incident or withdrawal will vary depending on the incident type.
There are 2 ways to access the Event History:
Through the Driver Record Service at the bottom of the Driving History page
In the MAX main menu under Management, the Event History will be found in the Event Management
menu options
How to Use the Event History
1. Select an Incident or Withdrawal. If you select an incident then MAX will open the Incident History tab
on the next page. If you select a withdrawal MAX will open the Withdrawal History tab on the next page.

Incident History Tab
Displays details such as description, violation code, occurrence date, status date, and status updated by, etc.
The 'Status Updated By' field will display one of the following:
Other - indicates it was updated by a person after MAX go live
RACF - Indicates that the incident was updated by a person prior to MAX go live
Conversion - Data that was transferred from TPX to MAX
Post Fix - Script run
The Interlock will be the only unit that will have the ability to see a user name for this field.
For Medical Incidents there will also be a drop down option to see the type of Medical Alert Type that was
added or changed:
Withdrawal History tab
Shows withdrawals that are associated to this incident.

The 'Updated By' field will display one of the following:
Other - indicates it was updated by a person after MAX go live
RACF - Indicates that the incident was updated by a person prior to MAX go live
Conversion - Transferred from TPX
Post Fix - Script run
The Interlock will be the only unit that will have the ability to see a user name for this field.
The check-mark and colored row indicates a change was made. There is no meaning behind the colors on
each line.
Compliance History tab
Shows you when a compliance was updated.
2. Select Return to All Event History to view details about another incident or withdrawal, or select Return
to Driving Record to exit Event History.

## Event Management Overview

`Driver/Event_Management/Event_Management_Overview.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Event Management
Overview
Event Management Overview
In MAX, Event Management is the term we use for the process of managing Incidents, Withdrawals,
and Compliance.
Incidents are things that may impact a driver’s privileges or a vehicle’s status, such as DUIs,
failure to comply with insurance requirements, etc.
Withdrawals are the impacts to a driver's privilege or a vehicle's status as a result of the Incident.
This includes stops, cancellations, suspensions, revocations, or disqualifications.
Compliance is the action(s) the customer must take to complete a withdrawal, such as paying a
fee, providing documentation, or waiting a period of time.

## Hearings and Summary Review Overview

`Driver/Event_Management/Hearings_Overview.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Hearings and
Summary Review Overview
Hearings and Summary Review Overview
Hearings and Summary Reviews are conducted by the Executive Hearing Office (EHO). Customers
have strict deadlines to file their requests, so any requests received by MVD need to be forwarded to
the EHO immediately upon receipt.
A customer who has requested a hearing or summary review is referred to as a 'Petitioner' at the EHO.
Any questions pertaining to a hearing request (e.g., date/time of hearing, eligibility for a hearing,
etc.), the petitioner may contact the EHO by email at hearingoffice@azdot.gov or by calling
(602) 712-7737.
MVD can answer questions about how the petitioner may reinstate their license after the
hearing, as applicable.
Which Actions are Eligible for Review at the EHO?
The EHO reviews a wide range of MVD actions. Petitioners can review the notice sent by MVD
(Withdrawal Notice, Corrective Action Notice, etc.) to determine if there is a hearing right for a specific
action. All actions have a time limit to request a hearing, which in most cases is 15 or 30 days from the
date of the notice.
The EHO also reviews requests for the removal of restitution liens and the imposition of penalties
against lienholders who have not timely released a lien once it has been satisfied.
The EHO does NOT remove financial liens, issue titles, or remove DOR, DES, or TTEAP liens. The
EHO does not remove or waive nonpayment of judgment holds.
Forms for Hearing Requests
Admin Per Se/Implied Consent Affidavit (back portion), form #40-5807 (provided by law
enforcement)
To seek a hearing on an Admin Per Se or an Implied Consent
Hearing Request, form #40-5008
General hearing request for administrative DUI suspensions, or other actions eligible for a
hearing
Lienholder Complaint Report, form #96-0330

To seek a hearing to impose penalties on a lienholder who has not timely released the lien
after it has been satisfied
The EHO will NOT remove the underlying lien
Restitution Lien Hearing Request, form #40-5009
To seek removal of a restitution lien placed due to a criminal restitution order
The EHO cannot remove DOR, DES, TTEAP, or other liens.
Accepting a Hearing Request
The CSR does not need to determine if a hearing or summary review request is timely (within
filing deadlines). All request are reviewed by the EHO and a judge will determine if the request
was filed in time to get a hearing or summary review.
Petitioners will be notified by the EHO if their matter will be set for a hearing, typically within 60
days of the hearing request.
When a petitioner is ready to submit a completed hearing or summary review request form, follow the
procedures listed below. You can also accept an Admin Per Se/Implied Consent Affidavit with a
completed hearing request/summary review request section.
When the petitioner does not have a completed request form, provide one of the appropriate forms
listed above to complete. The petitioner can complete the form on site or can take the form to submit
directly to the EHO, if they wish.
When taking a hearing request at an office, the CSR will write the following on the request:
Date and time received on the request
CSR's  RACF
Office number
Submitting Hearing Request to EHO
The CSR will scan the hearing or summary review request form, along with any additional
documents the petitioner wants to submit with their hearing request, to the EHO.
Do NOT upload the request to the customer's documents in MAX, you MUST scan and email to
the EHO at hearingoffice@azdot.gov.
Scan the request and email it to the EHO at hearingoffice@azdot.gov
Photo copy the request to keep with data processing
Give originals back to the petitioner
The CSR will not mail or interoffice the original request to the EHO, as the petitioner will retain
the original request for their records.

A petitioner or petitioner’s attorney may also file their request directly with the EHO by:
Email: hearingoffice@azdot.gov
Mail to: Executive Hearing Office, Arizona Department of Transportation, Mail Drop 507M, P.O.
Box 2100, Phoenix, AZ 85001-2100
In person at the EHO: 3838 N. Central Ave. 3rd Floor, Phoenix, AZ 85012
Example of Hearing Request

## How to Link/Unlink Convictions to Withdrawals

`Driver/Event_Management/How_to_Link_Unlink_Convictions to Withdrawals.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > How to Link/Unlink
Convictions to Withdrawals
How to Link/Unlink Convictions to Withdrawals
On occasion, incidents and withdrawals will generate and will not be linked to one another. Linking is
important to help understand and correctly reflect what incident(s) caused which withdrawal(s).
The incident and withdrawal below should be linked as the 28-1321 is what triggered the First refusal
suspension. The lack of the little blue arrow next to the incident indicates there is no linked withdrawal.
1. Click on the Hamburger menu (three horizontal lines).
2. Click on AAMVA/Helpdesk.
3. Click on Link Withdrawals to Conviction.

4. Type in Customer Name or Number.
5. Click Search.
6. The Manage Withdrawal Conviction Details window will populate. Click on the drop down arrow under
Withdrawals.
Withdrawals are in chronological order with the oldest at the top and the newest at the bottom
7. Select the withdrawal you want to update (in this example it would be 'First refusal to submit to test for
alcohol-implied consent law').

Once the withdrawal is selected, the details of the withdrawal will be displayed (this will allow you to confirm
you have the correct withdrawal as you MAY have multiple withdrawals that are the same).
The list of Convictions/Incidents will also populate
Blue checked boxes - Conviction is linked/will be linked to that Withdrawal
Uncheck an incident - Unlinking conviction from withdrawal
8. Locate the incident you want to link to the withdrawal and click on the box located in the 'Link' column.
9. Click on Save Link.

Once you click Save Link the following box will appear.
10. Click Yes.
A green box will pop up in the upper right corner stating the link was successful.
The incident and withdrawal are now linked. This can be confirmed on the customer record.

## Incident Management

`Driver/Event_Management/Incident_Management.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Incident
Management
Incident Management
Incident management is part of the larger topic of Event Management. An incident is anything that can
impact a driver’s privileges or a vehicle’s status, such as traffic citations, DUIs, failure to comply with
insurance requirements. There are 1000s of potentially impactful incidents ranging from bouncing a
check to abandoning a vehicle to receiving a legal judgment or conviction, and many more. Incidents
get added to MAX in a few key ways:
Transmitted into MAX electronically (by courts, law enforcement, towing companies, etc.).
MVD Central Services enter incidents manually (primarily from court abstracts).
No matter how incidents get entered into the system, MAX automatically applies associated business
rules and, then, when appropriate, automatically triggers any applicable withdrawals (stops,
cancellations, suspensions, revocations, or disqualifications) and compliance requirements the
customer must meet before they can get their driving privileges and or vehicle T&R services restored.
When an unlicensed driver commits an offense for which a person's driving privilege could be
suspended, the Division shall not accept the unlicensed driver's application for an instruction
permit (IP) or driver license (DL) until the suspension period has ended.

## Interlock Reinstatement and Issuance

`Driver/Event_Management/Interlock_Issuance.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Interlock
Reinstatement and Issuance
Interlock Reinstatement and Issuance
For a customer with a DUI suspension that requires an ignition interlock device to reinstate and issue a
new credential with the interlock restriction there are two key steps:
1. To reinstate all compliance related the DUI Incident/Withdrawal EXCEPT the IID Installation
requirement must be satisfied. Compliance you should satisfy may include Suspension
Reinstatement Fees, Alcohol Screening/ Education, SR22, etc. Do NOT satisfy the IID Installation
requirement.
2. Once all Compliance is satisfied, overnight, if the installer has transmitted the IID install data, MAX
will build the IID monitoring line in Compliance Monitoring which will automatically satisfy the IID
Installation Compliance requirement.
Once all compliance requirements are satisfied, the customer will no longer show suspended and you
will be able to issue their restricted credential.

## Certified Ignition Interlock Overview

`Driver/Event_Management/Interlock_Overview.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Certified Ignition
Interlock Overview
Certified Ignition Interlock Overview
A Certified Ignition Interlock Device (CIID) is an in-car breath-analyzing device that is connected to the
vehicle's ignition.
When an individual is required to have a CIID, they are subject to:
Payment of all cost associated with installation and maintenance of the CIID
Providing proof of installation (Ignition Interlock Proof of Installation, form #99-0203) of a
functioning CIID in each motor vehicle operated by the individual
The installation form will list the CIID manufacturer who installed the device in the vehicle,
installing location, and the name of technician who performed the install
Providing proof of calibration of the CIID at least once every 90 days during the period the
individual is ordered to use a CIID
Only Technical Support, Driver Improvement, and Ignition Interlock Units have the authority to place
CIID requirements on a Customer Record. Any questions regarding CIID requirements should be
directed to the Technical Support Unit at 602- 712-4900.
Samples of Certified Ignition Interlock Device (CIID)
Driving a Vehicle with a CIID
Individuals Required to have CIID
There are circumstances where a person may present proof of CIID installation even though the CIID
requirement may not display on the Customer Record.
A judge may have ordered a CIID to be installed even though Arizona law doesn't mandate
the CIID or the driving under the influence conviction has not been received from the court.

## Manage Electronic DUI Rejections and Convictions

`Driver/Event_Management/ManageE-RejectionsConvictions.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Manage Electronic
DUI Rejections and Convictions
Manage Electronic DUI Rejections and Convictions
There are two functions used in MAX to help manage electronic convictions and rejections from the
various batch jobs:
Manage eDUI Rejections
Manage Electronic Conviction
These functions are primarily used by the Records team (non-commercial convictions) and
MVD Support Services (commercial convictions).
Convictions are transmitted electronically into MAX and when possible, are automatically placed on the
appropriate Driver Record, with the corresponding Withdrawals and Compliance.
However, not all Convictions transmitted electronically can be matched to a single customer for a
number of reasons, including but not limited to:
Multiple records from a common name
Missing or incorrect identifying data such as DOB, DLN, etc.
Receiving multiple or duplicate court actions for the same Incident (e.g. a satisfied Failure to
Appear, when no Failure to Appear yet exists on the record)
Receiving convictions that occurred in Arizona but for customers credentialed in another state (no
Customer Record exists in MAX).
Both the Records team and MVD Support Services work these items in MAX to research the correct
customer and apply the correct conviction data to the Driver Record.
The steps below reflect how to navigate the functions in MAX. Follow your department's defined
procedures for researching and remedying each conviction.
Manage Electronic Conviction
Manage eDUI Rejections

## NRVC Processing

`Driver/Event_Management/NRVC_Processing.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > NRVC Processing
NRVC Processing
The Non-Resident Violator Compact (NRVC) is a United States interstate agreement between 44 states
and Washington, D.C., used to process non-compliance to traffic citations across state borders. NRVC
allows participating states to alert and share information with Motor Vehicle Administrators when a
resident of one state did not comply with citation terms. The home state will suspend the driver's license
until the driver takes care of the matter in the other state.
The NRVC Processing function in MAX allows the appropriate central services groups within MVD to
quickly add an NRVC record in MAX which then can be shared with the state the violator is from. For
example, if someone was visiting Arizona from Colorado and got a speeding ticket and failed to pay the
fine or failed to appear, the court would send information to MVD about the failure to comply. MVD
would enter the violation data in NRVC Processing and the information would be shared via an interface
with Colorado.
Upon notice of citation non-complicance, the NRVC is placed on the customer record by the Records
Unit, and the Division will initiate suspension of said customer's driving privilege. The NRVC suspension
will remain in effect until the nonresident violator complies with the citing jurisdiction's requirements.
Procedure for Issuing State
When a motorist fails to comply with the terms of a traffic citation, the appropriate official shall report the
"Failure to Comply" to the licensing authority of the state in which the traffic citation was issued. On
receipt of the report, the licensing authority of the issuing state shall transmit, to the licensing authority
in the home state of the motorist, the information in a form and content as contained in the compact
manual.
The licensing authority of the issuing state need not suspend the privilege for whom a report has
been transmitted
The licensing authority of the issuing state shall not transmit a report on any violation if the date of
transmission is more than six months after the date the citation was issued
The licensing authority of the issuing state shall not transmit a report in any violation where the
date of issuance of the citation predates the most recent of the effective dates of entry for the two
states affected
Procedure for Home State
On receipt of failure to comply, the Division shall notify the motorist and initiate a suspension action until
satisfactory evidence of compliance with the terms of the traffic citation has been furnished to the

Division.
The Division shall maintain a record of actions taken and make reports to issuing state as
provided in the compact manual
Due process safeguards will be accorded
In order to satisfy a NRVC, the customer must have one of the following:
Court abstract
Letter from the out-of-state court stating the citation has been resolved
If neither is available, the customer must contact the out-of-state court for the appropriate document.
A $10.00 reinstatement fee will need to be collected at time of reinstatement.
The provisions of this compact shall not apply to parking or standing violations, highway weight
limit violations and violations of law governing the transportation of hazardous materials.
Satisfying a NRVC
NRVC Processing to Other States
Edit an NRVC Incident
Delete an NRVC Incident
Processing NRVC Out of State Convictions (Records Unit Only)

## Revocation

`Driver/Event_Management/Revocation.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Revocation
Revocation
A person whose license or privilege to drive a motor vehicle on the public highways has been revoked
due to the following reasons:
Using a vehicle to commit a crime
Aggravated driving under the influence
Combinations of DUIs; reckless driving; and racing violations within 84 months
Fleeing from an officer
Failed re-exam or vision
When an unlicensed driver is revoked, the Division shall NOT accept a DL/ID Application,
form #40-5122 for an unlicensed driver while the revocation is in effect, unless the applicant is
eligible for issuance of a SIIRDL.
Questions relating to revocations may be directed to the Technical Support Unit at 602-712-4900.
Questions relating to medical revocation actions may be directed to Medical Review Program (MRP) at
602-771-2460.

## Special Ignition Interlock Restricted Driver License (SIIRDL) Issuance

`Driver/Event_Management/SIIRDL_Issuance.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Special Ignition
Interlock Restricted Driver License (SIIRDL) Issuance
Special Ignition Interlock Restricted Driver License (SIIRDL)
Issuance
A Special Ignition Interlock Restricted Driver License (SIIRDL) is license that is subject to all
CIID requirements, including accuracy checks. The SIIRDL permits individuals with an Arizona class D
or G driving privilege to operate a motor vehicle while they are under suspension, restriction, or
revocation for any of the following DUI violations:
A.R.S. § 28-1321: A first refusal of an Implied Consent
A.R.S. § 28-1383A3A or 28-1383A3B: A first violation of a specified Aggravated DUI
A.R.S. § 4-244.34, 28-1381, 1382: A violation of operating a motor vehicle while under 18 years of
age with any spirituous liquor in the person's body, if under a court-ordered restriction
A.R.S. § 28-1381 and 1382: A second violation of a DUI or Extreme DUI (can be a combination of
the two); second violation occurs after January 1, 2012
A.R.S. § 28-1385: Administrative license suspension (Admin Per Se) for a DUI ; violations occurs
on or after January 1, 2023
The SIIRDL will count as time-served towards the amount of time a customer is required to have a CIID
installed as long as the customer does not have a 'Failed to Calibrate' incident or remove the CIID
before the end of the suspension. If the customer is eligible for a CIID Deferment, the SIIRDL will not
count toward time for the deferment.
A Special Ignition Interlock Driver License (SIIRDL) Agreement, form #96-0782 is required for
violations of 28-1321 and 28-1385 that occurs on or after January 1, 2023.
If the Division suspends, revokes, cancels, or rescinds the initial SIIRDL or SIIRDL privilege
for any reason , except when the actions are pursuant to A.R.S. 28-1463, the Division may
not issue a new or reinstated SIIRDL to the person.
Admin Per Se or Implied Consents for drugs do not qualify for a SIIRDL.
Admin Per Se or Implied Consents for alcohol and drugs where the alcohol is less than .08 do
not qualify for a SIIRDL.

View MVD License Suspensions chart for more information on violation suspensions and eligibility.
Additional Information on Convictions & SIIRDLs
SIIRDL Blood Alcohol Concentration (BAC) Eligibility
Updating BAC for an Implied Consent and Admin Per Se Incident
All individuals applying for a SIIRDL must meet the following requirements:
Submit proof of financial responsibility (SR22)
A SR22 is not required for a SIIRDL when it is related to an Admin Per Se
View the SR22 topic > Verifying How Long a SR22 Is Required: DUI section to determine
how long they need to hold the SR22
Submit proof of installation of CIID
May need to provide proof of completion for any required Alcohol/Drug Screening or treatment/
evidence-based psychotherapy.
Reinstatement fees vary with a SIIRDL, but all fees including age appropriate reapplication fees
must be paid
If a person is applying for a first time DL or IP and is revoked or suspended, they must wait
until the revocation or suspension time is over prior to applying for the DL or IP.
The SIIRDL is issued to eligible individuals and is valid for the duration of the suspension, restriction, or
revocation period.
Wayne has a one year Implied Consent suspension from 01/05/2023 to 01/05/2024 for
refusing BAC testing.
During this period Wayne is eligible for a SIIRDL on 01/05/2023 that would allow him to drive
while on suspension.
Wayne has elected to have the SIIRDL issued on 06/05/2023.
Electing to have the SIIRDL issued on 06/05/2023 to 01/05/2024, permits Wayne to drive for
seven months when he would otherwise be suspended from doing so.
The seven months will go towards Wayne's IL requirement.

John has a one year Revocation for a 2nd or more DUI from 01/05/2023 to 01/05/2024.
During this period, John is eligible for a SIIRDL on 02/19/2023, that would allow him limited
driving while on suspension.
John has elected to have the SIIRDL issued on 06/10/2023.
Electing to have the SIIRDL issued from 06/10/2023 to 01/05/2024, permits John to driver for
seven months when he would otherwise be suspended from doing so.
However, the SIIRDL has not fulfilled John's one year IL requirement. John will need to keep
the CIID installed in his vehicle for the remaining time to complete his IL requirement.
If the SIIRDL is issued prior to January 1, 2023 having the CIID on the vehicle while the
individual is under a SIIRDL does not fulfill any part of the IL requirement.
However, if the individual issued the SIIRDL fails to comply with the CIID requirements, the
CIID requirements may be extended for an additional year from the end date of the SIIRDL.
At the end of the SIIRDL restricted time frame, the customer must then reinstate in order to restore his
or her full driving privileges. If the customer does not have an interlock requirement on record they may
remove the device after the period is up for the SIIRDL and pay to reinstate their license.

## Admin Per Se

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Admin_Per_Se.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Admin Per Se
Admin Per Se
When a law enforcement officer arrests a person for a DUI violation, the officer shall also serve an order
of suspension Admin Per Se / Implied Consent Affidavit, form #40-5807 to the person on behalf of the
Division. The officer will then forward the Admin Per Se / Implied Consent Affidavit, form #40-5807, to
the Division within 30 days after the arrest. The Admin Per Se / Implied Consent Affidavit, form #40-
5807 is an MVD action that is effective:
15 days after the date on which the order was served if the violation date is prior to January 1,
2023
30 days after the date on which the order was served if the violation date is on or after January 1,
2023
On receipt of the Admin Per Se/ Implied Consent Affidavit, the Division shall:
Suspend the customer's driving privilege for 30 days
After the 30-day suspension the customer is eligible for a 60-day restricted driving privilege, view
restriction requirements in A.R.S. 28-144
Alcohol or drug screening must be completed prior to issuing a restricted permit or
reinstatement of driving privileges. If more than one violation requiring a screening is
present, a screening must be completed for each violation
If a second or subsequent offense is issued, or the individual is involved in an accident with
serious physical injury, the customer will receive a 90-day suspension with no restricted permit
eligibility
The Admin Per Se is an administrative action. When a court dismisses a 'driving under the influence
violation', the Admin Per Se must be served in its entirety before it can be expunged.

If the Admin Per Se / Implied Consent Affidavit is not received within 30 days after the arrest,
the Division shall not enter the order of suspension on the customer's record. This does not
apply to violations that resulted in death or serious physical injury.
Difference Between DUI and Admin Per Se
Violations On or After January 1, 2023
In lieu of the driving suspension and restriction an eligible customer may request a SIIRDL if the
customer completes an alcohol or drug screening.
For more information about SIIRDL eligibility, view Special Ignition Interlock Restricted Driver License
(SIIRDL) Issuance topic.
When the customer requests a restricted permit and the Customer Record does not reflect the
Admin Per Se suspension or restriction, the customer must present the Admin Per Se/Implied
Consent Affidavit. When the Affidavit is submitted in person, the CSR shall contact Technical
Support Unit at (602) 712-4900. Only Technical Support Unit and the Records Unit personnel
are authorized to enter the Admin Per Se Affidavit.
The customer may request a hearing or summary review. A hearing or summary review request stays
the suspension until the hearing or summary review is held, except that the Division shall not return any
surrendered license or permit to the person but may issue temporary licenses.
Expunged (citation is removed from the customer's MVR), $50 reinstatement fee is due
Rescinded (dismissed), no reinstatement fees are required
The customer waives any rights to a hearing once a SIIRDL is issued.

## Child Support Arrearage

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Child_Support_Arrearage.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Child Support Arrearage
Child Support Arrearage
When an obligor fails to pay child support and or fails to comply with a child support subpoena, the court
may direct the Division to suspend the person's driving privilege until the obligor presents a Certificate
of Compliance from the court. The court may request a suspension of the license of the customer is not
in compliance with the agreement.
The Child Support Arrearage suspension is an indefinite suspension. The suspension will stay in effect
until the person submits the following:
Name
Date of birth
Date eligible to reinstate
Judge or Deputy Court Clerk's signature
Court seal
All applicable fees are paid
When the date of eligibility is prior to the current date and the suspension effective date is in the
future, the suspension is voided.
Upon receiving a Certificate of Compliance, the CSR will contact the Technical Support Unit at 602-712-
4900. The Certificate of Compliance will be scanned to the Technical Support Unit who will determine
eligibility and update the customer's record, closing the child support suspension. The CSR may then
reinstate the customer’s record, if there are no additional withdrawal actions prohibiting the
reinstatement.
Questions (from the public) pertaining to child support arrearage shall be referred to the Arizona
Division of Child Support Enforcement (DCSE) at (602) 252-4045 or when outside of Maricopa County
(800) 882-4151.

## Discretionary License Suspension

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Discretionary_License_Suspension.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Discretionary License Suspension
Discretionary License Suspension
The Division may suspend the license of a person upon receiving sufficient evidence that the person:
Convicted or adjudged to have violated traffic regulations with such frequency that it indicates
disrespect for traffic laws and disregard for the safety of others on the highway
Accumulation between eight to 12 points on the driving record within a 12-month period and has
successfully completed the Traffic Survival School (TSS) course within 24 months of the most
recent violation
Is medically, psychologically or physically incapable of operating a motor vehicle and, based on
law enforcement, medical or other Division information, the continued operation of a motor vehicle
by the person would endanger the public health, safety and welfare.
Committed or permitted an act of involving an unlawful or fraudulent use of the license
Committed an offense in another jurisdiction that if committed in this state is grounds for
suspension
Convicted of any of the following violations
A.R.S 28-1381 Driving or actual physical control while under the influence
A.RS. 28-1382 Driving or actual physical control while under the extreme influence
A.R.S 28-1464 Ignition interlock devices

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Failure_to_Install_CIID.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Failure to Install a Certified Ignition Interlock Device
Failure to Install a Certified Ignition Interlock Device
When a person who is required to install a Certified Ignition Interlock Device (CIID) fails to comply with
the order, the Division shall suspend the person's driving privilege and prevent registration renewal of
any vehicles owned by the person until proof of installation or compliance is submitted.
Only Technical Support, Driver Improvement, and Ignition Interlock Units have the authority to place
CIID requirements on a Customer Record.

on failure to install CIIDs.

## False or Fraudulent Application

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/False_or_Fraudulent_Application.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > False or Fraudulent Application
False or Fraudulent Application
When it is determined that a person falsely or fraudulently applied for an original or duplicate driver
license, identification license or permit, the Division shall cancel the license or permit and suspend the
person’s driving privilege for a period of six months from the date of cancellation. A second or
subsequent offense will result in a one-year suspension.
For customers who are registering to vote, certifying that he or she is eligible to vote and that
submitting a false voter registration is a felony.
The Office of the Inspector General was established by an Executive Order in 2004 to evaluate and
investigate fraud, abuse and misconduct within ADOT programs.

## Failure to Attend (FTA) Traffic Survival School (TSS)

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/FTA_TSS.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Failure to Attend (FTA) Traffic Survival School (TSS)
Failure to Attend (FTA) Traffic Survival School (TSS)
A Traffic Survival School (TSS) offers training and education, to drivers who are required to attend TSS.
The training and educational sessions are designed to improve the safety and habits of drivers.
An individual will receive a Corrective Action Notice, informing them that they must attend and complete
TSS within 60 days or their driving privilege will be suspended.
Service of the Corrective Action Notice is considered complete upon the mailing of the notice. The
Division is not required to prove actual receipt of the notice or actual knowledge of the suspension
action.
The customer will be responsible for reinstatement fees, and any other applicable fees.
Depending on the action the individual may be required to complete a Mandatory Traffic Survival School
Assignment or Discretionary Traffic Survival School Assignment.
For suspensions on or after August 9, 2017, a customer will no longer remain suspended until
reinstatement fees are paid but will be required to pay the fees when renewing, obtaining a
replacement or updating credential.
Multiple active TSS assignments, with the same TSS assignment ID, can attend once to
satisfy all open TSS assignments. If a new assignment comes in, a new TSS assignment ID
will be generated and the customer must attend again.

## Implied Consent

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Implied_Consent.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Implied Consent
Implied Consent
When a person refuses to consent to a test or tests of the person's blood, breath, urine or other bodily
substance for the purpose of determining alcohol concentration or drug content, the officer will then
forward the Admin Per Se / Implied Consent Affidavit, form #40-5807 to the Division within 30 days after
the arrest. The Division shall suspend the person's driving privilege for a period of one year. MVD action
takes place after 15 days if the violation date was before January 1, 2023 or after 30 days if the
violation date was on or after January 1, 2023.
A second or subsequent refusal within a 84-month period shall result in a two-year suspension
The Implied Consent suspension will stay in effect until the person submits the following:
Proof of future responsibility (SR22)
View the SR22 topic > Verifying How Long a SR22 Is Required: DUI section to determine how long they
need to hold the SR22.
If the Admin Per Se / Implied Consent Affidavit is not received within 30 days after the arrest
the Division shall not enter the order of suspension on the customer's record. This does not
apply to violations that resulted in death or serious physical injury.
Violations after September 21, 2006 but before January 1, 2023 - Upon completing the mandatory
90-day suspension and meeting all applicable eligibility requirements, the customer may apply to the
Division for a SIIRDL if the person has no previous Implied Consent suspensions within the past 84
months.
Violations on or after January 1, 2023 - Upon meeting all eligibility requirements, the customer may
apply for a SIIRDL starting the first day of the Implied Consent suspension if the customer has no
previous Implied Consent suspensions within the past 84 months.
For more information about SIIRDLs, view the Special Ignition Interlock Restricted Driver License
(SIIRDL) Issuance topic.
A timely request for a hearing stays the suspension until the hearing is held, except that the Division
shall not return any surrendered license or permit to the person but may issue temporary permit. If the
results of the Hearing is not recorded, contact the Executive Hearing Office (602) 712-7737.
The customer waives any rights to a hearing once a SIIRDL is issued

The Division takes no action when the Implied Consent violation occurred in another state,
unless the licensee holds a Commercial Driver License (CDL).
Alcohol Screening

## Medical Reasons

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Medical_Reasons.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Medical Reasons
Medical Reasons
The Division may suspend a person's driving privilege when the person fails to:
Meet the Division's vision or medical standards
Successfully complete a re-examination
The issuing of a DL is prohibited to any person who:
Is required to take an examination, unless that person successfully passes the examination
The Division has good cause to believe would be a threat to the public safety or welfare
Has been adjudged to be incapacitated and who, at the time of application, has not obtained
either a court order allowing the person to drive or a termination of incapacity
General Summary Suspension and Revocation Information
Summary Suspension and Revocation - Vision
Processing a Summary Suspension and Revocation - Vision
Medical Re-Exams
The Division may require a re-examination to determine the driver's ability to safely operate a motor
vehicle. The Division may use information obtained from the Customer Record or other governmental
agencies to determine cause. Refusal or neglect of the licensee to submit to the re-examination is
grounds for suspension and revocation of their license.
View the Medical Re-Exam Testing topic for more information on handling re-exams in the office (e.g.,
window responsibilities, handling pass/fails of vision screening, knowledge tests, etc.).
View the following topics for more information on how to conduct a Medical Re-Exam (must be certified
in conducting road tests):
Medical Re-Exam Skills Test
Medical Re-Exam Instructions and Parking Maneuvers

Medical Re-Exam Scoring Guidelines
Requesting a Re-Exam

Rules of the Re-Exam
Rules of Failing a Re-Exam
Failed Re-Exam: Suspension/Revocation Time Frame is Over

## Minors and Juveniles

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Minors_and_Junveniles.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Minors and Juveniles
Minors and Juveniles
When a juvenile fails to appear or fails to comply with an order of a violation, the Division shall suspend
the juvenile's driving privilege until the juvenile reaches 18 years of age or appears in court as directed
or complies with the court's order.
When the juvenile is convicted of any one of the following violations, the Division shall suspend the
minors driving privilege as follows:
A.R.S. 4-244(34) Underage Drinking and Driving, 28-1381 Driving or actual physical control while
under the influence, and 28-1382 Driving or actual physical control while under extreme influence,
for a period of two years
A.R.S. 28-1383 Aggravated driving or actual physical control while under the influence, for a
period of three years
A.R.S. 13-1602(A)(1) Criminal Damage, 13-1602(A)(5) or 13-1604(A) Aggravated criminal
damage, until the person is 18 years of age
Any violation involving the purchase or possession of materials used for graffiti, until the minor is
18 years of age
Any violation of A.R.S. Title 13, Chapter 34, until the person is 18 years of age
Second or subsequent violation of A.R.S. 4-244(9) Unlawful acts, for a period of two years, if
ordered by the court
A.R.S. 13-1802 (A)(1) Theft, 13-1803(A)(1) Unlawful use of means of transportation, 13-1803(A)
(2), or 13-1814(A) Theft of means of transportation, until the person is 18 years of age
The minor may be eligible for a restricted permit.
The suspension will stay in effect until the person submits the following:
Applicable reinstatement fee
Any other applicable fees
Juvenile - Admin Per Se
The court may direct the Division to restrict the juvenile's driving privilege:
Until the juvenile's 18th birthday
For a period of two to three years

For a period of up to 180 days for the first time conviction violation
The juvenile is restricted to driving privilege allows the individual to drive between the juvenile's place of
residence and juvenile's school (Including Traffic Survival School) according to the individuals
educational schedule.
The juvenile is not allowed to drive to the treatment facility.

## Traffic Violations

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Traffic_Violations.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Traffic Violations
Traffic Violations
Citations
A citation is a law enforcement action (e.g., running a red , speeding, failure to stop, etc.). If the citation
is not recorded, the CSR will enter the citation manually into MAX. This may prevent the customer from
going under suspension. Other actions a citation may affect are:
Cause points on the driving record resulting in a suspension
Waive the points
Void (set aside) the suspension
Initiate a Traffic Survival School Assignment
Only court abstracts with a final disposition code and final disposition date may be entered by a CSR. A
court abstract will have:
The customer's name, address, license number, violation codes and the results of the court
hearing (disposition codes)
A court abstract can be a separate document from the court or the original citation with a judge or
clerks signature. It does not need a court seal
Any other document, such as a receipt without a signature, must be sent back to the court for a
court abstract. A copy of the court abstract is kept with the CSR's daily work.
Court Abstracts
A Court Abstract is a legal document issued by the courts to report fact and findings and will include
most of the information from the citation. A customer must provide a court abstract when it is necessary
to add or update a violation on their record.
Citing Agencies

## Underage Drinking

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Underage_Drinking.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Underage Drinking
Underage Drinking
When a person who is under the legal drinking age is convicted of a violation A.R.S. 4-241(L) or 4-
241(N), the Division shall promptly suspend the person's driving privilege and identification license,
when applicable as follows:
First conviction, a period of six months
Second or subsequent convictions, a period of one year
Pursuant to A.R.S. 4-241(P), the court may suspend the driving privilege of a person convicted for
influencing the sale, giving or serving of spirituous liquor to a minor and may suspend the driving
privilege of a licensee or licensee’s employee who knowingly admits a minor onto any premises in
violation of A.R.S. 4-244(22) as follows:
First conviction, a period of not more than 30 days
Second or subsequent conviction, a period of not more than six months
Convictions for underage drinking will stay in effect until the person submits the following:
Applicable reinstatement fee
Any other applicable fees
Pursuant to A.R.S. 28-3320(D), the Division shall suspend the driving privilege of a person under 18
years of age convicted for the first time violation of A.R.S 4-244(9) for a period of up to 180 days when
ordered by the court pursuant to A.R.S 4-246.
If ordered by the court, the Division shall restrict the driving privileges of the person for a period of
up to 180 days

## Vehicle Immobilization or Impoundment

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Vehicle_Immobilization_or_Impoundment.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Vehicle Immobilization or Impoundment
Vehicle Immobilization or Impoundment
The agency initiating the immobilizing or impounding action is responsible for vehicle immobilization or
impoundment program actions. The following information is provided only for informational purposes. All
questions pertaining to vehicle immobilization or impoundment actions are referred to the immobilizing
or impounding agency.
A peace officer may cause the immobilization or removal and impoundment of a vehicle when:
The person driving the vehicle has his or her driving privilege canceled, revoked or the person is
not licensed to operate a motor vehicle in any jurisdiction
The person driving the vehicle is unable to provide proof of Financial Responsibility
The person driving the vehicle is involved in an accident that results in either property damage or
injury or death to another individual
Law enforcement officer has probable cause to search and either immobilizes or impounds the
vehicle
The vehicle shall be immobilized or impounded for a period of 30 days. The vehicle owner, spouse of
the owner, or any persons identified on the Vehicle Information Page shall be provided an opportunity
for a post storage hearing conducted by the immobilizing impounding agency.
In order for the person's to take possession of the said vehicle proof of current and valid DL and current
registration must be provided. The impounding immobilizing agency shall accept a current vehicle
registration as proof of ownership.
When a claim has not been made for the return or possession of the vehicle by a person legally entitled
to the vehicle within 30 days after a vehicle is immobilized impounded, the person who has possession
of the vehicle shall submit an Abandoned Vehicle Report. View the Abandoned Vehicle Overview and
the Abandoned Vehicle Fees topics for more information.
When the driver of the vehicle at the time of immobilization impoundment is required to install a
CIID, the CIID must be installed prior to the release of the vehicle.
The immobilizing impounding agency, storage yard, facility, person or agency having physical
possession of the vehicle shall allow access to the immobilized impounded vehicle for the
purpose of installing the CIID and shall not charge a fee for providing access for the installation
of the CIID.

The owner is responsible for paying all towing, storage and administrative charges related to the
immobilization impoundment of the vehicle, unless the vehicle is stolen and the theft was reported to the
appropriate law enforcement agency.
Questions pertaining to suspensions may be directed to the Technical Support Unit at (602) 712-4900.

## Withdrawal Types and Descriptions

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal Types and Descriptions/Withdrawal_Types_&_Descriptions.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal Types &
Descriptions > Withdrawal Types and Descriptions
Withdrawal Types and Descriptions
Suspensions
The Division shall suspend a person's driving privilege after receiving notice of a conviction of the
person in another jurisdiction and, determining that the conviction was for an offense that if committed in
Arizona would be grounds for suspension. The suspension of a person's driving privilege does not affect
any pending actions against the person (e.g., cancellation, other types of suspension, traffic survival
school assignment, certified ignition interlock restriction, revocation, etc.). The expiration of a person's
license during the suspension period does not invalidate or terminate the suspension.
A person whose driving privilege is suspended shall not operate a motor vehicle in Arizona under a
license, permit or registration certificate issued by another jurisdiction, or otherwise during or after the
suspension until the person has reinstated their driving privilege. Likewise, a person whose driving
privilege is suspended under a license, permit or registration issued by another jurisdiction shall not
operate a motor vehicle in Arizona during or after the suspension until the person has met all applicable
requirements in the other jurisdiction.
When it is determined that a person's driving privilege must be suspended, a Corrective Action Notice,
form #26-9611 will be sent to any address known to the Division. Accordingly, the Division may issue a
temporary identification licenses (ID) to a person whose driving privilege is suspended.
Prior to voiding any suspension, the CSR must obtain approval from the Technical Support Unit.
Service of the Corrective Action Notice is considered complete upon the mailing of the notice.
The Division is not required to prove actual receipt of the notice or actual knowledge of the
suspension action.
Types of Withdrawals
Admin Per Se
Child Support Arrearage
Discretionary License Suspension

Failure to Instal Certified Ignition Interlock Device
False or Fraudulent Application
Financial Responsibility
Failure to Attend (FTA) Traffic Survival School (TSS)
Implied Consent
Medical Reasons
Minors and Juveniles
Traffic Violations
Underage Drinking
Vehicle Immobilization or Impoundment

Withdrawal Management
Withdrawal Management is part of the larger topic of Event Management. A withdrawal is any stop,
cancellation, suspension, revocation, or disqualification on a driver's privileges or on a vehicle's status.
Whenever an incident is recorded into the system, MAX automatically evaluates the occurrence and,
based on associated business rules, determines whether a withdrawal should be applied.

## Withdrawal Occurring During a Service

`Driver/Event_Management/Withdrawal_Occurring_During_a_Service.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > Withdrawal
Occurring During a Service
Withdrawal Occurring During a Service
Withdrawal Management is part of the larger topic of Event Management. A withdrawal is any stop,
cancellation, suspension, revocation, or disqualification on a driver's privileges or on a vehicle's status.
Whenever an incident is recorded into the system, MAX automatically evaluates the occurrence and,
based on associated business rules, determines whether a withdrawal should be applied.
In some instances the incident, and its resulting withdrawal, occur as part of providing a service.
Credential Issuance Services Examples
Failed Vision Screening: If the customer fails an in-office vision screening, the Vision Screening
requirement, when you click the action icon, MAX will not allow you to satisfy the requirement by
selecting Vision Screening in the Source field. The customer will need to visit an optometrist and
return to the MVD with a completed Vision Exam Report.
Failed Knowledge Test: The requirement remains unsatisfied and you will not be able to process
and finalize the service until the customer is able to successfully pass the knowledge test.
Failed Skills Test: The requirement remains unsatisfied and you will not be able to process and
finalize the service until the customer is able to successfully pass the skill test.
Answering Yes to Military Question on Questionnaire Tab: If this has not already been
satisfied, answering yes to this question will trigger a new requirement on the Requirements tab.
 Ensure the customer has a Veteran or Military Personnel characteristic on their customer record
and satisfy the "Proof of Veteran Status" document requirement on the Requirements tab.
Answering Yes to Medical Capabilities Questions on Questionnaire Tab: Answering yes to
any of these questions could trigger a Medical Review withdrawal or may trigger an additional
Medical Exam or Vision Exam Certificate from a medical professional.
Vision - Answering yes and adding any of these conditions will trigger a Vision Exam
Certificate requirement.
Monocular Vision
Glaucoma
Cataracts
Diabetic Retinopathy
Nystagmus
Color Blind

Poor Night Vision
Biopic Telescopic Lens System
Medical - Answering yes and adding any of these conditions will trigger a Medical Exam
Report (MER) requirement and/or a Medical Professional Statement on the Requirements
tab. Some conditions may also trigger an MVD Med Review withdrawal. Med Review has
its own Business Unit (BU) Queue and can review and evaluate Medical Exam Reports and
Medical Professional Statements, but this may not be instantaneous.
Epilepsy
Parkinson's Disease
Narcolepsy
Alzheimer's
Bipolar
Cardiac Issue
Cognitive Issues
Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Disease (COPD)
Dementia
Diabetic Event
Hypertension
Limb Impairment
Memory Loss
Neuropathy
Retinal Disease
Schizophrenia
Seizure
Sleep Apnea
Stroke
Loss of Consciousness
Traumatic Brain Injury (TBI)
Alcohol/ Drug Dependency
Declared Incapacitated by Court

# Management


## Biometric Authentication Tools - On-Demand Authentication

`Management/Biometrics.htm`

You are here: Customer > Biometric Authentication Tools - On-Demand Authentication
Biometric Authentication Tools - On-Demand Authentication
The On-Demand Authentication service located in the Management menu has two functions:
1. On-Demand Authentication (ODA) - the ability to send a link to a customer to have them
authenticate on their device with facial recognition
2. Digital Identity Biometric - the ability to view the results of biometric authentication attempts for
fraud prevention purposes
The Sources that feed into this utility include:
Apple mDL
Google mDL
ETitle Transfer Kiosks
Enhanced Authentication through AZ MVD Now and eAZ
On-Demand Authentication
On-Demand Authentication

Call Center
Field Office of the Future (OOF) BUs
MVM
Special Projects IT
Level 2 Support
LECU
OIG Enforcement

Ignition Interlock (MVD Support)

## FMS Collections

`Misc._Business_Topics/FMS_Collections.htm`

You are here: Finance & Cash Management > FMS Collections
FMS Collections
FMS may create a 'Collections' Withdrawal Action on a customer record that will NOT stop a service. Some of
these collection descriptions include but are not limited to:
IFTA audit collections
IRP audit collections
Aircraft audits collections
Aircraft interest collections
Amount due to receipt adjustment collections
Cardlock collections
Foreign Based IRP collections
Dealer Violation Civil Penalty collections
Ignition Interlock Return collections
Past Due Collection Notifications will be mailed out at 30, 60, and 90 calendar days of delinquency. A
customer may choose to pay these notices by mail, at any MVD Customer Service or Authorized Third Party
office, or online. If customer is paying:
At an office: Use the Compliance Management service to collect
Online: using AZ MVD Now, the 'Services' menu option will be used and then 'Manage Compliance
Issues' selected in order to make the payment
Mail: The customer may send a Money Order, Cashiers Check, Official Check, or Personal Check
made payable to ADOT. They will need to write the account number indicated on the Collections Notice
on the check or money order. Mail to:
ADOT Collection Unit,
PO Box 2100, MD 529M
Phoenix, AZ 85001-2100

Delinquencies that are 90 days or more past due will need to be paid to the Bankruptcy and
Collection Enforcement Section at the Office of the Attorney General. For questions regarding
payment the customer may contact the Attorney General at (602) 542-1719.
Any questions, contact the Collections Unit by phone at (602) 712-7633 or by email at
ADOTCollections@azdot.gov.

## Fraudulent_Documents_or_Identity

`Misc._Business_Topics/Fraudulent_Documents_or_Identity.htm`

You are here: Documents and Scanning > Fraudulent Documents or Identity
Fraudulent Documents or Identity
Fraud Investigations
CSRs will not process any DL and/or Title & Registration service when the customer's identity or
documentation presented appears to be fraudulent. The Office of the Inspector General (OIG) is the
investigative section of the ADOT Enforcement and Compliance Division (ECD). They perform the
following functions:
Conduct case investigations and audits designed to prevent and deter fraud, abuse, and
misconduct within ADOT programs
Evaluate the effectiveness and adequacy of ADOT program safeguards and concerns
Advise and make recommendations to, the Governor's Office and the Director of ADOT and ECD,
on ways to strengthen and improve program procedures and operations
Coordinate with law enforcement agencies on case investigations and enforcement actions
Provide criminal investigative follow-up including investigations related to the transportation
industry
ADOT follows some federal and state statute and regulations. The agency or prescribing authority are
listed in Workplace Posters.
Fraudulent Documents
Fraudulent Documents are any documents (e.g., driver license, title, registration, or any documents to
prove identity or process a transaction) that does not appear to be legitimate (e.g., document's security
features distorted, missing, obliterated, or altered).
Fraudulent Identity
Fraudulent Identity is if an applicant presents any identity documentation that appears to be valid, but
you suspect the person presenting the documentation is not the person the documentation belongs to.
Other Fraudulent Identity Issues with Credentials
Facial Recognition
ADOT has advance capabilities with facial recognition software to fight identity theft in the state. The
technology has the ability to, examine the driver license photo, identify key features on the individual's
face, and compare those features to photos in the Division database.

If there is a match in the system, the software may be picking up an error in the record, fraudulent
activity, or in some cases twin siblings.
Deceased or Reported Deceased
When a customer applies for any type of license and a response returns with 'DECEASED' or
'REPORTED DECEASED' the CSR will:
Not disclose to the customer of the 'deceased status'
Photo copy all documentation before any further discussion with the customer
Inform the customer that their information is being forwarded to another department for review
and after five business days, they will need to contact OIG by phone to schedule an appointment
Send the original and all supporting documents to OIG
View the Contact Information for OIG section for address and phone number.
How to Handle a Fraudulent Situation
A CSR will never attempt to physically detain a person when either fraudulent documents or identity
is presented.
Before explaining anything to the customer, you will excuse yourself from your customer and take ALL
documentation that the customer has presented.
1. Gather all the documentation and scan all documents (colored, if possible) to your email.
Exceptions: T&R Application (Single), Bond Affidavit, Travel
License/Identification Application, or any other pre-filled Division form
Any original Division form will be kept and not returned to the customer
2. Inform the applicant that before any service can be completed it will be necessary to send the
photocopies of the documentation to another department for review and verification of the
information.
For possible identity fraud, you will actually need to issue a credential; DO NOT print the
temp credential
Collect fees and Cancel Print (Folio) (Credential Print Status Queue)
3. Advise the customer they can contact OIG by phone after five business days to schedule an
appointment.
4. After applicant has left, properly complete an Incident Report, form #46-4411
View the Incident Reports topic posted in the Office Standard Work section of MVD One
Source for more information on how to properly complete the report
5. Email the completed report to OIG at fraud@azdot.gov with all copies (from Step 1).
Only mail the report to OIG if you have original documentation
View the Incident Reports topic posted in the Office Standard Work section of MVD One
Source for more information on how to preparing email to OIG
6. If the customer is suspected of fraud (documents or identity) for a credential:
Add notes (view the Notes Management topic) to the customer record: 'Do not issue anything
- refer to OIG'

Make a copy of the report and any supporting documentation (including any licenses) and place
it in the designated file.
