## Non-Sufficient Funds

`Misc._Business_Topics/Non-Sufficient_Funds.htm`

You are here: Finance & Cash Management > Non-Sufficient Funds
Non-Sufficient Funds
The Division may cancel a license (CDL, DL, IP), suspend a vehicle registration, and/or stop a
registration credit disbursement for nonpayment of money due. An NSF will add a stop on the
customer's record and prevent all services (with the exception of an ID card) until full payment is made
to resolve the returned payment.
There are four withdrawals related to return payments that will add a stop to a customer record, these
are:
NSF Penalty Due
Online payment Return
Chargeback JUD
Port of Entry Return Payment
MAX will allow for the issuance of an ID when there is a stop on the record due to a NSF,
Online Payment Returned, or Chargeback (JUD).
If the NSF is for an ID card transaction, you may still issue a new ID card without
collecting the NSF fee first
If the NSF transaction was not for a driver license transaction (e.g., registration renewal)
and their driver license is active, the customer MUST complete a Driver License/ID
Cancellation Request, form # 96-0384 before an ID card can be issued (MAX will provide
an alert that the current credential will be canceled prior to issuance)
An attempt will be made to collect all funds due and a fee for the check or electronic payment returned
for nonpayment due to insufficient funds, payments stopped, or closed accounts.
Full payment is required within 45 days. When the returned payment is not paid within that time-frame,
MAX will create an incident/ withdrawal action. If the NSF was for:
A credential, the credential will be canceled, MAX will stop services
A T&R or registration, the registration will be suspended, MAX will stop services
If the incident/ withdrawal is for some other type of service (e.g., MVR, Aircraft Returned Payment
or Port of Entry Returned Payment), MAX will stop services until satisfied
Example of T and R Suspension

Example of DL Cancellation
Before a person can become eligible for any other products or licensing services offered by the Division,
all returned payment and all associated fees must be paid in full. Ineligible Services include:
Credential Issuance
Credential Renewal
Credential Duplicate
Print Title
Title Only
Registration Only
Duplicate Registration
Update Registration
Renew Registration
Reinstate Registration
Title and Registration
Issue Permit
Reprint Permit
Tab Replacement
Temporary Registration Plate
The CSR must always resolve the outstanding returned payment amount on the Customer
Record before completing any title and/or registration services, view Reinstatement Scenarios >
Reinstating a Vehicle Registration with a NSF/Chargeback Withdrawal section for instructions
on how to collect and reinstate a vehicle record.

## Reported Deceased

`Misc._Business_Topics/Reported_Deceased.htm`

You are here: Customer > Reported Deceased
Reported Deceased
If a customer applies for any type of credential issuance or vehicle service and a response returns with
'Deceased' or 'Reported Deceased' the CSR will**:
1. Ask the customer if they have any documentation on the official letterhead from the Social Security
Administration (SSA) or Vital Records that is dated within 30 days. If the customer was not born in
AZ they will need to contact vital records of the state they were born in.
2. If the customer does not have any documentation, provide the customer with the Reported
Deceased in Error, form #96-1003. This form provides the customer with the phone numbers and
websites for both the Social Security Administration and Arizona Vital Records, and gives detailed
information on what the Division requires to remove the "Deceased" status from their record.
Social Security Administration
Arizona Vital Records
(800) 772-1213
(602) 364-1300
3. The customer will reach out to one or both agencies to request a letter stating the deceased status
was reported on their record in error.
4. The customer will return with this document as quickly as possible and provide it to a CSR.
5. The CSR will contact Tech Support (602-712-4900) and either upload to the customer documents or
scan a copy to the agent assisting.
6. Tech Support will review the documentation and if everything is validated will remove the Deceased
status on the Customer's Record.
7. The CSR should try to complete the Service while on the phone with Tech Support. If unsuccessful,
have an upline approver submit a Service Portal ticket to get it resolved so they can complete
issuance.
**If there is any suspicion of fraud, please see Fraudulent Documents or Identity > Deceased
or Reported Deceased section on how to involve OIG.
Forms
#96-1003 Reported Decease in Error

## Residency Requirements

`Misc._Business_Topics/Residency_Requirements.htm`

You are here: Customer > Residency Requirements
Residency Requirements
Residents of Arizona are required to have an Arizona Driver License and/or Arizona Vehicle
Registration, those persons are:
A person who, regardless of domicile, remains in this state for a cumulative period of seven
months or more per calendar year
Any person who engages in a trade, profession or occupation, or who accepts employment
except for:
Seasonal agricultural work
Temporary seasonal work for a period of not more than three months in the state, in which
the temporary seasonal worker is permanently domiciled, has a similar exception
Any person placing children in a public school in Arizona without payment of non-resident tuition
fees
Any person who declares they are a resident of this state for the purpose of obtaining, at resident
rates, a state license or tuition fees at an educational institution maintained by public funds (e.g.,
ASU, U of A, NAU)
Any person operating a vehicle for their employer, which bases and operates motor vehicles in
this state, and uses the vehicles for intrastate operation other than seasonal agricultural work
Any person who is registered to vote in this state
A Resident Does Not Mean
A nonresident owner of a vehicle that is registered and in a state adjoining this state and used in
this state for the transportation of passengers or property for compensation, or if the nonresident
owner and vehicle are domiciled in an adjoining state, as long as the owner resides and the
vehicle is registered in that state, regardless of whether the nonresident owner engages in a
trade, profession or occupation in this state or accepts employment
An out-of-state student enrolled with seven or more semester hours regardless of whether the
student engages in a trade, profession or occupation in this state or accepts employment in this
state. For the purposes of this paragraph, "out-of-state student" means either:
A person who is enrolled at an educational institution maintained by public monies and who
is not classified as an in-state student
A person who is a student at a private educational institution and who would not be
classified as an in-state student

Exemption from an Arizona Driver License

## Window Tinting

`Misc._Business_Topics/Window_Tinting.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Vehicle Management > Window Tinting
Window Tinting
A person required for medical reasons to be shielded from the direct rays of the sun may receive an
exemption from the window tinting requirements.
The exemption is granted to either a vehicle owner or an individual who is a habitual passenger in a
vehicle.
Pursuant to A.R.S. § 28-959.01, a substance or material in conjunction with a glazing material that has
a light transmission of 33% plus or minus 3% and a luminous reflectance of 35% plus or minus 3% may
be applied to front side wing vents and rear windows.
To apply for the Certificate of Exemption, the applicant must submit an Application For Window Tint
Medical Exemption, form # 40-1511, which is certified by a Doctor of Medicine (MD), Doctor of
Osteopathic Medicine (DO), Naturopathic Medical Doctor (NMD), or Ophthalmologist.
Medical Review will examine the documentation and either approve or deny the customer’s request.
This process could take Medical Review anywhere from 4 to 6 weeks to approve. If approved, Medical
Review will add the Light Sensitivity Window Tint characteristic on the customer's record, scan the
Certificate of Exemption to the customer's documents, and mail the Certificate of Exemption to the
driver to keep in his vehicle.
The letter and characteristic do not prevent the driver from getting pulled over; it is only to show
the officer when pulled over
Application For Window Tint Medical Exemption, form # 40-1511 is for front side and rear
windows only. If driver is requesting a windshield tint then they would need to obtain a letter
from a physician the specifically states windshield tint requested. Medical Review will send
them a separate notice for the windshield tint.
Sending the Window Tint Medical Exemption Application to Medical Review

## Accommodative Data and Mobile Home Snapshot

`Mobile Homes/Accommodative Data.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mobile Homes > Accommodative Data
and Mobile Home Snapshot
Accommodative Data and Mobile Home Snapshot
If there is no record for the Mobile Home in MAX and you need to add the Mobile Home Record from
TPX, you will use the Accommodative Data Search in MAX. The Accommodative Database is a
resource that has legacy information from TPX, that allows the ability to search for a Mobile Home
Snapsot.

## Delinquent Taxes

`Mobile Homes/Delinquent_Taxes.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mobile Homes > Delinquent Taxes
Delinquent Taxes
When delinquent taxes are due, a Tax Clearance Receipt will be required BEFORE ANY transaction can be
completed. The customer will obtain the Tax Clearance Receipt from the County Assessor, and in some cases
from the Sheriff’s Department, from the county where the personal property taxes are delinquent in.
The Tax Clearance Receipt MUST contain the following information:
Name of the county that issued the clearance
Mobile home year
Mobile home make
Vehicle Identification Number (VIN)
Tax roll number
Year of delinquency
If the information on the Tax Clearance Letter does not match what is in MAX, the customer will have to contact
the County Assessor or Treasurer's office to have them fix the information in MAX through the AZ MVD Now
Business portal.
The County Assessor or Treasurer Office may have a different owner on file than what is on the mobile
home record in MAX. For this reason the Division will accept the Tax Clearance receipt with a different
owner's name as long as the rest of the required information matches our records.
MAX will display an Alert when attempting to process a mobile home title service and there is a County tax
due Stop on record.
Example of a Tax Clearance Letter
Clearing Delinquent Taxes in MAX

## Mobile Home Bonded Titles

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mobile Homes > Mobile Home Bonded
Titles
Mobile Home Bonded Titles
Bond title procedures are required when:
An applicant cannot prove ownership of the mobile home OR
The Division is not satisfied that there are no unpaid lien(s), as a condition of issuing a title
The Division will require the customer to file a surety bond at the time of final application.
The bond process protects prior or future owner(s) and/or lienholder(s). This procedure will require an
applicant to go through several steps before the title process can be completed.
The CSR will need to complete a Bond Title Application - Mobile Home, form #40-1003 for the customer,
which helps the applicant to understand exactly what is required for a bond title.
For inspection appointments for mobile homes, the customer will need to email
InspectionProgram@azdot.gov.

## Mobile Home Title Transfers

`Mobile Homes/MH_Title_Transfers.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mobile Homes > Mobile Home Title Transfers
Mobile Home Title Transfers
Mobile Home Unit Backlog - The Mobile Home Unit will be working their backlog of titles that are
missing documentation post mobile homes in MAX. For these records the Mobile Home Unit may
contact the CSR.
Documentation Needed
For MCO(s) or out-of-state title(s):
A customer must provide the following for each section of the mobile home:
Manufacturer's Certificate of Origin(s) (MCO)
Manufacturer's Statement of Origin(s) (MSO)
Out-of-state title(s)
A properly completed, including the specific information required for MHs, T&R Application (Single),
form #96-0236 or a signed Title Summary
The customer must also provide:
Lien information for filing, or a lien release, if applicable
A Certificate of Compliance (before 1985) or Certificate of Waiver (after 1985) issued by the Arizona
Office of Manufactured Housing, if applicable
Out-of-State Mobile Homes
For Arizona Title Transfer:
The customer must submit the following documentation (and any supporting documentation) for each section
of the mobile home:
The Arizona mobile home title(s), Title Transfer (Bill of Sale), form #38-1306, if applicable
When a Title Transfer form is submitted, ownership on the form must be verified that it is the
same as ownership listed in Vehicle Snapshot - Accommodative Data
Tax Clearance Receipt, if applicable*. View the policy 8.13.2 Mobile Homes > Requirements for Title
Issuance section (G)(1) and (H) for more information
A properly completed, including the specific information required for MHs, T&R Application (Single),
form #96-0236 or a signed Title Summary
Level I Inspection, if applicable
Information for filing a lien, or a lien release, if applicable

Mobile Home lienholders are not required to be ELT
A lien release is required to release a lien on a mobile homes record no matter the age of the lien. For
additional information see the Lien Release topic.
If a lien did not convert over to MAX, then during a mobile home title transfer service MAX will display
an alert indicating there is a lien in accommodative data:
Check the accommodative data for the lien information before continuing.
If the customer does not have a lien release, use the Manage Liens and Brands service to add
the lien to the record in MAX. The customer will need to get a lien release before continuing
with the service
If the customer is providing a lien release, the you may continue with the title transfer service
without adding the lien to MAX
For additional information see the Accommodative Data and Mobile Home Snapshot topic .

## Mobile Home Brands

`Mobile Homes/Mobile_Home_Brands.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mobile Homes > Mobile Home Brands
Mobile Home Brands
What is a Brand?
Brands are descriptive labels (applied by state motor vehicle agencies) regarding the status of a motor
vehicle or mobile home, such as 'salvage', or 'restored salvage'.
The reason for branding a title helps identifies what has happened to the mobile home so the next
purchaser is aware prior to buying the mobile home, as this may influence the value.
If a mobile home's title has been branded, it is an official designation made by the Division and should
display on the mobile home's title.
Brands Added in Error
If a brand was added in error, a Level I Inspection is required, as a mobile home does not have a
confidential VIN.
Crushed
Crushed mobile homes are not considered branded but a status of a vehicle and indicates the mobile
home has been scrapped or destroyed.
There is no fee for this service.
The 'Crushed' status will not be applied when a valid lien displays.
For 'crushed' status, the inspection must include the vehicle was not crushed.
Automotive Recyclers
Dismantle Brand
When an owner intends to dismantle (take apart) any section of the mobile home to use or sell as parts
must first apply for a Dismantle branded title, this should be completed prior to taking parts from the
mobile home.
If there are multiple sections of the mobile home, only the section that is being dismantled will receive a
Dismantle branded title.

When the customer has already obtained a title with a Non-repairable brand, the owner is not required
to obtain a Dismantle branded title.
An automotive recycler is not required to obtain a title with the dismantle brand prior to dismantling the
vehicle when the recycler possesses a previously issued Dismantle, Salvage or Non-repairable branded
title
Before Dismantled Brand is Added
When applying for a Dismantle brand title, the customer must submit:
A Title of record
A lien release, if applicable
A Tax Clearance Receipt, if applicable
Flood Damage Brand
A flood or water damaged mobile home is identified by an out-of-state title that is branded to indicate the
mobile home has sustained flood or water damage.
This indication will be 'carried forward' to an Arizona title in the Previous Brands section, stating
'Flood Damaged'
A flood damaged mobile home is not considered to be a total loss.
If a mobile home that is titled in Arizona becomes flood or water damaged, the owner is not required to
apply for a Flood Damaged branded title, but the mobile home may be branded as 'salvage', 'non-
repairable', or 'dismantled'. However, the owner may request the Flood Damaged brand on their Arizona
title.
Non-Repairable
A non-repairable mobile home is a vehicle that has little or no resale value except as a source for parts
or scrap metal. Only the owner or insurer can designate the mobile home as non-repairable.
The Division will not issue a title to a vehicle when an out-of-state non-repairable branded title has been
issued.
If an insurance company is filing for the branded title, view the Insurance Companies Applying for
Salvage Title section for required documentation.
Before Non-Repairable Brand is Added
When applying for a non-repairable brand title:
The title of record must be surrendered
The non-repairable brand title may be reassigned no more than two times
Subsequent title transfers will not be processed and the purchaser will not be issued any type of title.
The only actions permitted once a mobile home has been branded non-repairable are:
Sale of component parts using a bill of sale

Scrapping the mobile home
Duplicate title by the registered owner
Restored Salvage Brand
A Restored Salvage branded mobile home title is when the mobile home has been repaired in order to
be lived in again. Once a mobile home has been rebuilt or restored, the owner must obtain a Restored
Salvage branded title before living in the mobile home.
Before Restored Salvage Brand is Added
When going from Salvage to Restored Salvage brand, the following documentation are required:
Level I Inspection
Salvage branded title of record must be surrendered, when:
The title has not been branded Salvage, the customer must first transfer ownership into
their name with the Salvage branded title before a Restored Salvage branded title can be
issued
Salvage title of record does not need to be surrendered provided all other requirements are met,
including a Level I Inspection, when:
The same owner is applying for a Restored Salvage title and the customer does not provide
the current Salvage branded title of record
Verify ownership
Transferring an Arizona Salvage branded title into an Arizona Restored Salvage branded
title with a new owner
The customer can transfer the title and change the brand at the same time
Transferring an out-of-state Salvage branded title to an Arizona Restored Salvage branded
title, whether it is the same owner or new owner
Salvage Brand
Any mobile home that becomes a total loss due to an accident, un-recovered theft, or becomes non-
repairable, will be referred to as a salvage mobile home. The owner of a mobile home that has been
claimed as previously stated will apply for a Salvage or Non-repairable branded title.
Before Salvage Brand is Added
The owner of record may retain possession of the salvaged mobile home
Title of record must be submitted
A lien release must be submitted, if applicable
A Tax Clearance Receipt, if applicable
If the mobile home has multiple sections and only one section of the mobile home is considered a
total loss, use only that section’s VIN to process a Salvage branded title
Insurance Companies Applying for Salvage Title
Adding a Brand

Removing the Salvage Brand

Mobile Homes & Travel Trailers
When a travel trailer (TV) is being used as a mobile home (MH), the TV record may be changed to a
mobile home record, or a MH record into a TV trailer record. Prior to changing a mobile home to a travel
trailer, verify the mobile home title has not been canceled due to being affixed, see Affixing and Un-
Affixing a Mobile Home topic for additional information on how to un-affix a mobile home.
To change a mobile home to a travel trailer use the Title and Registration service in MAX. For
additional information on travel and tent trailers see the Travel and Tent Trailers topic
To change a travel trailer to a mobile home use the Title Only service in MAX. For additional
information see the Mobile Home Title Transfers topic

## Mobile Home Overview

`Mobile Homes/Mobile_Homes_Overview.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mobile Homes > Mobile Home Overview
Mobile Home Overview
Mobile Home Unit Backlog - The Mobile Home Unit will be working their backlog of titles that
are missing documentation post mobile homes in MAX. For these records the Mobile Home
Unit may contact the CSR.
Mobile Home Background
Mobile homes (MH) can be used as a single-family dwelling or used for commercial purposes with or
without a permanent foundation. A mobile home is a structure consisting of one or more transportable
sections including the following:
Plumbing
Heating
Air conditioning
Electrical systems
When the mobile home is assembled on site it must be greater than eight feet wide, and at least 32
feet (8x32) in length and is built on a permanent chassis.
A mobile home may be titled as a travel trailer when it is used for recreational, camping, or travel use.
For additional information on how to title a mobile home as a travel trailer and vice versa see the Mobile
Home Miscellaneous topic or see the policy 8.10.10 Trailer Title and Registration for more information.

Add Vehicle(s) to IRP Fleet
When a carrier has established their IRP and/or IFTA account, they can add vehicles to their IRP fleet at anytime
during the registration year. Mileage information provided on the original application or in the last renewal will be used
to calculate the fees due.

T&R Application (if title work is being done, or for vehicles with out of state registration or titling (ARO))

wants to Add the vehicle to the fleet (must complete items 1 though 15, and 20)
IRP Lease Agreement Certificate, form #70-0904
Required Documentation for IFTA
If the vehicle(s) also require an IFTA decal you will need:
IFTA/IRP Account Changes Application, form #96-0430
Required Documentation for Proof of Vehicle Ownership

Vehicle ownership documentation (MCO, out-of-state title, current out-of-state registration, current Arizona
registration, or Arizona title or Title Transfer (Bill of Sale), form #38-1306)
Federal Heavy Vehicle Use Tax (Form 2290) for vehicles being registered as 55,000 lbs. and above
T&R Application, form #96-0236
Invoice or Bill of Sale (for vehicle purchased from a dealer)
Lienholder documentation, if applicable

## Cab Card Correction

`MotorCarrier/Cab Card Correction.htm`

You are here: MotorCarrier > Cab Card Correction
Cab Card Correction
If you need to change the Unit Number, USDOT, TIN or MCRS on a apportioned vehicle you will use
this service. You should confirm current vehicle details in either the IRP Fleet Vehicles section or the
Vehicle Record to compare (especially if the Carrier provided no comments on the FRP Application for
what is being changed.)
If changing one of the following in addition to a change in USDOT, TIN or MCRS, use the Amend
Vehicle service:
Unladen Weight
Axles/Combined Axles
Weight Group/GVW (increase or decrease)
Price
Ownership date (if error was made when added)
Colorado or Utah special circumstances options
Motor Carrier Responsible for Safety (MCRS)
Required Documentation
FRP Apportioned Registration Application, form #70-0502
IRP Lease Agreement Application, form # 70-0904 if applicable
For equipment or USDOT/TIN
If updating an invalid USDOT this service must be processed prior to the Edit Account Details
service
All vehicles with an invalid USDOT must be updated
Correcting the Cab Card
If updating an invalid USDOT this service must be processed prior to the Edit Account Details
service.
ALL vehicles with an invalid USDOT must be updated.
1. From the Motor Carrier Account, Fleets section, select Cab Card Correction from the Fleet Action
drop down.

2. Click the Select icon of the fleet the vehicle belongs to.
3. In the Service Prequal screen, confirm the fleet that required Cab Card Correction(s) and click Add.
4. Search for the vehicle that requires a Cab Card Correction using Plate Number, VIN or Unit
Number.
5. Click Search.
6. Confirm the vehicle found is correct.
7. Click Continue.
8. Update the required details.
9. Click Correct Cab Card. If there are multiple vehicles in the fleet that need to be updated on this
supplement, repeat steps 4-8.
10. You can do Cab Card Corrections for as many vehicles in the IRP Fleet as needed.
11. Once the vehicle(s) have been updated, click Confirm.

12. Review the list of vehicles updated.
13. Click Confirm.

## Create IFTA Account

`MotorCarrier/Create IFTA Account.htm`

You are here: MotorCarrier > Create IFTA Account
Create IFTA Account
When a customer wants an IFTA account opened, the carrier must submit the proper documentation.
Vehicles are IFTA Qualified
If the customer is not applying for an IFTA but the vehicle(s) is IFTA qualified, proof of IFTA is required,
such as a IFTA Lease Agreement Certificate, form #70-0901.
IFTA qualifying vehicles are vehicles:
That have two axles and a registered gross vehicle weight rating (GVWR) of 26,001 lbs. or more;
OR
That have three or more axles regardless of weight; OR
Those that are used in combination, when the weight of such combination exceeds a GVWR of
26,001 lbs.
Forms Required for IFTA
IFTA/IRP Application, form #70-0508
IFTA Bond, form #96-0224 (plus proper POA) or IFTA Cash Bond, form #96-046 if the carrier
requires a bond
Proof of Established Place of Business. View the Motor Carrier Basics topic > Established Place
of Business section for details on acceptable proof
$10.00 filing fee (regardless of the number of vehicles in the fleet)
Proof of current registration for any IFTA qualified vehicles that are not on the carriers apportioned
IRP fleet
Note: If the carrier is obtaining IRP apportioned registration at the same time as opening an
IFTA account, the carrier does not need to provide proof of registration (the cab card they
will receive when they complete registration will service as proof)
View the Motor Carrier Basics topic > Established Place of Business/Proof of Residence
section for more information on established place of business requirements.
New IFTA Accounts

When a carrier requests to open an IFTA and an IRP account, you must open the IRP account
before opening the IFTA account. Most of the information will be acquired from the
IFTA/IRP Application, FRP Apportioned Registration Application, or vehicle documentation.

Manage Motor Carrier Account
There are three areas in a Motor Carrier account where specific Motor Carrier information is managed. The purpose of this topic is to cover
the first section, where you will manage Motor Carrier Account details.

## Motor Carrier Correspondence

`MotorCarrier/Motor Carrier Correspondence.htm`

You are here: MotorCarrier > Motor Carrier Correspondence
Motor Carrier Correspondence
The Correspondence tab in a Motor Carrier Account provides access to correspondence you may need to
use with that carrier. Currently the following correspondence options are available.
IRP
IRP Additional Requirements
IRP Renewal Information
IRP Apportioned Registration Refund Request
Record Keeping Agreement
Audit Notification Template
IFTA
IFTA Additional Requirements
IFTA Surety Bond Requirement Notice
IFTA Bond Additional Requirements
IFTA License Revocation
IFTA Bond Claim Notification
IFTA Tax Payer Notification
IFTA Delinquent Quarterly Report (for FTLU use)
Record Keeping Agreement
Audit Notification Template
FMS Billing Correspondence (for FMS Use)
Sending Correspondence
Each correspondence item generally works the same way, they just have different information. This example
demonstrates generating an IRP Additional Requirements letter:
1. From the carrier's MC Account, click the Correspondence tab.
2. Select either IRP, IFTA or Audit to have access to the associated correspondence.
3. Select the Correspondence Type from the drop down.
4. Click Select.

5. The information available to select will vary in each different type of correspondence. In this example the
additional requirements letter is for an IRP renewal--click Renewal.
6. Select the message option(s) to add to the correspondence.
7. The Contact Information automatically defaults to the Motor Carrier Unit details. If you want the customer
to contact you directly type your email and/or phone.
8. Click Preview.
9. Scroll to the bottom of the page to review a mock up of correspondence that will be emailed to the
carrier.

10. If the information is as desired, scroll back up to the top of the page and click Submit.
11. If the correspondence is sent successfully, you will see this notification. Copies of all correspondence will
be saved in the customer's Document Center.

## Motor Carrier Basics

`MotorCarrier/Motor_Carrier_Basics.htm`

You are here: MotorCarrier > Motor Carrier Basics
Motor Carrier Basics
The Motor Carrier Licensing Unit (MCLU) handles the following for interstate trucks, tractors and other
commercial vehicles based in Arizona:
International Registration Plans
International Fuel Tax Agreements
Interstate Registrations
In order to maintain these specialized registrations, the unit uses the program MAX .
The information listed in this topic is miscellaneous business information vital to your role.

## Process Motor Carrier Supplement

`MotorCarrier/Process MC Supplements.htm`

You are here: MotorCarrier > Process Motor Carrier Supplement
Process Motor Carrier Supplement
When processing Motor Carrier Supplements there are a few rules you must remember:
You can only collect payment on Supplements that have a status of Invoiced.
Supplements must be payed in chronological order. You will get an error if you attempt to process
out of order:
........ 
If any supplement (except renewal supplements) was invoiced in one month and the date it's
being paid for is in a future month (example: invoiced a supplement on July 27th but customer is
paying for it on August 2nd) you must re-invoice the supplement PRIOR to processing payment
for that supplement.

# Office_SW


## Dangerous Action or Accident During Road Test

`Office_SW/Management/Accident_During_Road_Test.htm`

Dangerous Action or Accident During Road Test
If a dangerous action or accident happens during a road test, follow the appropriate below steps to
handle the situation.
View the Road Test Overview topic > Causes for Disqualification or Automatic Failure section for
examples of dangerous actions
CSR Duties during Road Test: Dangerous Action
CSR Duties during Accident: Injuries or Property Damage
CSR Duties after Accident: Back at the Office
Supervisor Duties after Accident: Employee is Injured
Additional Information
If the CSR is the only witness to the accident:
Statements made by the CSR will be limited only to factual information and observation
Statements that establish blame are not voiced
If there is an accident where there are no injuries and minor damage, it is up to the vehicle
operator if they want to call the local police or exchange insurance information
If the local police are called by one of the vehicle's operators and they do not respond, MVD does
not have to take any further action (e.g., Incident reports, etc.)
If local police are called, try to at least obtain a policy report number to include in the
MVD Incident Report, form #46-4411 being sent to Risk Management
If the report number cannot be obtained, Risk Management can contact the police
department if they need a copy of the report

## MVD: Error Resolution Queue

`QC_Mgmt/Field/Error_Resolution_Q.htm`

You are here: Quality Check Management > Field Offices > MVD: Error Resolution
Queue
MVD: Error Resolution Queue
The Error Resolution Queue will consist of the Error Queue and Disputes.
Error Queue: Errors issued by Quality Assistance (QA) unit during their Quality Check (QC)
process. MVD Offices will be required to QC their errors
Disputes tab: Errors found by the QA unit during their QC process that the MVD Offices believe
is not a valid error, and a formal Dispute was initiated. Used for the Dispute process to review the
QA unit's response
MVD Offices
MVD Offices consists of the following:
Regional office field offices
Contact Center
Equipment Services
Title Production
Reviewing Error Queue
Reviewing Dispute Response

`Reports/Customer_Details-Accommodative_Data.htm`

You are here: Reports > Customer Details - Accommodative Data
Customer Details - Accommodative Data
MVD's legacy system (TPX) will be unavailable in the near future. The driver data available in TPX has
been exported so it can still be accessed when needed. Here you will find information on how to find
what you need in the Customer Details report available from the Reports Dashboard.

## Customer Knowledge Exam Attempt Details Report

`Reports/Customer_Knowledge_Exam_Attempt_Details.htm`

You are here: Reports > Customer Knowledge Exam Attempt Details Report
Customer Knowledge Exam Attempt Details Report
This report is used by the Division to review and/or investigate individual exam candidate's attempts, scores,
and answers. This report is designed to allow for that statistical information to be queried as needed directly
by MVD staff.
Accessing the Report
1. From the Management menu, select Report Dashboard.
2. In the Driver - Credential Issuance section, click Customer Knowledge Exam Attempt Details.
3. Type the Start Date, End Date, and Customer Number.
4. Click View Report.

The report will show you the Start Date, End Date, Location, Exam Type, Delivery Source, Results, Questions
Answered, Correct, Incorrect, Answered Score, and Answered Questions.
 
 
HELPFUL LINKS
MVD / DMV Public Website
 
 
 
 
 
Customer Portal
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Last Updated: May 08, 2026


## Fee Adjustment Report

`Reports/Fee_Adjustment_Report.htm`

You are here: Reports > Fee Adjustment Report
Fee Adjustment Report
This report displays a detailed history of fees waived in MAX. This report will include any fees waived
due to customer characteristics added or manually waived by the CSR.
This report is for MVD INTERNAL USE ONLY, do not give to customers.
Overview
Review the report monthly looking for trends
Document process errors on the employee scorecard
Escalate to your Senior Manager any transactions or trend that appears to be outside of normal
business practices for additional investigation
See the Service History topic in One Source for instructions on how to view the service documentation.
Key Points
Double-check the following:
Are there waived registration fees
Are late fees waived in excess of $100
Does one CSR appear to waive more “normal business reason” fees than others
Do we have documentation to support waiving fees
In the transaction column look for transactions without a normal business reason to waive fees
MVRs (vehicle or driver)
Credential Issuance with other than standard fees
Compliance payments
Do we have documentation to support waiving fees
Accessing the Report
Saving the Report to Google Drive
 

HELPFUL LINKS
MVD / DMV Public Website
 
 
 
 
 
Customer Portal

## History Reports

`Reports/History_Reports.htm`

You are here: Driver & Event Management > Event Management > History Reports
History Reports
History Reports includes the option to print out no fee MVRs. The MVRs generated using History
Reports is for internal MVD use only.
For situations when an MVR is required for law enforcement or another agency who would
normally get them at no cost, use the Vehicle MVR or Driver MVR service and adjust the fees
to $0.00.
Generate No Fee MVR (Internal Use Only)
Change State of Record (CSOR)

## MVD Compliance Required Report AKA Reinstatement Requirements Report

`Reports/MVD_Compliance_Required_Report.htm`

You are here: Reports > MVD Compliance Required Report AKA Reinstatement
Requirements Report
MVD Compliance Required Report AKA Reinstatement Requirements
Report
This report is designed print and give to customers a summary of all open and future compliance items they
must satisfy. This report should only be given directly to the customer on the report, no other party should
ever be given this information.
Only create one report, per day. As generating more than one report will add all copies to the
customer's documents.
Generating the Report
1. In Driver Record service, search for the customer who is requesting this information.
2. Click Timeline.

3. Click View MVD Compliance Required Report.
4. Click Create Document.

5. The report will generate and display as a download. Click to open and then print.
Example Report
1. Eligible for Immediate Action: Displays what can be taken care of today.
2. Eligible for Future Action: Displays what the customer will need to take care of after any required hard
time, if applicable.
3. Actions Required at Next Driver Licensing Service: Displays the final compliance(s) that must be
satisfied before reinstatement.

 
HELPFUL LINKS
MVD / DMV Public Website
 
 
 
 
 

Customer Portal

## Vehicle History Detail Report

`Reports/Vehicle_History_Detail_Report.htm`

You are here: Reports > Vehicle History Detail Report
Vehicle History Detail Report
This report displays a detailed history of the title for a specific vehicle and contains the history of
services that were completed in MAX (or within the state of Arizona).
This report is for MVD INTERNAL USE ONLY, do not give to customers.
This report does NOT include the complete Title History for vehicles across all states. See
AAMVA Helpdesk - Vehicle for more information on viewing that history.

## Vehicle Snapshot - Accommodative Data

`Reports/Vehicle_Snapshot.htm`

You are here: Reports > Vehicle Snapshot - Accommodative Data
Vehicle Snapshot - Accommodative Data
At the point the legacy system (TPX) is no longer available for reference, the data has been uploaded
into Accommodative Data services located in MAX for reference. There are 4 different data resources
that have been made available:
Mobile Home Snapshot
Plate Credit
Vehicle Snapshot
Customer Details (see Customer Details - Accommodative Data for more specific details on
this resource)
Each of these historical data resources work similarly. All data is a snapshot of the customer or vehicle
as of April 20, 2020, when MAX went live.

MAX Known Issues

Keyword(s)
Issue / Error
Description
What You Should Do
ARO/SPI
Adding
OOS Title to
ARO/SPI
When adding a an OOS title
to an ARO/SPI, CSR receives
'Error - Title Out of
Jurisdiction Date Cannot Be
Greater Than Ownership
Date.'
Temporary Work Around: Look
the vehicle up in AAMVA -
Vehicle Helpdesk (NM14) and
use the last Title Issue Date that
is BEFORE the ARO ownership
date as the title issue date in the
service. The permanent fix
should be in MAX by March
30th.
Credential
Requirements
Vision Exam
Report
MAX is requiring all Visual
Fields to be completed before
CSR can save the Vision
Exam Report results.
Enter 0 in each empty field to
activate the Save button.
Credential
Requirements
Unable to Save
OOS Credential
from Guam
When adding a Guam DL on
the Credential tab, must
select the correct Country
and State.
Add the Guam license to the
credential tab and if the dates
are correct/current it will auto
satisfy the test requirements.
You must make sure when
adding it to the credential tab the
country must be United States
and the state is Guam (GU).
Credential
Requirements
Limited term
credential (ie
Foreign)- Vision
Requirement and
No application
fee
When doing a limited term
credential (i.e. Foreign) the
Vision Requirement is added
when it shouldn't be. It's also
not adding fees for the
credential service.
Waive the vision requirement
and add the Limited Term fee for
$10 in taxes and fees screen.
Credential
Requirements
Test Examiner
dropdowns
In MAX, the Select Examiner
dropdown does not include
any of my Examiners.
A change has been requested. In
the meantime, just select anyone
from the list.

Credential
Restrictions
Unable to add B
restriction
Especially when an Out of State
(OOS) DL is presented with a
restriction, MAX isn’t allowing
the restriction to be added on
the Requirements tab, even after
adding the OOS credential on
the Credential tab.
 
Temporary Workaround:
1. Add the OOS Credential on the
Credential tab, with the Corrective
Lenses Restriction.
2. On the Requirements tab, click
Add Restriction to manually add the
Vision Requirement.
3. Edit the Vision requirement,
select Vision Screening, select
Pass, and indicate Corrective
Lenses restriction here!
4. On the Requirements tab click
Add Restriction to add the restriction
to the credential.
Note: If you didn’t add the
Restriction in the Vision screening,
you won’t be able to add it here and
will need to submit a MAX Support
Form to have Level 2 help you fix it.
Credential
Restrictions
Issuing Credential
to Remove
Restriction
 
When reinstating a driver who
was eligible for a restricted in the
past, MAX is still issuing a
restricted license when the
driver should be issued a full
non-restricted credential.
Temporary workaround: The first
issuance service after completing a
'Compliance fee' service may give
the driver a restricted credential, if it
does and you can verify the driver
should NOT be restricted, a second
issuance (no fee) service should be
done and that will produce a full
non-restricted credential. If you
restart a Credential Issuance
Service from WIP, you may need to
delete and restart the service to
remove the Restriction.

Credential
Restrictions
M Endorsement
with 3-wheel
restriction + TID
MAX is not allowing an M
Endorsement with 3-wheel
restriction and Travel ID.
CSR needs to complete two
different services, allowing the
first to finalize before adding the
second:
1. To issue the TID.
2. To issue the Credential adding
the M Endorsement and 3-wheel
Restriction (Do NOT click Class
D or Travel on this one - only
click the endorsement you are
adding).
Customer
Account
Customer
Account is
required
After clicking Pay Now within
a service and moving to the
checkout screen, MAX
provides an error that the
"Customer Account is
required". This means that
the customer (agent) in the
service (on the right panel of
the checkout screen) is not a
Level 3 customer. In other
words, they are missing at
least one of the 4
requirements for a customer
account.
Review Customer Account
Requirements in MVD One
Source and review the Customer
Record to ensure all 4
requirements have been met (3
requirements for Organizations).
 
 
Customer
Record
Customer Name
Change
When doing a customer
name change, if the customer
is over 18, MAX is not asking
for the Name Change
Documentation.
CSRs need to be aware of this
issue and continue to upload the
Name Change documentation
even if not being prompted for it.

Customer
Record
Duplicate
Customer
Records
Two records exists for the
same customer:
1. One record is a Level 3
record with an SSN and AZ
MVD Now access.
2. The other record is an old
'pseudo' record that displays
the customer's vehicles.
At this time, you must follow the
process for combining records.
In the meantime, you can do the
following to ensure all payment
receipts for services are visible
to the Level 3 customer with AZ
MVD Now access:
1. Ask questions to validate both
customer records - Don't
provide confidential
information to the customer
over the phone.
2. Ensure that the record with
the SSN is a Level 3 customer -
if not, make that record a Level 3
customer.
3. Complete a TR Corrections
Service, then submit a Combine
Records Request and complete
the original service as normal.
Customer
Record -
Address
Updating a
customer record
from an Out of
Country address
to a US Address
When updating an address
on a customer record, and
the prior address is from a
country other than the US,
there is a bug where you may
get an error if you update the
address fields in the wrong
order.
Until this is fixed, to avoid this
error, enter United States as the
country FIRST, then update the
state and county fields.
Fees
Re-Application
Fee not
populating for
Credentials
Reapplication fee: MAX is not
collecting the $25
reapplication fee Its collecting
$12. The reason is that after
collecting the suspension fee
the customer no longer
shows suspended and the
system collects for a dup DL.
.
The fees can be manually
adjusted to $25 - click Add Fee
on the Taxes and Fees screen in
the Credential Issuance service.
If a CSR forgets to manually
adjust the fee, we will take the
loss - we will not call the
customer back in or ask them to
pay after the fact. It may still be a
QA finding, though.
ID

customers with

Vehicles (ABV)

are creating withdrawals for
both T&R and Drivers
services. When issuing a

on the withdrawal.

in the Credential Issuance

with the Service.

# T_&_R


## Add Insurance

`T_&_R/Mandatory_Insurance/Add_Insurance.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mandatory Insurance > Add Insurance
Add Insurance
When a customer obtains insurance for themselves or a vehicle they own, the insurance company
electronically submits coverage details to Arizona Insurance Verification System (AZIVS) at
arizonaivs.com. MAX interfaces with that company to confirm insurance coverage. In special
circumstances where customer has proof of insurance but the information has not yet been submitted
electronically, certain insurance policies can be added manually in MAX during a registration service
(e.g., update registration, renew registration, etc.). When added manually do NOT enter an expiration
date, an error will populate if you do (this does not apply to Military Vehicle Insurance).
MAX requires insurance companies to submit liability and binder insurance information
electronically
A CSR may manually enter a collectible or military insurance policy on the vehicle
information page or during a registration service, view the Search Insurance - Vehicle
section for more information on how to verify insurance that was submitted electronically
** It is not recommended by the Division to add insurance policies manually but there are
special circumstances that may require a manual entry for example Military Vehicle Insurance.
If insurance is manually added in MAX and it's not confirmed with AZIVS within 30 days, an
incident for a mandatory insurance requirement will be created and a notification will be sent to
the customer. If they do not provide the necessary proof of insurance within the defined period,
a withdrawal is created and their registration will be suspended.
There are several types of insurance. Some are associated to a vehicle, others, like an SR22 is
associated to a customer. This also means you will view, add, edit insurance in the respective area
where the insurance is associated.

Insurance Associated to Vehicle
(available in Vehicle Information page)
Insurance Associated to a Customer
(available in Customer Record)
Binder Policy
Certificate of Deposit
Collectible Vehicle Insurance
Fleet Policy
Commercial Insurance
SR22
Liability
Self Insurance
Military Insurance
 
MAX will only allow a CSR to add military, collectible, and commercial insurance.
For vehicles that have a 'Financial responsibility - no insurance' withdrawal, the insurance
company MUST submit the insurance electronically.
Search Insurance - Vehicle
Search Insurance - Customer
Insurance Information
Add Insurance to Vehicle
Adding a SR22 Insurance to Customer
Add Military Vehicle Insurance
There are other services in MAX where Insurance can be added as part of the service. Those include:
Registration Only
Title & Reg
Update Registration
Renew Registration

## Commercial Vehicle Insurance Requirements

`T_&_R/Mandatory_Insurance/Commercial_Vehicle_Insurance_ Requirements.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mandatory Insurance > Commercial
Vehicle Insurance Requirements
Commercial Vehicle Insurance Requirements
An owner or operator of a motor vehicle that is used in a commercial enterprise in Arizona will comply
with Arizona’s financial responsibility requirements when the motor vehicle:
Has a declared gross vehicle weight rating (GVWR) of more than 20,000 lbs.
Transports hazardous materials, hazardous substances or hazardous wastes
Operates as a bus to transport passengers for hire (other than a car pool operator)
Is listed on the Division’s records as a taxi, livery vehicle or limousine that is used to transport
passengers for hire
While proof of insurance is not required at the time of registration, if the motor carrier voluntarily submits
proof of current insurance, the pertinent insurance information will need to be added to the Vehicle
Information Page.
The blue Self-Certification Affidavits are no longer acceptable as proof of insurance.

may be directed to the Mandatory Insurance Unit by opening a Service Portal ticket.

## De-Insure

`T_&_R/Mandatory_Insurance/De-Insure.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mandatory Insurance > De-Insurance
Certificate
De-Insure
A vehicle owner or lessee who does not operate a vehicle on a state highway in this state, is not
required to meet the financial responsibility (insurance) requirements. The owner must submit a De-
Insured Certificate, form #46-0602, stating the vehicle is not operational, will be placed in storage, or will
not be operated on a highway in this state.
The De-Insured Certificate is only valid until the vehicle's expiration date, or after one year, whichever is
less.
Registration credit will not be given when a motor vehicle is being de-insured.
An owner or lessee convicted of a financial responsibility violation is not eligible to submit a de-
insured certification for a one-year period, from the date of conviction.
Vehicles with a MI Withdrawal are not eligible for a 30-Day General Use Permit or 90-Day
Resident Registration.
Questions pertaining to de-insuring a vehicle may be directed to the Mandatory Insurance Unit by
opening a Service Portal ticket.

## Mandatory Insurance Actions

`T_&_R/Mandatory_Insurance/Mandatory_Insurance_Actions.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mandatory Insurance > Mandatory
Insurance Actions
Mandatory Insurance Actions
Insurers who issue, cancel or become aware of a cancellation, non-renewal of or failure to renew a
motor vehicle liability insurance policy issued on a motor vehicle in this state will notify the Division.
Once notified, it becomes a Mandatory Insurance action and can lead to a Mandatory Insurance (MI)
Withdrawal. The MI Withdrawal will show in the withdrawal description as 'Financial responsibility - no
insurance' on the Vehicle Information Page.
Proof of insurance must be submitted electronically by the insurance company before the
registration is eligible to be reinstated.
A valid MI Withdrawal will not prevent the owner of a motor vehicle selling the motor vehicle to another
person or prevent the transferee from registering the motor vehicle, view Vehicle is Sold with
MI Withdrawal section for more information.
A MI Withdrawal must be cleared before the following services can be performed on the
vehicle:
Duplicate registration
Registration renewal
Lost plate or tab
Use of registration credit
A 3-Day Restricted Use Permit may be issued when there is a MI Withdrawal.
Cancellation, Non-Renewal or Failure to Renew an Insurance Policy
Suspending the Registration and License Plates
Reinstating the Registration and License Plates
Vehicle is Sold with MI Withdrawal
Accident Cases

## Non-Commercial Vehicle Insurance Requirements

`T_&_R/Mandatory_Insurance/Non-Commercial_Vehicle_Insurance_Requirements.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mandatory Insurance > Non-Commercial
Vehicle Insurance Requirements
Non-Commercial Vehicle Insurance Requirements
The Division requires every owner or lessee of a registered non-commercial motor vehicle to comply
with the Arizona financial responsibility (insurance) laws, unless exempted by statute. View the policy
6.1.1 Noncommercial Vehicle Insurance Requirements for a list of vehicles that are exempt from
financial responsibility.
The owner of a registered vehicle must provide the Division with proof of liability insurance within 30
days of the initial registration.
If insurance is not provided within the 30 days, the Division will review the Vehicle Information
Page to determine if the vehicle is properly insured, or if a notice of intent to suspend the
license plate and registration is required.
A person operating a motor vehicle in Arizona shall have evidence of insurance within the vehicle at all
times.
Questions pertaining to noncommercial vehicle financial responsibility requirements may be directed to
the Mandatory Insurance Unit by opening a Service Portal ticket.

## Self Insurance

`T_&_R/Mandatory_Insurance/Self_Insurance.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mandatory Insurance > Self Insurance
Self Insurance
An owner who has at least ten motor vehicles registered in the owner’s name or a motor carrier who is
required to comply with Arizona financial responsibility requirements may be eligible for a Certificate of
Self-Insurance or Partial Self-Insurance.
The owner or motor carrier applying for a Certificate of Self or Partial Self-Insurance will submit:
Certificate of Self-Insurance Application, form #26-0501
Evidence that the owner or motor carrier is financially able and will continue to be able to pay the
entire amount of the Certificate of Self or Partial Self-Insurance allowed by the Division for
judgments obtained against the owner for liability arising out of the ownership, maintenance, or
use of a motor vehicle
Evidence that the owner or motor carrier has a valid insurance policy issued by a captive insurer
that is domiciled and authorized by the Department of Insurance and Financial Institutions to
transact business in this state and that provides minimum coverage
Other information as required by the Division
Questions pertaining to Self-Insurance or data entry of the insurance may be directed to the
Mandatory Insurance Unit by opening a Service Portal ticket.
View the Add Insurance topic > Search Insurance - Customer section for more information on how to
add self-insurance.

## SR22

`T_&_R/Mandatory_Insurance/SR22.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Mandatory Insurance > SR22
SR22
An SR22 insurance policy is proof of future financial responsibility. When a person is convicted of a traffic violation or fails to maintain
liability insurance coverage for a motor vehicle owned or leased by the person, they will be required to show proof and carry SR22
Insurance for:
Withdrawal Type
SR22 Required Until
DUI (including Implied Consent)
3 years from the Withdrawal End
Date
Financial Responsibility Withdrawal
3 years from the Withdrawal Start
Date
Judgments
Required throughout the term of
the judgment plus a full 2 years
AFTER satisfaction of the
judgment
Court-ordered suspensions and any suspensions imposed by the Division may run concurrently with any other suspensions.
Once the SR22 is submitted, it becomes a requirement for maintaining a person's driver license. Failure to maintain the SR22 as required
will result in a 'Failure to maintain SR22' Withdrawal of the person's driving privilege.
View the Financial Responsibility topic > Financial Responsibility & Judgment sections for more information on Withdrawals that require an
SR22.
The Central Issuance Mailing Dashboard > Processing Dates tab provides the current date an SR22 that was mailed is being
worked.
Requirements for Needing an SR22
A person cited for driving without insurance
A driver found guilty of driving under the influence (DUI) of alcohol*
A driver that refused to be tested for possibly driving under the influence
A person who defaults or cancels on the initial SR22 requirement
*Typically the SR22 requirement for a DUI is for a second or subsequent DUI conviction. However, there are rare instances that a
first time DUI would have an SR22 requirement. This is when the DUI conviction from the court comes through to MAX before the
Admin Per Se (from law enforcement) does.

## Apportioned Registration

`T_&_R/Motor_Carrier/Apportioned_Registration.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Motor Carrier > Apportioned Registration
Apportioned Registration
The Division may provide for an apportioned registration, which allows commercial vehicles operating
on an interstate basis in two or more International Registration Plan (IRP) member jurisdiction(s). Under
the IRP agreement, the Division is authorized to:
Collect registration fees based on the percentage of fleet miles operated in various member
jurisdictions requested by the applicant
Transmit the registration fees remitted by the applicant to the member jurisdictions requested by
the applicant
Issue a license plate and registration card (cab card) per vehicle that provides for payment of
vehicle registration fees based on the total mileage operated in all jurisdictions the fleet vehicle is
given apportioned registration
Apportioned Registration, refer applicant to the Motor Carrier Unit at mvdmcs@azdot.gov or 602-712-
6775.
Example of Cab Card
Titles and IRP Apportioned Registrations
The following are three different scenarios involving IRP Apportioned Registrations and how you will
process in MAX:
For each of the below scenarios, the rules regarding documentation are the same as a regular
non-apportioned title and/or or registration service.
Scenario #1: Customer has an IRP apportioned registration in another state, but
needs the vehicle titled in Arizona:
Scenario #2: The customer has an apportioned registration in Arizona (ARO)
and wants to transfer the vehicles title to Arizona from another state:
Scenario #3: The customer currently has an Arizona IRP Apportioned
Registration Only (ARO) but wants to move the title to Arizona:
Scenario #4: The customer has an apportioned registration, but wants to make
it a regular registration (and get unused fees refunded):

## Interstate Registration (Non-Resident)

`T_&_R/Motor_Carrier/Interstate_Registration_(Non-Resident).htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Motor Carrier > Interstate Registration
(Non-Resident)
Interstate Registration (Non-Resident)
The Division may provide a Nonresident Interstate Registration to owners of commercially operated
motor vehicles registered in another jurisdiction, who are conducting interstate operations, and who do
not qualify or do not wish to register under the International Registration Plan (IRP).
The Division is authorized to:
Collect registration fees based on the vehicle's declared gross weight
Issue an Interstate Border Permit and registration
An interstate vehicle is a foreign motor vehicle owned by a nonresident and that is:
Operated in Arizona for the transportation of passengers or property for compensation
To conduct business, to be registered and licensed in Arizona in accordance with ARS 28-2321.A
Motor vehicles owned by a nonresident that would be exempt from payment of weight fees
under ARS 28-5432, if owned by a resident of Arizona, are exempt from the above registration
requirements.
Interstate Registration, refer applicant to the Motor Carrier Program at
IRegistration@azdot.gov or 602-712-6775.

## 3-Day Restricted Use Permit

`T_&_R/Permits/3-Day_ Restricted_Use_Permit.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Permits > 3-Day Restricted Use Permit
3-Day Restricted Use Permit
A 3-Day Restricted Use Permit (3-Day Permit) allows a person to legally operate an unregistered
vehicle from the vehicle's present location to a specified destination. The 3-Day Permit is valid for the
following purposes only:
Emissions testing
Vehicle inspection
Application for title or registration
Vehicle repair to comply with emissions or inspection
Vehicle permits will not be issued if a NSF Withdrawal is on the Vehicle Information Page.
More Information about a 3-Day Restricted Use Permit
Issuing a 3-Day Restricted Use Permit
Duplicate 3-Day Restricted Use Permit
A customer can request a duplicate 3-Day Restricted Use Permit that has been lost, stolen, or mutilated
with the Division for $4.00.
Or the customer may reprint from home through AZ MVD Now or use a kiosk in office to print a
duplicate permit for no fee.
View the Duplicate Permit topic for more information.

## 30-Day General Use Permit

`T_&_R/Permits/30-Day_General_Use_Permit.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Permits > 30-Day General Use Permit
30-Day General Use Permit
The 30-Day General Use Permit allows an unregistered vehicle to operate on the roadway for a 30 day period, and can
be issued only once in a 12-month period. The 30-Day General Use Permit is valid in and out-of-state and has no
movement restrictions or residency requirements.
The permit may be purchased with the Division or through AZ MVD Now for $15.00.
 
 
 
When the customer completes a Title and Registration service, the data from the permit(s) will be used to
convert to a title and registration record. MAX will display an alert to notify the CSR of the permit conversion.
When 30-Day Permits can be Issued
An owner of a vehicle may request a 30-day permit for any reason as long as a prohibition is not present on the vehicle
record. See 30-Day General Use Permit section for more information.
A Commercial International Registration Plan (IRP) Apportioned vehicle is not eligible to receive a 30-Day
General Use Permit. Motor carriers may use temporary proportional registrations for vehicles that are added
to an existing fleet or in lieu of lost registrations pending receipt of permanent or replacement registrations.
Prohibitions to Issuing a 30-Day Permit
Required Documentation for a 30-Day Permit
Proof of ownership is required for a permit to be issued.
Vehicle Certificate of Title
Vehicle registration
Bill of sale
Dealer invoice
Copy of security agreement
Authorized possession of the vehicle
When an ownership document cannot be provided, the vehicle needs a Level I Inspection. A supervisor’s approval is
required before issuing a 30-Day General Use Permit. The customer may need to process a bonded title if unable to
obtain the ownership document. View the Bonded Title topic for more information on bonds.

30-Day General Use Permits Issued on AZ MVD Now
Issuing a 30-Day General Use Permit
Duplicate 30-Day General Use Permit
A customer can request a duplicate 30-Day General Use Permit that has been lost, stolen, or mutilated with the Division
for $4.00.
Or the customer may reprint from home through AZ MVD Now or use a kiosk in office to print a duplicate permit for no
fee.
View the Duplicate Permit topic for more information.
Policies
8.11.1 Restricted Use 3-Day Permit, 30-Day General Use Permit, 90-Day Resident Registration
8.11.3 Temporary Registration Plate (TRP)

## 30-Day Non-Resident OHV Permit

`T_&_R/Permits/30-Day_Non-Res_OHV.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Permits > 30-Day Non-Resident OHV
Permit
30-Day Non-Resident OHV Permit
The Nonresident Off-Highway Vehicle (OHV) 30 day permit is issued only to nonresidents of Arizona
with a qualifying OHV. This permit is available as not all states offer registration to off-highway vehicles
and Arizona requires registration (including decals or permits) to operate the vehicle in this state.
Arizona residents are not eligible for this permit
Must have a Non-Resident OHV decal issued from the Game & Fish (G&F) Department prior to
applying for the permit
Permit may be purchased in field or ATP offices, or through the Contact Center
Only vehicles listed with a Vehicle Types as 'All Terrain Vehicle' or 'Golf Cart' in MAX qualify for
the permit
For more information, see the Qualifying OHVs section
Permit is valid for 30 calendar days
No limit to the number of permits that can be acquired within a 12 month period
A new permit can only be issued after the current permit has expired
Small size permit (size of small trailer plate)
Cost is $15.00
The non-resident has to certify that:
Their OHV is titled in another state
They have a non-resident OHV decal from Game & Fish (G&F)
Their OHV meets the definition of a qualified OHV
Certification will be completed when the customer signs the Affidavit for 30 Day Non-Resident
OHV Permit (printed after the permit has been issued in MAX).
Qualifying OHVs
The OHV must have all of the following:
Is designed primarily for recreational non-highway all-terrain travel
Is not more than 80 inches in width
Has an unladen weight of not more than 2,500 lbs.

Travels on four or more non-highway tires
Has a steering wheel for steering control
Has a rollover protective structure
Has an occupant retention system (safety restraints; e.g., seat belts, rollover protection structure)
Essentially, qualifying OHVs are a side-by-side OHV, has four wheels, and can't have handle
bars for steering.
Examples of Qualifying OHVs
Examples of Non Qualifying OHVs
Field Office: Issuing the Nonresident Off-Highway Vehicle (OHV) 30 Day Permit
and Generating Affidavit
Contact Center: Issuing the Nonresident Off-Highway Vehicle (OHV) 30 Day
Permit and Generating Affidavit
Out of State OHVs
The Nonresident Off-Highway Vehicle 30 Day Permit is issued to allows vehicles to be operated on
maintained roads, including maintained forest roads.
If the vehicle is to be operated off-road, the vehicle will need a Non-Resident OHV decal. For additional
information, see the Off-Highway Vehicle (OHV) Decal topic > Out-of-State OHVs section.

## 90-Day Non-Resident (Drive-Out) Permit

`T_&_R/Permits/90-Day_Non-Resident_Permit.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Permits > 90-Day Non-Resident (Drive-
Out) Permit
90-Day Non-Resident (Drive-Out) Permit
The 90-Day (Drive-Out) Permit allows a non-resident to drive their vehicle back to the state in which
they reside and valid for 90 days from the date of issuance or upon foreign state registration, whichever
comes first.
The permit may be purchased with the Division or an Arizona licensed motor vehicle dealer for $15.00.
When needed, the Division may issue the permit for a dealer sale on behalf of a motor vehicle dealer
licensed to sell new or used vehicles.
Issued to commercial vehicles that do not transport persons or property in Arizona (A semi-trailer
that is manufactured in Arizona, and purchased by a non-resident qualifies for the permit to
transport goods from Arizona)
If a customer has been unable to leave the state within the 90-day time period, a 30-Day
General Use permit may be issued. If the 90-Day and subsequent 30-Day General Use Permit
expires and the vehicle remains in Arizona, the owner must register the vehicle.
When 90-Day Non-Resident Permits can be Issued
A non-resident who purchases a vehicle in Arizona and will be registering the vehicle in their
home state regardless if the vehicle is driven or delivered out-of-state
An enrolled member of an Indian tribe, who resides on a reservation, and who purchases a
vehicle in Arizona, but will be using the vehicle exclusively on their reservation and will not
register it in Arizona
Required Documentation for a 90-Day Non-Resident
Vehicle title
Vehicle registration
Bill of sale
Dealer invoice
Manufacturer’s Certificate of Origin (MCO)
Proof of Non-Residency

Yes, proof of non-residency is required. The customer must present one of the following:
Out-of-state driver license
Indian reservation physical address for a member of an Indian Reservation and Tribal enrollment,
census number, or identification number
Additional Information on a 90-Day Non-Resident Permit
The purchaser of a 90-Day Non-Resident Permit who registers the vehicle in Arizona within 365 days
after issuance of the permit will be liable for any Arizona Department of Revenue (ADOR) tax, penalty,
and interest due to ADOR. It is the sole responsibility of ADOR to collect monies due.
When the permit is printed, an Affidavit for Arizona 90-Day Nonresident Permit prints at the same
time. You will give the permit to the customer and have the customer sign the affidavit. The affidavit will
be placed with your data processing paperwork (DPs).
Issuing a 90-Day Non-Resident Permit and Generating Affidavit
Duplicate 90-Day Non-Resident Permit
A customer can request a duplicate 90-Day Non-Resident Permit that has been lost, stolen, or mutilated
with the Division for $4.00.
Or the customer may reprint from home through AZ MVD Now or use a kiosk in office to print a
duplicate permit for no fee.
View the Duplicate Permit topic or service to learn more.
Permits & Licensed Motor Vehicle Dealers
A motor vehicle dealer licensed to sell a new or used vehicle can issue a 90-Day Non-Resident Permit
electronically through the use of an Authorized Third Party (ATP) provider.
The 90-Day Non-Resident permit will not be issued to:
A vehicle delivered in Arizona to an Arizona resident by an out-of-state dealer
A dealer-to-dealer purchase or an auction-to-dealer purchase

## 90-Day Resident Registration

`T_&_R/Permits/90-Day_Resident_Permit.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Permits > 90-Day Resident Registration
90-Day Resident Registration
The 90-Day Resident Registration allows Arizona residents to operate their unregistered vehicle on an Arizona roadway
for a 90 calendar-day period while they take care of issues preventing their vehicle from being titled or registered in
Arizona.
The 90-Day Resident Registration is valid in and out-of-state and has no movement restrictions.
The permit may be purchased with the Division or by an authorized financial institution for $15.00.
 
 
When the customer completes a Title and Registration service, the data from the permit(s) will be used to
convert to a title and registration record. MAX will display an alert to notify the CSR of the permit conversion.
When a 90-Day Resident Registrations Can Be Issued
Prohibitions to Issuing a 90-Day Resident Registration
Required Documentation for 90-Day Resident Registration
Dealership Processing Title Work
No Documentation Available
Issuing a 90-Day Resident Registration
1. From the Services menu, select Issue Permit.
2. Search for the Customer and Vehicle related to this service.

3. Once the Customer and Vehicle are located and selected, click Add.
Note: If the vehicle is not in the system it will need to be added to complete the MAX Service. After verifying
the vehicle is not in the system, you can Add a Vehicle
4. Click 90 Day Resident.
While the service is open in MAX, if you hover your mouse over the Customer and Vehicle tabs you can
quickly confirm you have the right information.
5. Click Confirm.

6. In Service Summary, review information for accuracy and click Continue to calculate taxes and fees.

7. The Taxes and Fees screen displays. Review for accuracy and click Pay Now if the customer is ready to pay. If you
need to complete additional services for the customer, click Add to Cart so you can collect payment when you are
completed with all services.
8. Enter the payment amount.
9. In the Checkout screen, process the payment for the service(s) and click Complete Checkout (see Payment
Processing).

10. Download and print the service document(s) and provide  to the customer. Note: If you need to access the
Payment Receipt or any of the service documents in the future, search for the customer record, and view the
customer's Documents. The customer can access these documents in AZ MVD Now.
11. Click the printer icon in the upper right hand corner of the downloaded document(s).
12. Click Print in the print dialogue box.

## Cancel Permit

`T_&_R/Permits/Cancel_Permit.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Permits > Cancel Permit
Cancel Permit
When a permit was issued in error (e.g., 3-Day was issued and customer really needs a 30-Day), the
Cancel Permit service will be used.
If a Temporary Registration Plate/Permit (TRP) needs to be canceled, you will NOT use the
Cancel Permit service!
Dealers will cancel the TRP in the Dealer Suite of Service Arizona. In the rare instance where a
TRP needs to be canceled by MVD, please have an upline approver submit a Service Portal
ticket, including the customer #, VIN, and TRP #, and request a Cancel TRP service.
Permits that can be Canceled
3-Day Restricted Use Permit

## Oversize & Overweight Permits

`T_&_R/Permits/Oversize_&_Overweight_Permits.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Permits > Oversize & Overweight Permits
Oversize and Overweight Permits
The Class A oversize or overweight permits are issued for vehicle and load combinations that are within
specific size and weight limits. The load must be non-reducible.
The Class A permit is issued for a single trip or 30 days. Class A permits are issued only for travel on
state routes.
The following eligibility criteria must be met before issuing a Class A permit:
Is non-reducible
Has a specifically described load
Has a width and/or load is 14 feet or fewer
Has a height and/or load is 16 feet or fewer
Has a length and/or load is 120 feet or fewer
Has a combined weight of vehicle and/or vehicle combination is 250,000 lbs. or fewer
This permit may not be used to routinely transport legal loads on over-width trailers.
A vehicle must be currently registered for 80,000 lbs.
Fees
Oversize single-trip: $15
Oversize 30-day: $30
Oversize or overweight single-trip: $75
Oversize or overweight 30-day: $75
Visit Oversize/Overweight Permits for more information on these types of permits, including
how/where to purchase.

## Temporary Registration Plate (TRP)

`T_&_R/Permits/Temporary_Registration_Plate_(TRP).htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > TRPs > Temporary Registration Plate (TRP)
Temporary Registration Plate (TRP)
A 45-Day Temporary Registration Plate (TRP) is a paper plate that is issued when the customer has
purchased a vehicle from an Arizona Licensed Dealer.
The dealership issues the TRP in order for the vehicle to be driven legally while the paperwork is processed.
The dealership may only issue one TRP per vehicle.
Once the vehicle is sold to a bona fide purchaser, the dealer has 30 days in which to submit the transaction
to the Division before a late title transfer penalty fee will be assessed.
The TRP will display in the Permits section on the Vehicle Information page, along with who the permit was
issued to. No FLP will display or which dealership issued the permit.
*A Temporary Registration Permit (TRP) OR Sales Contract/Invoice is NOT acceptable to issue an
Arizona Registration Only (ARO)/ Special Interstate Registration (SPI).
If there is a valid TRP in place, a customer cannot obtain an ARO. The customer must wait until the
vehicle is fully registered in the state where the TRP was issued before registering the vehicle in
Arizona.
Lost TRP
Converting a TRP to a Title and Registration Record
The TRP record will be converted into a title and registration record when the proper paperwork has been
submitted.
A message, as shown below, will display when the permit is recognized, noting that the permit will be
converted into a T&R record. View the Title and Registration service for more information on converting the
record to a full T&R record.
 
 
 
When the customer completes a Title and Registration service, the data from the permit(s) will be
used to convert to a title and registration record. MAX will display an alert to notify the CSR of the
permit conversion.

When the vehicle is registered for full registration period, fees are assessed based on the TRP issue date.
Expired TRP
If the TRP was voided or has expired before the title and registration transaction is completed, an online 30-
Day General Use Permit may be issued by the dealership.
Confirming Registration Credit from another Plate
The plate credit will display in the Permit section of the Vehicle Information page under TRP Credit
Description.
Or you may look up the credit by:
1. Search for the customer record to whom the TRP was issued.
2. Click the Suitcase icon in the upper right hand corner of the customer record.
3. The plate number entered at the time the TRP was created is the name of a new Credit Account.
4. The amount of the credit to be transferred displays under the account balance.
5. Click on View to see the Account Details.
When the Title and Registration service is completed, the amount in the Credit Account is sent to the
dealer that created the TRP. If there is any additional funds they are credited back to the customer
account where they can request a disbursement for any money over the fees for the T&R service.
 
Policies
8.11.1 Restricted Use 3-Day Permit, 30-Day General Use Permit, 90-Day Resident Registration
8.11.2 90-Day Nonresident (Drive-Out)
8.11.3 Temporary Registration Plate (TRP)

## Cancel Disability Placard (Permanent or Temporary)

`T_&_R/Placards/Cancel_Disability_Placard_(Permanent_or_Temporary).htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Placards > Cancel Disability Placard
(Permanent or Temporary)
Cancel Disability Placard (Permanent or Temporary)
When a placard was issued in error (e.g., Temporary was issued and should have been Permanent), the
Cancel Disability Placard service will be used.
If a customer comes in with a placard that needs to be canceled, the placard should be destroyed. The
placard record in MAX will remain. When a customer is deceased, the information is automatically updated to
the MAX customer record from the Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS) and will automatically
cancel the placard.
The steps listed below will work for canceling either type of placard, as they function the same way in MAX.
1. From the Services menu, select Cancel Temporary Placard or Cancel Permanent Placard, depending
on the type of placard you need to cancel.
2. Search for the Customer related to this service.
3. Identify the Placard to cancel under Placard Results and click the Select icon.

4. Click Add.
5. Click Continue.

6. This is a no fee service. If you want to continue, click Pay Now to finalize the transaction. If you need to
complete additional services for the customer, click Add to Cart.
7. Service is final. A Payment Receipt can be downloaded and printed for the customer if requested. The
customer can access these documents in AZ MVD Now.

8. To verify the cancellation of the placard, review the Placards section of the Customer Record, under
View History. The Status will show Canceled.
