## Permanent Disability Placard

`T_&_R/Placards/Permanent_Disability_Placard.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Placards > Permanent Disability Placard
Permanent Disability Placard
The Permanent Disability Placard is issued to an individual with a permanent physical disability or to an
organization (profit or nonprofit) that owns or leases a motor vehicle that primarily transports persons
with physical disabilities. View the Placards Overview for more information on placards.
This placard is valid for as long as the person to whom it is issued qualifies for the issuance.
For individuals, up to three Permanent Disability Placards may be issued if the Division determines the
applicant's request demonstrates why the placards are required.
For nonprofit organizations, that provide assistance to senior citizens, may be issued up to three
Permanent Disability Placards and shall maintain record on each volunteer who used placards to
transport persons with temporary or permanent physical disabilities (trusts do not qualify).
Permanent placards are two-sided and blue/white.
Issuing a Permanent Disability Placard
Policies

Forms

## Placards Overview

`T_&_R/Placards/Placards_Overview.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Placards > Placards Overview
Placards Overview
A Disability placard can be issued by the Division to an individual who has a permanent or temporary
physical disability or is deaf/hard of hearing and has been determined eligible by a healthcare
professional. A completed Disability-Deaf/Hard of Hearing Plate/Placard Application - Individual, form
#96-0104 will be required.
If a Medical Doctor (MD) or Physician Assistant (PA) license number is missing on the
disability application, you may look up the license number on the Arizona Medical Board
website. To look up a license number of a Physical Therapist (PT) visit Arizona State Board
of Physical Therapy website.
See the Disability Plate & Placard FAQs topic for additional information on disability
applications.
A Disability placard can be issued by the Division to an organization that uses a vehicle primarily to
transport persons with one or more of the conditions listed on the Organization Disability - Plate/Placard
Application, form #96-0104B (trusts do not qualify).
Based on the application, a permanent or temporary placard may be issued. Disability or Deaf/Hard of
Hearing plates may be issued as well for individuals with permanent disabilities or organizations, view
the License Plate Overview topic or the Personalized and Special Plates topic > Disability and Deaf/
Hard of Hearing section for more information on Disability and Deaf/Hard of Hearing plates.
There is no charge for a permanent or temporary placard (initial, renewal, or replacement).
The Permanent or Temporary Disability Placard must be displayed in any vehicle (upon parking)
in which the customer is the driver or passenger.
Either application will be scanned and uploaded into MAX and then filed with your data processing
paperwork.
The Permanent or Temporary Disability Placard are issued in MAX and recorded on the customer
record.
Individual's Eligibility for Placard
Medical Certification Exceptions
Deaf/Hard of Hearing Placards

The Deaf/Hard of Hearing placard:
Has no identifying numbers
Not processed in MAX
May be displayed in any vehicle operated by the placard holder
Is an 8 1/2" x 5 1/2" white/blue card
Can be ordered through OIMS (form #46-4315)
Kept in stock within the office
No fee for initial, renewal, or replacement
View the Personalized and Special Plates topic > Disability and Deaf/ Hard of Hearing section for more
information on Disability plates.
Issuing, Replacing, or Canceling Permanent or Temporary Placards

Temporary Disability Placard (Issue)

## Replace Disability Placard (Permanent or Temporary)

`T_&_R/Placards/Replace_Disability_Placard_(Permanent_or_Temporary).htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Placards > Replace Disability Placard
(Permanent or Temporary)
Replace Disability Placard (Permanent or Temporary)
The Replace Disability Placard service provides the ability to replace an existing Permanent or Temporary
Disability Placard for a customer without making any changes to the placard issue date or expiration date.
When the customer is in office, no forms are required to be completed for a replacement disability placard.
There is no fee charged for a replacement or a reprint (while waiting for official placard in mail).
For mail-in replacement requests, a customer may use the Disability- Deaf/Hard of Hearing
Plate/Placard Application - Individual, form #96-0104 or Organization Disability - Plate/Placard
Application, form #96-0104B. A Medical Certification is not required for replacement placards.
The steps listed below will work for replacing either type of placard, as they function the same way in MAX.
1. From the Services menu, select Replace Permanent Placard or Replace Temporary Placard,
depending on the type of placard you need to replace.
2. Search for the Customer related to this service.
3. Identify the Placard to replace under Placard Results and click the Select icon.
If no placards are found, you cannot complete this service for the customer. Check the customer
record to verify what, if any, placard can be replaced.

4. Click Add.
5. If the customer needs to use a one time mailing address for this service, click Add One Time Mailing
Address to add that address, view One Time Mailing Address for more details
Click Edit Customer to edit a customer's address, AKA's, characteristics or contact information, view
Edit Customer for more information

6. Click Confirm.
7. The Placard Issue Date will be the original issue date of the placard and cannot be changed with this
service. For Temporary Placards, the Placard Expiration Date will be the original expiration date cannot
be changed with this service. Click Continue.
8. This is a no fee service. If you want to continue, click Pay Now to finalize the transaction. If you need to
complete additional services for the customer, click Add to Cart.

9. Download and print the service document(s) and provide  to the customer. Note: If you need to access
the Payment Receipt or any of the service documents in the future, search for the customer record, and
view the customer's Documents. The customer can access these documents in AZ MVD Now.
10. Click the printer icon in the upper right hand corner of the downloaded document(s).
11. Click Print in the print dialogue box.

12. Provide the document(s) to the customer. The replacement placard will be mailed to the customer within
5-7 days.
Policies
8.16.6 Disability and Hearing Impaired License Plate Placard
Forms
#96-0104 Disability- Hearing Impaired Plate/Placard Application - Individual
#96-0104B Organization DisabilityPlate/Placard Application

## Temporary Disability Placard

`T_&_R/Placards/Temporary_Disability_Placard.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Placards > Temporary Disability Placard
Temporary Disability Placard
The Temporary Disability Placard is issued to an individual with a temporary physical disability or issued
to an organization (profit or nonprofit) that owns or leases a motor vehicle that primarily transports
persons with physical disabilities. View the Placards Overview topic for more information on placards.
This placard is valid for six months. A new Disability-Deaf/Hard of Hearing Plate/Placard Application -
Individual, form #96-0104, with a completed medical certification is required if the person who has a
temporary physical disability requires or requests a subsequent Temporary Disability Placard.
For individuals, up to three Temporary Disability Placards may be issued if the Division determines the
applicant's request demonstrates why the placards are required.
All placards must be issued the same day. If the customer comes back another day requesting an
additional placard, they must have a new Disability Application with a new medical certification
For nonprofit organizations, that provide assistance to senior citizens, may be issued up to three
Temporary Disability Placards and shall maintain record on each volunteer who used placards to
transport persons with temporary or permanent physical disabilities (trusts do not qualify).
Temporary Disability Placards are two-sided and red/white.
To qualify for a Temporary Disability Placard, the customer needs to provide a completed
Disability-Deaf/Hard of Hearing Plate/Placard Application (form 96-0104 or 96-0104b for
organizations).
The Medical Certification section must be completed by an authorized Physician, Registered
Nurse Practitioner, Audiologist, or Hospital Administrator and have the original wet signature of
the examiner (stamp signatures are not acceptable).
Issuing a Temporary Disability Placard
Renewing a Temporary Disability Placard
There is no option to renew a Temporary Disability Placard. A new Temporary Placard with a full six-
month use period may be issued upon completion of a new application and appropriate medical
certification.
Policies
8.16.6 Disability and Hearing Impaired License Plate Placard

Forms

## Abandoned Vehicle Overview

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Abandoned_Vehicle_Overview.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Abandoned Vehicle Overview
Abandoned Vehicle Overview
An abandoned vehicle is a vehicle, trailer, or semi-trailer that has been abandoned (whether it was lost,
stolen, abandoned or otherwise unclaimed) on a public, federal, state trust, national forest, state park,
Bureau of Land Management land or private property.
Evidence that a vehicle was left unattended is considered abandoned when the vehicle was left for a
period of:
48 hours within the right-of-way of any highway, road, street or other public thoroughfare or
72 hours on public or private property

form #46-4401 and email it to abvpublic@azdot.gov. These requests are processed by the Abandoned
Vehicle Unit ONLY.
The Abandoned Vehicle Unit will contact the customer for payment once the request is received.

## Fuel Types & Alternative Fuel Vehicles

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Alternative_Fuel_Vehicle.htm`

Fuel Types & Alternative Fuel Vehicles
Non-Alternative Fuel Types
Diesel
Electric Non-compliant*
Gasoline
Hybrid; includes all types of hybrid vehicles
None; used for trailers or vehicles without a motor
Alcohol
Hybrid is recognized as a valid fuel type in MAX and should not be changed from Hybrid to
Gas when processing.
*If a golf cart has a Fuel Type of 'Electric non-compliant' they will receive a Alternative Fuel
Motorcycle plate; however, they are not eligible for Alternative Fuel registration.
Alternative Fuel Types
Alternative fuels are defined as any fuel, other than gas or diesel, determined and certified by the
Environmental Protection Agency (EPA).
Natural gas
Hydrogen
Liquefied petroleum or a blend of hydrogen with liquefied petroleum or natural gas
A blend of 70% alternative fuel and 30% petroleum based fuel
Solar
Electric
Alcohol, vehicle must have been purchased prior to August 24, 1998
Any other fuels as determined by the EPA that is not defined in A.R.S. § 1-215
Alternative Fuel Vehicles

Arizona Law requires a vehicle to be powered exclusively by one of the fuels listed in the Alternative
Fuel Types section in order to receive an Alternative Fuel special plate and to be eligible for reduced
Vehicle License Tax or Tier 1 & Tier 2 vehicles.
When transferring ownership and a customer does not select a plate, the Alternative Fuel
plate will be the default plate for alternative fuel vehicles.
Eligible Vehicles
Required Documentation for Initial Transaction
Required Documentation for Subsequent Title Transfers
Emissions Requirements
Alternative Fuel Vehicle Fees
Title and Register in MAX
View the Title and Registration topic for more information on how to process an alternative fuel.
 Alternative Fuel Vehicle(AFV) Sticker
Replacing the AFV Sticker
Converting an Alternative Fuel Vehicle
Bi-Fuel Vehicles
Bi-fuel vehicles, which are vehicles that have the capability of using either an alternative or non-
alternative fuel independently, are no longer issued AFV registration.
A truck equipped with an alternative fuel (propane) tank that also has the ability to run on gas
is not eligible for an AFV registration.
Current owners of a bi-fuel vehicle with an Alternative Fuel special plate will be allowed to maintain their
plate and pay a reduced Vehicle License Tax until they sell or transfer the vehicle. They will be allowed
to transfer the Alternative Fuel special plate to a qualifying AFV (same owner and must provide proof )
but will not be allowed to transfer it to another bi-fuel vehicle.
Emissions Requirements for a Bi-Fuel
Energy Efficient Vehicles
Energy Efficient Plate (EEP) were given to qualifying plug-in hybrid electric vehicles. The program will
be discontinued as of March 2, 2020; however, there will still be existing vehicles with the EEP.
Owners of these vehicles may keep the EEP, but are only eligible for replacement (lost, stolen, or
destroyed) or requesting a disability symbol on the EEP. A duplicate (second) plate will not be allowed.
The EEP will need to be removed from the vehicle and issued a Passenger plate when the following
happens:
Another owner is added

The vehicle is sold, dismantled, salvaged, or crushed
Another qualifying plug-in hybrid electric vehicle is purchased; the plate is non-transferrable
Vehicles with the EEP are able to be driven in the High Occupancy Vehicle (HOV) lanes regardless of
the number of passengers in the vehicle.
If the vehicle is sold and the owner request Registration Credit, a $12 Registration Credit fee will still
apply.
If a plate is issued for any of the following, Postage and Handling Fees will apply.
Lost or Stolen EEP
When an EEP is lost or stolen, an owner may apply for a replacement plate with the Division.
View the Update Registration topic > Updating Registration service to process this request.
Disability EEP
The owner of a plug-in hybrid electric vehicle may apply for an Energy Efficient Disability plate (EEDP)
when qualified.
A Disability - Deaf/Hard of Hearing Plate/Placard Application, form #96-0104 is required when there is
not a Disability plate or placard on file for the owner.
View the Update Registration topic > Updating Registration service to process this request.
Standard EEP and wants a EEDP
A Special Plate Application, form #96-0143 is required and a Disability - Deaf/Hard of Hearing
Plate/Placard Application, form #96-0104 is required when there is not a Disability plate or placard on
file for the owner.
No fee will be charged to replace the standard EEP with a EEDP.
View the Update Registration topic > Updating Registration service to process this request.

## Bonded Title

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Bonded_Title.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Title Documents > Bonded Title
Bonded Title
Bond title procedures are required when:
An applicant cannot prove ownership of the vehicle or trailer OR
The Division is not satisfied that there are no unpaid lien(s), as a condition of issuing a title
An Arizona resident purchases an out-of-state repossessed vehicle. See Reasons to Complete a
Bond Title section for additional information
The Division will require the customer to file a surety bond at the time of final application.
The bond process protects prior or future owner(s) and/or lienholder(s). This procedure will require an
applicant to go through several steps before the title process can be completed.
The CSR will need to complete a Bond Title Application - Vehicle, form #40-1001 for the customer,
which helps the applicant to understand exactly what is required for a bond title.
View the Mobile Home Bonded Title topic for the differences on a mobile home bonded title process.

## Brand Management

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Brand_Management.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Brands > Brand Management
Brand Management
What is a Brand?
Brands are descriptive labels (applied by state motor vehicle agencies) regarding the status of a motor
vehicle, such as 'salvage', or 'restored salvage'.
The reason for branding a title helps identifies what has happened to the vehicle so the next purchaser
is aware prior to buying the vehicle, as this may influence the value of the vehicle.
NMVTIS keeps a history of brands that have been applied to the vehicle by states.
If a vehicle's title has been branded, it is an official designation made by the Division and should display
on the vehicle's title. While each state has its own list of brands, the Division uses the following brand
designations:
Dismantled
Flood Damage
Non-Repairable
Reconstructed
Refurbished
Restored Salvage
Retrofit
Salvage
State Assigned VIN
Stolen
In MAX, only one brand (most recent/current) will display on the Vehicle Information Page. To
see a full history of brands for the vehicle, see AAMVA Helpdesk - Vehicle for more
information.
If more than one owner and regardless of legal status, you can add or remove a brand
without additional owners, as it is not a normal title transfer of ownership. View the Signature
Requirements for T&R signature requirements based on legal status.

Vehicle Inspection Appointments must be made through AZ MVD Now.
Individuals and Organizations may use the Scheduling as a Guest option when scheduling
an appointment. Click here to view the steps.
Individuals and Organizations may also sign in to their account on AZ MVD Now to schedule
the appointment.
Click here to view the steps for Individuals
Click here to view the steps for Organizations
Dealers will have to use Dealer Suite account to schedule an appointment. Click here to view
the steps.
Vehicle Inspection Appointments for mobile homes, tow companies, or anyone who needs
on-site bulk inspections, email InspectionProgram@azdot.gov.
Dismantled
Flood Damage
Non-Repairable
Reconstructed
Refurbished
Restored Salvage
Retrofit
Salvage
State Assigned VIN
Stolen
Other Statuses of a Vehicle
Crushed and Junk vehicles are not considered branded but a status of a vehicle. However, once they
have been given this status in MAX, it will display in the Brand section on a Vehicle Information page
These statuses will not be added by a CSR in a field office, but by the Abandoned Vehicle Unit after all
the requirements are met.

## Cancel and Re-Establish Registration

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Cancel_ and_Re-establish_Registration.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Cancel and Re-Establish Registration
Cancel and Re-Establish Registration
DO NOT use this service until instructed to do so by a Supervisor or Level 2 MAX Support!
Scenario 1: You renew a registration and you realize you've renewed the wrong registration.
Scenario 2: You renew a registration for a customer and when completed they changed their mind about the
registration term (example: they wanted a 1 year and you processed for 2 years).
Scenario 3: You renew a registration for a customer without applying the requested exemption.
Scenario 4: You renew a registration for a customer and you realized you forgot to remove the Penalty when
they signed the penalty fee-waiver.
Scenario 5: You issue an Arizona Registration Only (ARO) and it was issued in error and/or the incorrect fees
were charged.
For these scenarios you can easily cancel that registration, and it will re-establish back to the original
registration and credit the fees to the account of the Customer (Agent) that paid them. You must follow this
process to ensure it's canceled and re-established correctly. Once completed you can register the vehicle
correctly.
If you complete any other services (such as Print Title, Issue Permit, Update Registration, etc.) after
the incorrect service, then there will be no fees to credit to the customer (agent) who paid for the
original service.
1. From the Services menu, select Cancel Registration.
2. Search for the Customer and Vehicle related to this service. In this case you will use the customer to
whom you mistakenly transferred the registration.
3. Select the registration you want to cancel.

4. Once the Customer and Vehicle are located and selected, click Add.
5. Select Renewed in Error - Re-Establish from the Cancel Registration Reason drop down (for Scenario
1 to 4). IMPORTANT: This option, when selected, is what will re-establish the prior registration and credit
back the fees to the customer's account.
OR
......Select ARO Entered in Error (for Scenario 5). This option is only available when processing on an ARO
record.

6. Click Confirm.
7. Review the information to ensure the accuracy of the information on how the registration will be re-
established.
8. If everything looks correct, click Continue.

9. The Taxes and Fees screen will display with the fees paid that will be credited back to the account of the
customer (agent) who paid it.
10. Click Pay Now after reviewing the fees to verify for accuracy.
If any other services (such as Update Registration, Print Title, Issue Permit, etc.) were completed
after the incorrect Registration service, then there will be no fees to credit to the customer (agent)
who paid for the original service.

11. The service is finalized. Download and provide receipt to the customer if desired.
12. To confirm the registration was reverted back you can go to the customer record and view the customer's
vehicles.
13. To confirm the fees have been credited to the customer account, click the Account icon (briefcase). Those
fees are available to apply toward the correct Renew Registration.

14. You can now do the Renew Registration again for the customer.
If you selected Replace or Issue New Plate during the incorrect Registration Renewal, a plate will be
queued up to print and be mailed to the customer. You will need to cancel that plate. Email the
Special Plates Unit at specialplates2@azdot.gov that the new plate issued should be canceled. To
find the plate number, go to your customer's documents and bring up the Temporary Plate document
that was generated in the original service.

## Cancel and Re-Establish Title

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Cancel_ and_Re-establish_Title.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Cancel and Re-Establish Title
Cancel and Re-Establish Title
DO NOT use this service until instructed to do so by a Supervisor or Level 2 MAX Support!
In a situation where you transferred a title to a customer and you determine you've titled it to the wrong customer, you
can easily cancel that title, and it will re-establish back to the original owner and credit the fees to the account of the
Agent that paid them. The reversal will also take the registration back to how the record was as well, if it was part of
the original service.
You must follow this process to ensure it's canceled and re-established correctly. Once this is done you are free to
transfer  the title into the correct customer's name.
If fees are collected for any additional services (e.g., Print Title, Issue Permit, Update Registration, etc.) after
the initial title service that is being corrected, an Alert will display in MAX:
1. From the Services menu Select Cancel Title.
2. Search for the Customer and Vehicle related to this service. In this case you will use the customer to whom you
mistakenly transferred the title.
3. Once the Customer and Vehicle are located and selected, click Add.

4. Select Entered in Error - Re-Establish from the Cancel Title Reason drop down. IMPORTANT: This option,
when selected, is what will re-establish the prior title and credit back the fees to the customer's account.
5. Click Confirm.
6. You will have the option to review who the title will be "re-established" to and from. Click Yes to review.

7. Review the information to ensure the accuracy of the information on how the title will be canceled and re-
established.
8. If everything looks correct, click Entered in Error - Re-Establish.

9. The title, along with the registration (if part of the original transaction) will be transferred back to the original
owner and any fees paid will be credited back to the account of the customer (agent) who paid it.
If any other services (such as Print Title, Issue Permit, etc.) were completed after the incorrect T&R service,
then there will be no fees to credit to the customer (agent) who paid for the original service.

NOTE: If a registration was part of the service that was done in error, you will need to cancel the printing of
the plate that was generated as part of that registration. Email the Special Plates Unit at
specialplates2@azdot.gov that the new plate issued should be canceled. To find the plate number, go to your
customer's documents and bring up the Temporary Plate document that was generated in the original
service.

## Commercial Vehicles

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Commercial_Vehicles.htm`

Commercial Vehicles
Cab and Chassis
A cab and chassis vehicle is made up of two parts:
The frame of the vehicle (Chassis) and
The covered compartment (in which the machinery is housed and the operator sits (Cab)
Typically, a cab and chassis vehicle is purchased with the intent of converting the vehicle by
permanently installing equipment, either at the time of purchasing or a later date.
Permanently installed equipment is defined as equipment which once installed, creates a definite
interior or exterior change to the vehicle and is not easily removable
Equipment that is permanently installed on a cab and chassis, whether at time of purchase or
later, is subject to having the Factory List Price (FLP) increased as well as the Vehicle License
Tax (VLT)
Establishing the Factory List Price for a Cab and Chassis
Inspections for Cab and Chassis
Renewal Registration of a Cab and Chassis
Title and Register in MAX
View the Title and Registration topic for more information on how to process a cab and chassis.
Commercial Vehicles
An owner of a vehicle that is requesting commercial registration for a vehicle or trailer or vehicle
combination (a motor vehicle with a semi-trailer being pulled) is required to pay certain additional
commercial fees plus all normal registration fees.
Vehicles that are required to have commercial registration are:
Used for commercial purposes (in the name of a company or individual)
Pickup trucks and vans that have a manufacturer’s gross vehicle weight rating (GVWR) of one-
ton or larger (even if the vehicle is being used for personal use)
Commercial Terms
Titling and Registering a Commercial Vehicle
Federal Heavy Vehicle Highway Use Tax (Form 2290)

Commercial Fees
Motor Carrier Commercial Fees
Farm Vehicles
A Farm vehicle is any vehicle being used on a farm for commercial farming or the raising of commercial
stock. A Farm vehicle must meet all of the following requirements:
Used for commercial farming or the commercial stock raising
Controlled and operated by the owner or owner’s family members or employees
Used to transport agricultural products, machinery or supplies to or from a commercial farming or
commercial stock raising
Cannot be used in the operations of for hire motor carrier (means a person engaged in and
compensated for the transportation of good or passengers)
Is not exempt from registration
“Commercial Farming" is the intensive cultivation of arable land by raising agricultural or
horticultural products as a principal source of the owner’s livelihood. "Commercial stock
raising" is breeding, raising and caring for domestic animals as a principal source of the
owner’s livelihood.
When the Farm vehicle is controlled and operated by the Farm vehicle’s owner(s), the owner’s family
member(s), or employee(s) within 150 miles of the farm, the driver is exempt from the Commercial
Driver License (CDL) requirement.
Farm Vehicle Registration
Seasonal Agricultural Vehicles
Seasonal Agricultural Registration (SAR) is issued to a commercial vehicle(s) that is used solely for
seasonal agricultural purposes. An agricultural vehicle is defined as a vehicle, trailer, or semi-trailer
used in the production or transportation of crops, livestock, machinery, or supplies in farming operations.
The owner of an agricultural vehicle must request the SAR.
SAR allows the owner to pay prorated commercial fees for a specific period of time; not less than
90 days and not more than a full year (3 to 11 months)
Must declare the weight each time the vehicle is registered
Rules of a SAR
SAR and Vehicle Registration
Until we are fully paperless, when you finish the T & R service for the customer, you will print
out the Title Summary (artifact), have the customer review and sign, and re-scan into MAX.
This will replace the process where you print the T&R Application for the customer to sign.

Non-Government Emergency Vehicle Registration
An emergency vehicle that is soley used for firefighting or rescue service qualifies for a reduced VLT
and is exempt from registration.
The following vehicles are eligible and will receive a Commercial Vehicle plate:
An ambulance
A firefighting vehicle
A hearse or any other vehicle that is used by a mortician in the conduct of the mortician's
business
A motor vehicle or vehicle combination that is used for the transportation of passengers
A trailer or semitrailer with a declared gross weight of 10,000 lbs. or more
A vehicle that is used for the transportation of department personnel (e.g., Fire Chief) for non-
emergency purposes is not eligible for the tax exemption.
View the policy 8.10.5 Non-Government Emergency Vehicle Registration for a list of non-eligible
vehicles.
The owner of an emergency vehicle is liable for the full registration fee and a prorated VLT and
commercial registration fee for each month the vehicle is used for purposes other than emergency
services.
The VLT will be prorated and is calculated on a basis of one-tenth (1/10) of the annual VLT due
for each month the vehicle is so operated
View the Fee Exemptions topic > Vehicle Qualifier section for more information on fee exemptions for a
non-government emergency vehicle.
When processing a Title and Registration service, in Registration section, select Non-
Government Emergency Service from the Use Type drop-down menu.
Title and Register in MAX
View the Title and Registration topic for more information on how to process any of the above
mentioned vehicle types.
Remember to issue a Commercial plate, as well as choosing the correct Use Type in the
Registration section.

## Credit Estimate

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Credit_Estimate.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > TnR Fees & Credit > Credit Estimate
Credit Estimate
This was formerly referred to as a Plate Credit.
The Credit Estimate service is a simple inquiry regarding how much credit may be available.
The Request Registration Credit service processes the refund of paid fees and taxes to the
customer.
Credit Estimate allows the CSR to determine if the customer vehicle is eligible for a Registration Credit,
and will display the potential credit/refund the customer may receive when a registration is canceled or
a registered vehicle is sold.
You will not be able to run this Credit Estimate for a vehicle that is not currently registered to the
customer.
This is an estimate for the customer. The customer must specifically request a Registration
Credit on AZ MVD Now, or with a CSR in an MVD Office. If the customer does not request the
Registration Credit, the credit balance will decrease monthly until there is a zero balance.

## Emissions

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Emissions.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Vehicle Management > Emissions
Emissions
An emission is a smog test preformed on a vehicle to assure the vehicle meets Environmental
Protection Agency (EPA) standards enforced by the Arizona Department of Environmental Quality
(ADEQ). The EPA agency sets the standards for air quality in the United States. The State of Arizona is
required to meet these standards.
The EPA has identified cities, towns, and rural areas which meet the air quality standards and those
cities, towns, and rural areas which do not meet the air quality standards. These areas are identified as
follows:
Attainment Areas: Any city, town, or rural area that meets air quality standards. Vehicles
permanently located in attainment areas are NOT required to have emissions testing
Non-Attainment Areas: Any city, town, or rural area that does NOT meet the air quality
standards (smoggy areas). There are two non-attainment areas that do not meet the standards
for air quality and each have different testing standards
Metro Phoenix Area A: Maricopa, Yavapai, and Pinal counties
Metro Tucson Area B: Pima County
All other counties comply with EPA standards and therefore do not require emissions testing, unless the
vehicle is used to commute to work within the Metro Phoenix or Metro Tucson areas.
The requirement for an emissions testing is based on the zip code of the owner’s residential
address or the vehicle’s domicile address. Customers are not required to provide
documentation authenticating the vehicle address.

## Export Only

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Export_Only.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Export Only
Export Only
Foreign country motor vehicle dealers typically buy their vehicles from wholesale motor vehicle auction
dealers. Wholesale motor vehicle auction dealers do not buy, sell, own, or have any interest in the motor
vehicles that are being auctioned. They provide auction services solely in wholesale transactions to
motor vehicle dealers licensed by this state or any other jurisdictions.
An Arizona ‘Export Only’ title means that the vehicle is purchased by a foreign motor vehicle dealer
because most of the time vehicles are considered inoperable, cannot pass emissions and are
considered non-sellable in the US market. The vehicle may or should then be exported to a foreign
country for resale. The foreign country motor vehicle dealer cannot sell a vehicle(s) in Arizona unless
they are licensed by the State of Arizona to do so.
When the wholesale motor vehicle auction dealer sells a vehicle to the foreign country motor vehicle
dealer, the auction will stamp the title ‘Export Only’ and ensure the seller and buyer complete the
reassignment portion on the back of the title with the foreign country motor vehicle dealer listed as the
buyer. And if the wholesale motor vehicle auction dealer is an ATP, they will place a Sold Notice on the
Vehicle Information Page.
View the Sold Notice topic > Adding a Sold Notice for an Export Vehicle section for more
information.
Vehicles returning to the US with titles stamped ‘Export Only’ will be required to have a Level II/III
inspection to determine if the vehicle is roadworthy.

## Factory List Price

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Factory_List_Price.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Vehicle Management > Factory List Price
Factory List Price
The Factory List Price (FLP) is used to help calculate the Vehicle License Tax (VLT).
The FLP can also be referenced as the MBRP, which stands for the Manufacturer Base Retail Price.
The MBRP is the price a vehicle costs without any add-ons requested by the buyer.
Obtaining a Vehicle FLP for a Non-Commercial Vehicle or Trailer
The FLP can be established using one of the following:
NADAGuides.com
Factory Invoice
National Automobile Dealer’s Association Vehicle Appraisal Guide (NADA)
Manufacturer’s invoice
Manufacturer issued dealer’s invoice
Conversion invoice(s) and receipt(s)
Factory List Price Guide Book Trucks/Trailers
Truck Blue Book
Vehicle Manufacturer’s website
The T&R Application (Single) submitted, from the dealer, with all other documentation
Contacting a local dealership
When using this method, document the below in the “Other Information/Mobile Home
Section” of the T&R application or Arizona Title Summary page:
The name of the dealership you contacted
The phone number you called
The name of the representative you spoke with
The date you called
The FLP the representative gave you
If the MBRP cannot be determined, the Manufacturer’s Suggested Retail Price (MSRP) can be used as
the FLP.
For MCOs ONLY:

When the above ways to determine the FLP does not work, your absolute last resort will be to use the
purchase price on the sales invoices provided by the customer. You will need to make a copy of the
sales invoice to show how the FLP was determined.
See an  upline approver if the FLP cannot be obtained by any of the mentioned methods.
Establishing the FLP for a Commercial Vehicle (18,000+ lbs GVWR)
Manufacturer Certificate of Origin (MCO)
When a customer wants to title and register a commercial motor vehicle that has a gross vehicle weight
rating (GVWR) over 18,000 pounds with a MCO, the sales price will be used to determine the FLP.
The sales price is the purchase price of the vehicle itself, not including sales tax, document fees,
the price of a warranty, or add-ons requested by the buyer
The dealer sales invoice or contract will be required. If a sales invoice or contract is not available, use
the MBRP.
Used Vehicle (Out-of-State or Arizona Title)
When a customer wants to title and register a commercial motor vehicle that has a gross vehicle weight
rating (GVWR) over 18,000 pounds with an out-of-state or Arizona title, the following can be used to
determine the FLP:
Manufacturer base retail price
Price Digest (web-based resource)
Contacting a dealership or manufacturer may be done when a price may not be obtained from
one of the above
Cab and Chassis
The FLP for a cab and chassis will be obtained by adding the FLP of the cab and chassis vehicle to the
FLP of the new equipment. View the Commercial Vehicles topic > Cab and Chassis section for more
information.
Scanning Documentation
Copies of the documentation used to establish the FLP are considered to be supporting documents and
must be scanned into MAX during the service.
Policies
4.2.3 Imaging Preparation Scanning
8.15.5 Establishing a Factory List Price for Commercial Vehicles (Over 18,000 lbs GVWR)

To provide a customer an estimate of registration fees when you are outside of a registration service,

The Fee Estimation service will only provide a fee estimation for one year.

## Fee Exemptions

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Fee_Exemptions.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > TnR Fees & Credit > Fee Exemptions
Fee Exemptions
An exemption releases qualified owner(s) of a vehicle from paying Vehicle License Tax (VLT),
Registration fees, and commercial vehicle fees.
The owner may be granted a full or partial exemption, depending on the type of exemption and
the number of titled owners who qualify for the exemption
When the vehicle is in the name of a lessor, the lessor is not included in determining the number
of owners
If the lessee qualifies for a VLT exemption, the exemption characteristic should only go on
the lessee's record not the lessor's
When a vehicle is in the name of the trust, no exemption applies
If a trustee, or another qualified owner is on the title, an exemption may be applied
All reduced VLT rates must be applied at the time of initial registration or registration renewal.
Refunds will not be given unless it has been determined the VLT has been overpaid due to
error. View Refund Eligibility Scenarios to determine if the customer qualifies for a refund due
to an exemption.
Re-certification proof is not required when a lifetime exemption characteristic is already on the
customer record.
There are different kinds of exemptions for which a customer can qualify. Each exemption requires the
applicant to have specific documentation as proof of eligibility.
Approval from the upline approver is not required. A VLT Exemptions Report will be reviewed to confirm
the correct documents were received.
Characteristics on the Customer Record help to trigger Fee Exemptions within a Registration
service, but not all Exemptions require a Characteristic.
View the Characteristics topic for more information on Characteristics, or how to add or edit
them, if needed.

Types of Exemptions
There are four categories of exemptions:
1. Military/ Public Health
2. Non-Profit / Religious / School
3. Owner Qualifier
4. Vehicle Qualifier
Military/ Public Health
Non-Profit / Religious / School
Owner Qualifier
Vehicle Qualifier
Characteristics and Exemptions in MAX
Adding a Fee Exemption During a Service
All required supporting documents that are presented for a reduced or exempted VLT rate
must be scanned and uploaded in the associated service in MAX.

## Fleet Management

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Fleet_Management.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Vehicle Management > Fleet
Management
Fleet Management
What is a Fleet?
Fleet Registration is a convenient way for owners of many vehicles to maintain the vehicle registrations
on those vehicles. As an alternative to maintaining individual registrations that expire at different times
of the year, anyone owning two or more vehicles is eligible for a permanent fleet registration account.
The registration for all vehicles in the permanent fleet account will have the same expiration date.
Payment for permanent fleet registration fees are required annually.
Vehicles in a fleet are each issued a 'PERM FLT' tab and a one time registration that shows 'FLEET' as
the expiration date since the registration itself is not permanent . There is no need to locate a specific
vehicle to place the corresponding tab on the plate.
Fleet Details
There is no limit to the number of vehicles in a Fleet
A fleet must contain at least 2 vehicles
A customer can have a maximum of 12 fleets (no more than one fleet expiring each month)
A fleet can be renewed for only 1 year renewals (no 2 or 5 year renewals)
Fleet owners must have an AZ MVD Now login (unless they are renewing with an Authorized
Third Party)
A Fleet's renewal fees MUST be paid via a customer's account (no cash, check or credit card
payments UNLESS they are being renewed with an Authorized Third Party)
Vehicles are registered annually (every year)
A Fleet can be for either an individual or organization customer (must be level 3 or above)
The vehicle registration is a FLEET status and is issued a PERM FLT tab.
The fleet registration account must consist of two or more vehicles owned by the same individual
or organization in Arizona
The vehicle license tax (VLT), weight fee (WGT), and motor carrier fee (CRF) are prorated if
calculating a partial registration year
A vehicle is assessed a one-time $2 fee for each vehicle added or converted to a perm fleet
account
A vehicle cannot be added until it is within 3 months of the current expiration date of the vehicle
(when not in a fleet already)

A vehicle cannot be added to a fleet within 2 months of the fleet expiration
Registration expiration on a fleet will always be on the last day of the month
Managing Fleets in MAX
Creating a Fleet
Adding Vehicles to a Fleet
Removing Vehicle from Fleet
Suspending Fleet
Canceling Fleet
Renewing Fleet Registration
Bulk Registration Credit Requests
Authorized Third Parties and Fleets

## Government Registration Only (GRO)

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Government_Registration_Only.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Government Registration Only (GRO)
Government Registration Only (GRO)
The Division is authorized to issue a Government Registration Only (GRO), without a Certificate of Title, to a
vehicle that is being reassigned between federal and/or state government agencies.
A GRO can also be assigned to a government vehicle from another state doing business in Arizona. For
example, if a California government vehicle is doing business in Arizona, an Arizona GRO can be requested.
The vehicle must meet all applicable Arizona registration requirements
The vehicle must be registered in the name of a federal or state agency or a political subdivision
GRO Documentation
Issuing a GRO
Follow the steps listed in the topic Arizona Registration Only (ARO)/ Special Interstate Registration (SPI).
On the Registration tab, select Government from Use dropdown menu.

Until we are fully paperless, when you finish the T & R service for the customer, you will print out the
Title Summary (artifact), have the customer review and sign, and re-scan into MAX. This will replace
the process where you print the T&R Application for the customer to sign.

## Grey Market Vehicles

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Grey_Market_Vehicles.htm`

Grey Market Vehicles
A Grey Market vehicle is a vehicle that was not manufactured for the United States market and does not
conform to Federal Motor Vehicle Safety Standards (FMVSS) and Environmental Protection Agency
(EPA) requirements.
Indicators a Vehicle is a Grey Market Vehicle
The following are indicators of a possible Grey Market vehicle but do not necessarily mean the vehicle
is a Grey Market vehicle:
There is no manufacturer certification label or Registered Importer (RI) label of conformity
indicating the vehicle meets FMVSS
The VIN has less than 17 characters or the VIN does not conform to U.S. DOT identification
requirements
The 17 character VIN has been in use since September 1980 and is required for all model
year 1981 and newer vehicles
The EPA sticker is missing or the EPA sticker was manufactured and affixed by a licensed
converter
A foreign country title or registration is submitted as proof of ownership
The glass and lens covers (e.g., head lights, tail lights, windshields) are not approved by the U.S.
DOT
View the policy 8.13.1 Grey Market (Import) Vehicle for more information on requirements and
documentation for Grey Market vehicles.
Grey Market or Not Grey Market, That is THE Question!
Still confused if the vehicle is or isn't a Grey Market vehicle?
First, you need to determine if it is indeed a Grey Market vehicle or not before you can proceed in
requesting the proper documentation from your customer.

No, I'm
NOT a
Grey
Market
vehicle
Yes, I AM a
Grey Market
vehicle
Built to
conform
with U.S.
FMVSS
and EPA
standards
Not built or
modified to
conform
with U.S.
FMVSS
and EPA
standards
Does not
matter
where the
vehicle
was built
or where
it is
coming
from
Does not
matter
where the
vehicle was
built or
where it is
coming from
What else can help me determine if it a Grey Market Vehicle?
Another (semi) quick way of determining if a vehicle is a Grey Market vehicle is a Level I Inspection to
determine if the manufacturer certification label is there and that it states the vehicle conforms to U.S.
standards. Yes, a Level II or III Inspection is required, but a Level I Inspection may help determine if the
vehicle is truly a Grey Market vehicle and if those procedures or documents are required.
If the conforming label is there, continue as a normal vehicle (even if there is a foreign ownership
document)
View the T and R Scenarios topic > Out-of-Country Vehicles section for more information on
how to process
If the conforming label is NOT there, proceed as a possible Grey Market Vehicle and refer to
Level II or III Inspection
Documentation for Grey Market Vehicles
Ownership documentation
All documents must be written in English
Non-English documents must be accompanied by an original English translation performed
by a qualified translator, view policy 8.2.1 General Title InformationSection N for more
information on qualified translators
Level I Inspection for:
Vehicles 25 years and older
Foreign military (NATO) personnel with vehicles approved for temporary use in the U.S.
Level II or III Inspection for all other possible Grey Market vehicles

Manufacturer documentation verifying the vehicle complies with FMVSS and EPA standards (only
applies to vehicles manufactured for Canada
View the Vehicles Manufactured for Canada section
View the Level I Basic ABV Inspection Training Manual or Reference Manual, or the Vehicle Inspections
topic for more information on Level I Inspections.
Don't I need import documentation?
Import documentation is not required. The documentation, though, can help support their overall claim
of ownership and build a foundation for proper documentation to get a title in Arizona.
If import documentation is provided, along with an ownership documentation, it is best practice to view it
and compare it to the ownership documentation.
There are some instances where import documents are indeed required, such as NATO
personnel or export only vehicles.
Vehicles Manufactured for Canada
The Division may issue a title to a vehicle manufactured according to Canadian specification if it meets
all the following specifications:
Is not for resale
Has a GVWR of less than 10,000 lbs.
Is a passenger vehicle, motorcycle, or SUV
Manufacturer documentation verifying the vehicle complies with FMVSS and EPA standards
FMVSS (48 CFR 571) and EPA (40 CFR 86) labeling location requirements are waived
If manufacturer documentation indicates that a vehicle’s speedometer or headlights do not
comply with FMVSS and EPA standards, documentation verifying completion of a
modification that brings the vehicle into compliance is required
North Atlantic Treaty Organization Agreement (NATO)
Foreign military (NATO) personnel may import their vehicles from out-of-country and the Division is
authorized to provide Arizona Registration Only (ARO) for the vehicle, no title will be issued. View the
Arizona Registration Only (ARO)/ Special Interstate Registration (SPI) for steps on processing the ARO.
Required Documentation
Import documents
Box 12 needs to be marked to indicate vehicle will not be sold unless to another qualifying
owner (typically NATO to NATO personnel)
Level I Inspection
Title and/or registration from out-of-country

If vehicle is sold to another qualifying owner (typically NATO to NATO personnel),
additional, properly completed bills of sale may be submitted
Military orders
If vehicle is sold, military orders will need be submitted for current owners and for the
current buyer to verify the transfer is authorized per Import documentation, Box 12
Proper VLT exemption documentation, if applicable
Import Document, Box 12
The import document (Importation of Motor Vehicles and Motor Vehicle Equipment Subject to Federal
Motor Vehicle Safety, Bumper and Theft Prevention Standard, form OMB No. 2127-0002) will require
Box 12 to be marked to indicate they are foreign military members and that they will be complying with
the rules listed in Box 12.
Example of Import Document, Box 12

## License Plate Overview

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/License_Plate_Overview.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Plates > License Plate Overview
License Plate Overview
The Division is authorized to issue license plates to a vehicle. License plates are coated with a reflective
material and are designed so the alphanumeric characters and the license plates are sufficient size to
be plainly readable, during daylight hours, from a distance of 100 feet.
License plates are assigned during a service at a Customer Service offices and ATPs, while some
plates may be ordered through AZ MVD Now.
The license plate is assigned to the vehicle owner, and is attached to the vehicle they own. When the
vehicle is sold, the owner retains the license plate, which can be retained as a souvenir or surrendered
to the Division. Since the plate is retained by the owner, it may be transferred to another vehicle owned
by the customer.
When a plate is eligible to be transferred from vehicle to vehicle, a Plate Notice, form #48-3302 is
required. MAX may display a message prompting the form to be completed and submitted; however, the
CSR will need to know when the form is needed. View the Personalized and Special Plates topic >
Transferring a Personalized Plate section for more information on form and transferring plate
requirements.
MAX refers to this form as a Plate Relinquish form
The plate is displayed on the vehicle along with a registration tab.
One plate is issued
If the customer wants a second plate, they may ask for a duplicate plate for $10.00. View
the Duplicate Plate topic for more information and how to process
The plate with tab is displayed on the rear of the vehicle
The tab will indicate the month and year of registration expiration, along with whether the vehicle
is registered in the attainment or non-attainment area
Plates, registrations, and tabs are mailed out to the customer. If the temporary plate or registration is
lost while waiting for the permanent plate or registration:
A customer may print a duplicate temporary plate from their Customer Account through AZ MVD
Now at no charge
If a customer comes into the office to request a duplicate temporary plate because theirs has
been lost, stolen, or destroyed, a CSR will re-print from the customer's documents and will pay
$5.00

The Miscellaneous Purchase service will be used to collect Replacement Plate Fee
payment
A plate is issued according to the vehicle's:
Body style
Usage
Owner
There are four different categories of plates for use on a vehicle:
Regular
Vehicle Qualifier
Owner Qualifier (Special Plates)
Non-Qualifier (Special Plates)

MAX generates and assigns license plate numbers. If a temporary plate generates that looks
blatantly derogatory, profane, or inappropriate, or if the customer points out that the plate
seems inappropriate (Offensive Standard Plate), the CSR would need to:
Complete anUpdate Registration service
SelectIssue New Plate for a new plate to be generated. Fees will need to be zeroed out
during the service
Have your upline approver complete the following to request for refund of incorrect plate
if the initial service was completed the same day:
Submit a Service Portal ticket
SelectSpecial Plates from the Area Impacted drop down
In the Detailed description field, begin the description by typing the appropriate
cancellation reason, and then typeany additional information needed
Cancellation Reasons:
Address Error
Burner Plate
Offensive Standard Plate
Ordered wrong background/Plate Type Error
Personalization Error
Spacing Error
*If the service is completed after 4pm on the same day as the initial service, the customer
must be notified that they MAY receive both plates AND an upline approver should still submit
the Service Portal ticket to the Special Plates Unit (SPU).
**ATPs Only:This process can only be done Monday thru Friday (not on weekends), as you
must submit the form the SAME day as processing the plate.
If a customer reports a plate they believe to be inappropriate, and they are not the owner of the
plate, please have the customer complete the License Plate Complaint, form #96-0577.
If there is a reason to cancel a plate request (e.g., personalization error, plate type error, wrong
address, etc.), view the Personalized and Special Plates topic > Plate Ordering Issues section.
When the Special Plates Unit - Plate Support Request form is submitted for an Offensive
Standard Plate, this will inform the SPU to place the plate number on the 'no-no' list.
View the Personalized and Special Platestopic > License Plate Overview section for more
information.
Regular Issued Plates
These plates do not require additional fees for the plate itself and are issued to anyone.
Commercial

Motorcycle
Standard/Passenger
Trailer
Commercial License Plate
These plates are issued:
Upon request
To a vehicle being used commercially
To a vehicle with a declared gross weight of one ton or more
To a vehicle with a gross combined weight rating of 26,001+
To a towed unit with a weight rating that exceeds 10,000 lbs.
Commercially registered trailers will receive a Trailer license plate.
Motorcycle License Plate
These plates are issued to:
A motorcycle
A golf cart, depending on Fuel Type
A motor scooter (49 cc or above)
A motor driven cycle (49 cc or above)
An All-Terrain Vehicle (ATV) that is certified as road-worthy
View the Recreational Vehicles topic for more information on golf carts and ATVs.
Standard License Plate
These plates are issued to non-commercial passenger (owner operator) vehicle:
Automobiles
Motor homes
Trucks (3/4 ton or less)
Vans/Van Conversions (3/4 ton or less)
Standard plates may also be referenced as shelf or passenger plate.
Maroon Plates
From 1980-1997, the Division issued plates with maroon background and white lettering. These are
considered a standard plate (not an antique plate.) These plates are not transferable to another party,
only to the same owner (if available in MAX).
Example of Maroon Plate

Trailer License Plate
View the Trailer Identification topic for more information on Trailer license plates.
Vehicle Qualifier Plates
Depending on vehicle certain plates may be issued, such as Historical Vehicle, Alternative Fuel, or Farm
Vehicle license plates.
View the following policies for more information on Vehicle Qualifier Plates:
8.16.3 Alternative Fuel License Plate
8.16.7 Distinctive Vehicle License Plates
8.16.16 Vehicles of Historic Value License Plates
Owner Qualifier (Special Plates and Government Plates)
The owner must have the qualifying characteristic on their record to qualify for these types of plates,
view the Characteristics topic for more information.
View the Personalized and Special Plates topic for more specific information on qualifications for owner
qualifier special plates or policies 8.16.6 Disability-Deaf/Hard of Hearing License Plate/Placard, 8.16.11
Owner Qualifier Special Plates, and/or 8.16.14 Special Organization License Plates for a specific list of
plates.
View the T&R Scenarios topic >Adding a Government Exemption to a Vehicle that is Owned by a
Government Agency section for instructions on how to title and register a Government Vehicle or for
Government Plate information view policy 8.16.7 Distinctive Vehicle License Plates.
Non-Qualifer (Special Plates)
For these types of plates, anyone may request them without any qualifications.
View the Personalized and Special Plates topic for more specific information on special plates or policy
8.16.10 Non-Qualifier Special Plates for a specific list of plates.
Digital License Plates

Digital license plates are available for registered vehicles in Arizona, both standard and motorcycle
formats. Digital plates may only be displayed on an ADOT-approved digital license plate display
device. The digital license plate display devices are not issued or maintained by the Division.
Currently the only approved vendor is Reviver. The manufacturer determines the plate’s cost and MVD
does not receive any proceeds from sales.
If a customer wants a digital plate you will refer them to Reviver.com. MVD will continue to manage the
issuance of the license plate number and specialty background, if desired. The customer will contact the
vendor to have the MVD assigned plate number and background added to the digital format.
Currently there is no way in MAX to see if a customer has a digital plate.
For more information you can visit the Digital Plate section on AZDOT.gov or Reviver.com or view the
policy 2.7.1 Digital License Plate Display Device for more information on vendors and the display
devices.

## Add a Lien

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Liens/Add_a_Lien.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Liens > Add a Lien
Add a Lien
A lien is the legal right to hold another's property to satisfy an obligation or debt. In the case of a motor
vehicle, when a purchaser borrows money to pay for the vehicle, the person the money was borrowed
from becomes a lien holder and attaches a lien on the vehicle until the money is repaid. Jurisdictions
are either title holding or non-title holding.
The lien information will remain on the Arizona vehicle title record until proof is provided to the Division
the lien has been satisfied or canceled. View the Lien Release topic for more information on lien
releases.

## Electronic Lien and Title (ELT)

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Liens/Electronic_Lien_and_Title_(ELT).htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Liens > Electronic Lien and Title (ELT)
Electronic Lien and Title (ELT)
Electronic lien and title, also known as ELT, is a program offered by various U.S. states allowing for the
electronic exchange of lien and title information with lienholders in lieu of a paper certificate of title. The
ELT program is intended to improve data accuracy, data security, and faster lien releases and results in
lower paper, printing, and mailing costs.
Arizona requires lenders to participate in the ELT program. Therefore all organization lienholders must
have an ELT number used to identify the lender. The lienholder must select their own and register with
an Authorized ELT Service Provider. Once registered, any liens from the lienholder will be filed and
released electronically. View the Set up ELT Provider in MAX section for more information on where to
direct the customer and/or lienholder.
Trusts, Estates, and Government entities are not required to be set up as an ELT.
Mobile home lienholders are not required to be ELT.
Upon approval, any changes to the address must be completed by the AADA ELT Help Desk.
Missing ELT Number on Application
Contact Information
ELT States
To verify what which state is part of the ELT (title holding) program:
View the World Data Corp (Polk Book) Quick Reference Guide
Find the column 'Title Held by Lienholder'
YES = Title holding state
NO = Not a title holding state
Out-of-State Titles
When a customer presents an out-of-state title with an out-of-state lienholder and the lienholder is not
registered with an ELT Service Provider, and the lienholder is:
A large lender who conducts business in Arizona, a T&R permit may be issued and an
Additional Requirements form provided, indicating the lender must contact an ELT Service

Provider to obtain an electronic lienholder customer number
A small out-of-state lender who does not do business in Arizona, an Arizona Registration
Only (ARO) can be processed. The customer will retain the out-of-state title in their possession
until the lien has been satisfied and the title can be transferred to Arizona

## Lien Release

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Liens/Lien_Release.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Liens > Lien Release
Lien Release
When a car loan is paid off, the lender (lienholder) no longer has a legal interest in the vehicle and will
relinquish that interest by issuing a lien release. The lien release process can vary by state.
Arizona Titles and Liens
The first lien is the primary lien and is the only lien recognized when performing a repossession title
service. However, lien releases may be accepted in any order.
A second (or third) lien's release may allow the removal of the second (or third) lien, while the
primary lien remains on the record.
Electronic Lien Release
Paper Lien Release
Signature Requirements for Removing a Lien
Lien Release Exceptions
When a Lien Clearance is Not Required
 Lien-Free Title of Record
Out-of-State Titles and Liens
To remove a lien from an out-of-state title, Arizona will follow that state's process and requirements. A
title issued from out-of-state may be used to remove an Arizona titled lien if all the following conditions
are met:
Arizona had the previous title
The lien (previously record in Arizona) does not appear on the foreign jurisdiction title
The out-of-state title requirements for a lien to be released will be in conjunction with that state's
requirements
The out-of-state title was issued (at least) one year prior to the date of application

The customer originated a title in Arizona with a lienholder and moved to another state with the
title.
The customer returned to Arizona (less than a year) with no lienholder showing on the title.
The customer must show some type of lien satisfaction.
Release a Lien During Title Only or Title and Registration Service
Remove a Lien using Manage Liens and Brands Service

## Operation of Law Liens

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Liens/Operations_of_Law_Liens.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Liens > Operation of Law Lien
Operation of Law Liens
Operations of Law Liens are legal injunctions prohibiting the transfer of ownership placed on a vehicle
by the Division on behalf of a governing agency (DES, DOR, Courts of Law, etc.), which is different from
liens placed on a vehicle by a lender which are typically added to a vehicle at the time of purchase, and
which are intended to be automatically released when the loan is repaid.
These liens affects titles being transferred from the owner with the Operation of Law Lien,
meaning OUT of their name.
Titles can be transferred to the customer with an Operation of Law Lien, meaning INTO their
name, but once this is done, the vehicle will be non-transferable under the Operation of Law
Lien.
Operations of Law Liens can be added at any time, for no filing fee, and can remain in place indefinitely.
In some cases the customer can petition to remove these Operations of Law Liens either by permission
of the governing agency, or by satisfying legal requirements (pays court fees / fines, catches up on child
support, etc.).
Questions pertaining to the lien shall be referred to the filing agency. The CSR may provide the
customer with the lien information found in the Withdrawal section on the Vehicle Information Page. The
customer is responsible for contacting the filing agency.

Contacting the Ops Lien Unit (field & ATP offices): If you have a customer at your window and
the customer has an Operation of Law Lien issue that you are not able to resolve, have an
upline approver email the Ops Lien Unit at opsliens@azdot.gov.
This email should only be used by an Lead, Supervisor or Manager.
Subject: Customer Waiting - Type of Lien
Body of email:
Customer Name & DL Number
VIN
Document provided by customer
Uploaded to customer documents (yes or no)
Brief description of the customer's issue
A response to your email should be received within 15 minutes. If you do not receive a
response within 15 minutes or you receive an out-of-office response, please gather the
customer’s contact information to reach out once you hear back from the Ops Lien Unit.
**ATPs Only: Same-day responses will only occur Monday thru Friday. Any emails sent on
weekends or holidays will be responded to next business day.

Until we are fully paperless, when you finish the T & R service for the customer, you will print
out the Title Summary (artifact), have the customer review and sign, and re-scan into MAX.
This will replace the process where you print the T&R Application for the customer to sign.

## Manage Liens and Brands

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Manage_Liens_and_Brands.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Brands > Manage Liens and Brands
Manage Liens and Brands
Manage Liens and Brands should be used for the following situations:
Customer is retaining ownership of a vehicle, but they need to add a lien or release a lien.
Generally you should only release a lien when lienholder is an individual.
If the lienholder is an organization (ELT), a CSR should only release if the customer has the
lien release documentation and they can demonstrate they have taken every reasonable
action to get the lienholder to release the lien electronically, per policy 8.4.2 Lien Clearance
Requirements > Lien Clearance Exceptions section.
To add a brand outside of a title service (salvage, flood damaged, dismantled), so a new (e.g.,
restored salvage/reconstructed) can be recorded.
When some brands are added (e.g., Salvage) and the vehicle has a lien, the lien must be
released prior to adding the brand. The Manage Liens & Brands service will NOT stop when
a lien is on there, YOU must be aware of proper procedure.
To add a one-time mailing address (if the customer has released a lien or updated a brand and
wants the title sent to a one time mailing address).
Any changes to Brands will produce a new title number and notify NMVTIS electronically of the
changes.
When using the service and a lien exists, certain brands cannot be added. If a certain brand exists, a
lien cannot be placed on the record.

## NMVTIS Responses

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/NMVTIS_Reponses.htm`

NMVTIS Responses
The National Motor Vehicle Title Information System (NMVTIS) is an online, interactive data exchange
program that provides participating states with specific motor vehicle and title record information
intended to reduce fraud and deter the titling of stolen vehicles.
NMVTIS deals with:
Possible stolen vehicles; which includes the Possible Stolen Vehicle Report
Out-of-state brands transferring to Arizona titles
Verification of data entry of out-of-state title numbers during transfer to Arizona; which includes
verifying if the out-of-state title number is the most current title number
NMVTIS Multiple Vehicles Returned
NMVTIS Returned Non-Matching Title
Possible Stolen Vehicles
The NMVTIS Help Desk tracks and monitors every possible stolen vehicle hit. Therefore, every time a
Possible Stolen Vehicle Report message populates, the report must be completed and not overlooked.
This 'paper trail' is vital for the NMVTIS Help Desk to gather the statistical information for successful
resolution.
Possible Stolen Vehicle Report Message
Possible Stolen Vehicle Report Scenarios and Examples

## Non-Resident Daily Commuter

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/NonResident_Daily_Commuter.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Non-Resident Daily Commuter
Non-Resident Daily Commuter
A nonresident daily commuter is a person who is a resident of a contiguous state, who enters and
leaves this state on a daily basis for the purpose of employment and whose motor vehicle is principally
garaged in the contiguous state.
The vehicle’s owner’s place of employment cannot extend more than 35 air miles into Arizona from the
border at any point.
View the 35 Mile Corridor Zone chart for a list of cities and towns.
A Nonresident Daily Commuter Application, form #40-7807 can be completed once all conditions are
met. View the policy 7.1.11 Nonresident Daily Commuter for those conditions.
Customers must have a Customer Account in MAX in order to process the payment. View the
Customer Account Requirements (Level 3 Customer) topic for more information.
The application:
Is valid for two years from the last day of the month of issuance
Costs $8.00
Once the requirements are met, the CSR will:
Hole punch the decal with the appropriate month and year
Affix the decal to the application in the appropriate space
Complete a Miscellaneous Purchase in MAX to collect the Non-Resident Daily Commuter
Registration fee
Documentation is not required to be scanned and uploaded into MAX.

## Off-Highway Vehicle (OHV) Decal

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Off-Highway_Vehicle_Decal_(OHV).htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Off-Highway Vehicles > Off-Highway
Vehicle (OHV) Decal
Off-Highway Vehicle (OHV) Decal
An OHV decal is required for lawful Arizona off-highway operation on public lands of any all-terrain
vehicle (ATV) or off-highway vehicle (OHV) that meets the following criteria:
Is designed by the manufacturer primarily for travel over unimproved terrain
Has an unladen weight of 2,500 lbs. or less
Declared usage of 'Exclusively Off-Highway', 'Primarily Off-Highway' or 'Primarily On-Highway' on
the Off-Highway Vehicle Decal Application, form #96-0572, or verbally if renewing
See the OHV Registration Chart and the Unique Vehicle Guide for more information.
A driver license is required to drive on any maintained road.
Questions pertaining to OHV rules and regulations should be directed to Arizona Game and
Fish
or to local law enforcement.
Exclusively Off-Highway - No Maintained Road Use
The vehicle will be used off-road at all times, therefore, the vehicle is issued an Off-Road plate
and no yearly registration is issued
No class of driver license is required to operate the vehicle
The Vehicle Type in MAX is All Terrain Vehicle, All Terrain Cycle or Motorcycle
Primarily Off-Highway - Some Maintained Road Use
The vehicle will be used mostly off-road, but occasionally used on-road
Is issued a small-sized Motorcycle plate
Qualifies for reduced VLT of $3.00
Exempt from the Registration fee
Pays the Air Quality Fee and Air Quality Compliance Fee
Insurance is required
Applicable class of license is required to operate the vehicle

The Vehicle Type in MAX is All Terrain Vehicle, All Terrain Cycle or Motorcycle
Primarily On-Highway - Maintained Road Use
The vehicle will be used mostly on-road, but occasionally used off-road
Is issued a small-sized Motorcycle plate
Normal registration fees will apply
Insurance is required
Applicable class of license is required to operate the vehicle
The Vehicle Type in MAX is All Terrain Vehicle, All Terrain Cycle or Motorcycle
When completing a Registration service where the vehicle is Off-Highway, Primarily Off-
Highway, Primarily On- Highway, MAX will determine if a new / renewal OHV decal is required
and will add it when needed.
This means:
The Issue OHV Decal check box will automatically be checked
The appropriate OHV Decal fees will be added to the fees
Off Highway Vehicles (ATV or UTV) Over 2500 lbs Unladen Weight
All Terrain Vehicle with an unladen weight of 2501 or more pounds do not qualify for an OHV decal.
Note: The unladen weight will be taken from the MCO if applicable.
Current records will retain the established unladen weight unless the customer requests to update
it
Customer may elect to either have a title only or register the vehicle for highway use.
For highway use the following applies:
Must complete a Highway Use Certificate, form #96-0371
Is issued a small-sized Motorcycle plate
Normal registration fees will apply
Insurance is required
Applicable class of license is required to operate the vehicle
The Vehicle Type in MAX is All Terrain Vehicle
In order to prevent future OHV decals being issued, the unladen weight must be entered into
the vehicle record.

## OHV Education Course

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/OHV_EducationCourse.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Off-Highway Vehicles > OHV Education
Course
OHV Education Course
An Off-Highway Vehicle (OHV) Safety Education course will be required before an OHV decal can be
issued, per Laws 2024, Chapter 240, between January 1, 2025 to June 1, 2027.
The course is called: Required for OHV Decal: Safe & Ethical Riding in Arizona
The course:
Is free
Is 10-15 minutes long
Questions can be answered repeatedly until the correct answer is selected
Is only required once
E.g., Customer owns 4 OHVs; will only need to take the course once
Must be completed before any title and/or registration with the issuance of OHV decal is
completed
Can be completed on AZ MVD Now at any time through the following with an ASI:
In office on the Knowledge Testing stations in office may be used to complete the course
See Sending ASI for OHV Education Course to Knowledge Testing Station for steps
Customer's personal electronic device
See Sending ASI for OHV Education Course to Customer's Electronic Device for
steps
Education Course Requirements
Only Arizona residents are required to complete the course on AZ MVD Now
Nonresidents will need to complete the same course (Safe & Ethical Riding in Arizona) BUT
it will be taken on the Arizona Game and Fish website
Only one owner is required to complete the course
For organizations: A representative of the organization will be required to take the course;
will be required to sign into AZ MVD Now through the organization's account to take the
course
OHV Safety Certificates

When a customer has completed the OHV course with Game and Fish and the OHV Education
characteristic is not on the customer's record, you can add the characteristic as long as the customer
presents a completion certificate.
If the customer does not have a printed completion certificate, the course completion code can
be submitted via AMN. See Level 1 Call Center MVD One Source, Registration Menu topic >
Required for OHV Decal: Safe & Ethical Riding in Arizona (OHV Education Course) section for
steps on how to enter the completion code on AMN.
If the customer wishes not to complete the full AZ MVD Now account setup and authentication
process, send the customer an Assisted Self-Service Invitations (ASI), see Assisted Self-
Service Invitations (ASI) topic for additional information.
