## Ownership Priority, Role Types, & Legal Statuses

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Ownership_Priority_&_Legal_Status.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Ownership Priority, Role Types, & Legal
Statuses
Ownership Priority, Role Types, & Legal Statuses
Ownership Priority
When there is more than one owner associated to a vehicle, ownership priority must be assigned, as it
determines which owner is listed first on the vehicle title. When the priority is changed, MAX will automatically
adjust all other owners.
Ownership Priority may be tied in with the Role Type and Legal Status, view the sections below for specifics.
Role Type & Legal Status
Role Type and Legal Statuses are linked together. The Role Type indicates what type of ownership the
customer has over the vehicle. Legal Status indicates who has the legal authority to sign ownership
documents.
Depending on which Role Type is selected will indicate whether a Legal Status will be required. For instance,
when the vehicle is under a Lessee/Lessor situation, no Legal Status is required; however, for owners there
are three possible Legal Statuses to choose from.
Types of Roles
Legal Statuses
The Legal Status is the binding of two or more persons, companies, entities, or the combination of a person
or persons and a company sharing ownership of a vehicle or trailer. The status can also be applied to a
lienholder.
Legal Statuses will only be used for 'Owner' and 'Trustee' Role Types.
Each Legal Status indicates who has the legal authority to sign ownership documents for/when:
Ownership is changing
Title transfers
Original titles
Lienholders
Registration credit
A Legal Status between multiple owners determines which owner has first claim to any available Registration
Credit. View the Registration Credit topic > Registration Credit section for more information.

When transferring ownership into the new owners' name with the any of the below legal statues requires
ALL owners to sign the T&R Application or Title Summary page.
The Legal Status may be changed for $4.00 Title fee and the vehicle does not have to be currently registered.
When the record displays a lien, permission from the lienholder is required.
Types of Legal Status
Adding Additional Owners
1. To add an owner click the Add Customer button on the Owners tab of any registration related service.
2. Role Type: Use this field to select the ownership role this customer holds (e.g. owner, lessee, lessor,
registrant, etc.). If you have more than one owner, each may have a different Role Type.
3. Priority: Priority indicates the order owners names will be listed on Title documents. Depending on the
Role Type selected, you may or may not change the Priority.
4. Legal Status: If there is more than one owner, the legal status will define if they are an or, and/or or and
status. Depending on the Role Type selected (e.g. Lessee/Lessor), Legal Status will not be an option.
5. Registration and Title Mail Stars:If there is more than one owner, these stars will be solid blue for the
owner who will receive the title or registration documents, plates, renewal notifications, etc. Click the
empty star to indicate who should receive the Title or the Registration.
6. Notes: If notes are needed and are associate to this service, they are added here.
7. Add an Exemption:Use this feature to add an exemption specific to this owner (e.g. Non-Resident
Military, Tribal Member, etc.). View Fee Exemptions for more information on how to add an exemption.
8. Remove Ownership Icon: Use this to remove the owner from the service. Please note that this is instant
and cannot be undone. If you remove an owner by mistake you will need to add a customer.
9. Edit Customer: Use if the customer's address, AKA, characteristics or contact information needs to be
updated. View the Title & Registration topic > Edit Customer section for more information.

 
Until we are fully paperless, when you finish the T & R service for the customer, you will print out the
Title Summary (artifact), have the customer review and sign, and re-scan into MAX. This will replace
the process where you print the T&R Application for the customer to sign.

## Plate Order Search

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Plate_Order_Search.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Plates > Plate Order Search
Plate Order Search
Plate Order Search is a function within MAX where CSRs in field offices can view the status of plate
orders, and where the Special Plates Unit (SPU) can update the status.
If you are helping a customer because they say they haven't received their plate in the mail, ALWAYS
check this first to determine the status.
DO NOT reorder any plate that has a status of Pending, Ready to Order, In Production, or
Mailed.
If you have questions with the status of a plate order, please contact the Special Plates Unit by
email at specialplates2@azdot.gov with the Subject Line: Plate Order Error.
Mailed, Returned, Re-Mailed: Plates are mailed from and returned to the vendor.
The SPU does not handle any physical plates.
Plate Status Definitions
Pending - The plate order has not yet been reviewed or decided by SPU
Ready to Order - The plate order has been reviewed and approved by SPU and information has
been sent to vendor to make the plate
Canceled - The plate order was canceled.
Note: When a CSR needs to 'burn' a plate to Transfer Plates for a customer, they will need
to contact the SPU to cancel the plate order
Rejected - The requested plate was rejected by the SPU.
Example: A Personalized Plate may be rejected for exclusion reason such as profanity or
body part
In Production - The physical plate is currently being produced by the vendor
Mailed - The plate has been mailed to the customer and address indicated on the order
Returned - The plate was returned to the MVD by the Post Office
Re-Mailed - The plate was mailed a second time to the same address on the initial order
If address has changed, the customer will need to request a replacement plate

Returned Twice - The plate was mailed twice and returned to the MVD by the Post Office both
times
The plate will be destroyed and the customer will need to request a replacement plate
Checking the Status of  Plate Order

## Print Title

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Print_Title.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Print Title
Print Title
Formerly Duplicate Title
Vehicle Title documents are printed via Central Issuance, not at MVD field offices. In the MVD field
office you can print an Arizona Title Summary for a customer from the Customer Record.
The Print Title service is the only title service in MAX that triggers the centralized printing of the
physical title and mailing to the customer.
Only ATPs have the permission to print a paper title onsite and only for certain services. View the Dealer
Title Transfers topic > Authorized Third Party (ATP) - Printing a Title Over the Counter section for more
information on those services.
If a physical title was printed and owner claims non-delivery by the United States Postal
Department (e.g., renewed by mail, or AZ MVD Now, etc.), the $4.00 Title fee can be waived
provided the owner has not moved, still maintains the mailing address of record.
The claim of non-delivery must be made 10 days after the issue date, but within 45 days of the
issue date to qualify for a no-fee replacement.
When will you use the Print Title service?

## Reconstructed and Specially Constructed Vehicles

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Reconstructed_&_Specially_Constructed_Vehicles.htm`

Reconstructed and Specially Constructed Vehicles
The Division requires that every vehicle being titled and/or registered be identified by the name of the
manufacturer, a brief description of the vehicle and the VIN. Additionally, for the purpose of obtaining a
more complete description of the vehicle, the applicant is required to indicate whether the vehicle is
reconstructed or specially constructed.
Reconstructed Vehicles
A reconstructed vehicle is defined as any vehicle which has been assembled or constructed largely by
means of new or used essential parts from other vehicles. A change to any of the essential parts may
require a vehicle to be classified as a reconstructed vehicle.
Essential Parts
Documentation Required for a Reconstructed Vehicle
Inspections for Reconstructed Vehicles
Reconstructed Brand Categories
Specially Constructed Vehicles
There are four types of specially constructed vehicles:
Dune Buggies
Homemade trailers (view the Homemade Trailers topic for more information)
Kit Vehicles (Glider Kits) or Trailers
Sandrails
Dune Buggies
Kit Vehicles and Trailers
Sand Rails
Until we are fully paperless, when you finish the T & R service for the customer, you will print
out the Title Summary (artifact), have the customer review and sign, and re-scan into MAX. This
will replace the process where you print the T&R Application for the customer to sign.

## Recreational Vehicles

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Recreational_Vehicles.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Off-Highway Vehicles > Recreational
Vehicles
Recreational Vehicles
Recreational vehicles can be titled and registered in Arizona for specific uses. The following vehicles are
considered recreational and each have their own title and registration requirements.
Golf Carts
Mopeds
Motor Driven Cycles and Scooters
Neighborhood Electric Vehicles (NEVs)
Quadricycles
Off-Road/Off-Highway/Recreational Motor Vehicles
Golf Carts
The Division is authorized to register a golf cart for highway use. A golf cart is a motor vehicle that:
Is primarily designed to carry four or fewer persons
Is designed to be and is operated at speeds of 25 miles per hour or less
Has at least four wheels in contact with the ground
Has an unladen weight of 1800 lbs.or less
Vehicle Requirements for a Golf Cart
Unregistered Golf Cart
Modified Golf Carts
Golf Cart Designed by the Manufacturer
Golf Cart Modified by the Customer
Title and Registration Requirements for a Golf Cart
Golf Carts and Electric Non-Compliant Fuel Type
Golf Carts and OHV Decals
Examples of Golf Carts
Title and Register in MAX
View the Title and Registration topic for more information on how to process a golf cart.

Mopeds
A moped is a vehicle that meets the following criteria:
Human powered, aided with a helper motor
Engine must be no more than 50 cubic centimeters (cc)
Maximum rated speed is 25 mph or less
A cycle (49 cc or above) that does not meet all of the above moped criteria, but is
manufactured primarily for use on public streets, roads, and highways, will be considered a
motor driven cycle and is subject to title and/or registration as a motorcycle.
View the Unique Vehicle Guide for more information on mopeds.
Documentation Required for a Moped
How Registration is Issued for a Moped
Examples of Mopeds
Title and Register in MAX
View the Arizona Registration Only (ARO)/ Special Interstate Registration (SPI) topic for more
information on how to process a moped.
Motor Driven Cycles and Scooters
Certain types of motor scooters fall under the federal definition of a motor vehicle and are subject to the
same Federal Motor Vehicle Safety Standards (FMVSS) administered by the United States Department
of Transportation's (U.S D.O.T.) National Highway Traffic Safety Administration (NHTSA) that currently
apply to motorcycles and motor driven cycles if they are:
Of the same general body type as a motorcycle or motor driven cycle
Manufactured primarily for use on public streets and roadways
Manufactured to be operated at speeds above 20 miles per (MPH)
Motor scooters are further defined for the purpose of Arizona title, registration, and driver license
requirements as a motor driven cycle, which means a motorcycle, including every motor scooter, with a
motor that does not produce more than five horsepower.
Motor driven cycles and/or motor scooters are titled and registered by the Division only if all of the
following federal and state safety and equipment requirements are met:
Have a manufacturer assigned 17-digit VIN
Have a Federal label certifying the vehicle conforms to all applicable U.S. Federal Motor Vehicle
Safety Standards in effect on the indicated date of manufacture or all other official documentation
required to ensure FMVSS compliance
Have been manufactured primarily for use on public streets and roadways
Have been originally manufactured to exceed 20 MPH

Have an engine displacement of 49 cc or above
Have a seat to be straddled by the operator and handlebars for steering control
Have not more than three wheels in contact with the ground
Have met all applicable safety and equipment requirements in A.R.S. Title 28, Chapter 3, Article
16
The vehicle owner is responsible for making sure the required equipment is in place
This is accomplished by the submitting a properly completed Highway Use Certificate, form
#96-0371, certifying the vehicle meets all applicable safety and equipment requirements for
on-road use
CSRs will follow all current procedures on verification of VIN, body style, and import
documentation as applicable.
Neighborhood Electric Vehicles (NEV)
A Neighborhood Electric Vehicle (NEV) is a self-propelled electric powered motor vehicle that must meet
all of the following criteria:
Is emission free
Has at least four wheels in contacts with the ground
Complies with low-speed vehicles set forth in the Federal Motor Vehicle Safety Standard 500 and
49 Code of Federal Regulations
Vehicle Requirements for a NEV
Restrictions on a NEV
Issuing Title and Registration for a NEV
Examples of NEVs
Title and Register in MAX
View the Title and Registration and Alternative Fuel Vehicle topics for more information on Alternative
Fuel vehicles and how to process a NEV.
Off-Road Vehicles
An off-road recreational motor vehicle is defined as a motor vehicle designed primarily for recreational
non-highway all-terrain travel.
There are three types of off-road vehicles:
Off-highway vehicle
All-terrain vehicle
Off-road recreational motor vehicle
Off-Highway Vehicle

An off-highway vehicle is defined as a motorized vehicle that is operated primarily off of highways and
that is designed, modified or purpose built primarily for recreational non-highway all-terrain travel. An
off-highway vehicle may be a (an):
Utility vehicle
All-terrain vehicle
Motorcycle
Four wheel drive vehicle
Dune buggy
Sand rail
All-Terrain Vehicle
An all-terrain vehicle is defined as a motor vehicle designed primarily for recreational non-highway all-
terrain travel that travels on three or more non-highway tires.
Motorcycles, All-terrain Cycles (ATC), and All-Terrain Vehicles (ATV) are exempt from emission testing,
view Policy 8.9.3 Emission Requirements for more information.
Off-Road Recreational Motor Vehicle
An off-road recreational motor vehicle is defined as a motor vehicle designed, modified or purpose built
primarily for recreational non-highway all-terrain travel and that is not operated on a public highway.
A motor vehicle used for construction, building, trade, mining, or agricultural purposes is
NOT considered an off-road recreational motor vehicle.
Documentation Required for an Off-Road Vehicle
Issuing Title and/or Registration for an Off-Road Vehicle
View the OHV Registration Chart for more information.
Examples of Off-Road Vehicles
Title and Register in MAX
View the Title Only or Title and Registration topic for more information on how to process.

## Registration Credit

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Registration_Credit.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > TnR Fees & Credit > Registration Credit
Registration Credit
When a owner sells a vehicle, the plate and its registration fees are separated from the vehicle and becomes
Registration Credit and the owner will retain the plate.
In order for a customer to receive a Registration Credit, they must be a Level 3 Customer. See Customer
Account Requirements (Level 3 Customer) for more information.
If the customer is not a Level 3 customer, the service will just spin in MAX as shown:
Eligible Fees
Requesting Registration Credit
The owner(s) may request the funds (registration fees) to be transferred to their Customer Account. This can be done
by a CSR, or the owner through AZ MVD Now. View the Registration Credit with Multiple Owners section for more
information how credit is handled.
At the time the credit is requested, the $12 Registration Credit fee is automatically deducted. If the customer has $12
or less in Registration Credit, MAX will display a message 'No credit is available.'
If the funds are not transferred, at the request of the owner, to their Customer Account, the funds will
decrease each month.
The Request Registration Credit service allows a CSR to issue a credit for eligible fees. View the Requesting
Registration Credit section for more information.
Once the funds have been transferred to their Customer Account, the owner can request a disbursement of funds, or
can leave the funds in their account and use the funds toward the payment of any future Division service. View the
Request Customer Disbursement for more information. If multiple owners, view the Registration Credit with Multiple
Owners section.

When a new owner of a vehicle is entitled to the plate and/or registration credit (e.g., non-probate, probate,
etc.), registration credit may be applied during the service. View the Titles Without Court Orders topic >
Customer Entitled to Plate and/or Registration Credit section for more information on how to handle the plate
and/or credit.
Registration Credit with Multiple Owners
When there are multiple owners on an Arizona vehicle title record, the Registration Credit will still be transferred to a
Customer Account. Most of the time the credit will be transferred to the first owner making the request, regardless of
the legal status between owners, but if more than one owner wants a share of the Registration Credit, you will have
to take a couple extra steps. View the below chart for more information.
All owners technically have a right to the credit.
If one owner makes the request and the credit/funds are transferred to their Customer Account, the owners
will have to determine if one owner will keep all of the credit, or if funds (full or partial) need to be transferred
to another owner's Customer Account.
View the Transfer Customer Account topic for more information how to transfer funds.
View the Request Customer Disbursement topic for more information on disbursing the funds.
Registration Credit and Legal Statuses Chart
Requesting Registration Credit
Viewing the Registration Credit in the Customer Record
Customer Does Not 'Request' Registration Credit
The balance will remain associated to the Vehicle and decrement monthly until there is a zero balance.
If the customer does not request the Registration Credit, the credit balance will decrease monthly until there
is a zero balance. When the Registration Credit is requested and deposited into the Customer Account, the
balance will NOT decrease.
Disbursement Check for Credit Goes Unclaimed
View the Request Customer Disbursement topic > Disbursement Check Goes Unclaimed section for more
information
Policies

8.14.5 Registration Credit

## Registration Cycles

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Registration_Cycles.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Registration Cycles
Registration Cycles
All vehicle registrations expire according to schedules established by the Division. The 'initial
registration' of a vehicle is defined as the first time the vehicle is subject to registration in Arizona. This
is based on the Ownership Date entered in the Title and Registration tab in a Title service and creates
the registration cycle; which is the Effective Date and Expiration Date (which is the beginning and end of
the registration cycle).
Example of Ownership Date
Ownership Date in Title Service
The Ownership Date helps determine the registration cycle, the fees, and expiration date calculated by
MAX. In order to calculate the proper fees, the proper date needs to be entered.
Out-of-state titles: Use today's date
Arizona titles & MCOs: Use date of sale
Even though today's date or date of sale is entered as the Ownership Date, if permits were issued those
dates may factor into the registration cycle. View the Ownership Date & Month Portion section for more
information.
Incorrect Ownership Date Entered for Out-of-State Titles
First Registered
The First Registered is based on when the vehicle is first registered in Arizona and consists of a month
and year portion, which is partially based off of the Ownership Date. MAX determines the First
Registered based off information (dates or permits) entered during the Title service. The First
Registered date will not change, however, the registration cycle (Effective Date and Expiration Date) will
change.
Example of First Registered
Month Portion
The month portion of First Registered will be one month forward from the following (which ever is
earliest):
Purchase date
Temporary Registration (TRP) issue date

90-Day Resident Registration issue date
Date of application
Ownership Date & Month Portion
Out-of-State Titles: Ownership Date will be today's date, however, if there was a TRP or 90-Day
Resident Registration, MAX will use their issue dates to determine the month portion, which would also
affect the month portion of the Expiration Date.
MCOs & Arizona Titles: Ownership Date will be date of sale, unless any of the option listed above
apply, then MAX will use those dates to determine the month portion, which would also affect the month
portion of the Expiration Date.
Year Portion
For new vehicles (MCOs) will be the current year, unless purchased in December, then it will be
the next year
For vehicles that are more than two years old, the year should be the model year of the vehicle
For vehicles with an out-of-state title will be the model year of the vehicle
There is no policy that supports using the first registered information from an out-of-state title.
Incorrect First Registered
Registration Effective Date
The vehicle's Effective Date is based on when the vehicle is first registered in Arizona. It is the
beginning part of the registration cycle and consists of a month and year portion. MAX determines the
Effective Date based off information (dates or permits) entered during the Title service.
The Effective Date and Expiration Date (registration cycle) will change when ownership is transferred to
new owners.
Month Portion
Initially, the month portion is based on when the vehicle is first registered in Arizona and consists of a
month and year portion.
Once ownership has transferred, the month portion will be based on when the vehicle was purchased.
Year Portion
The year portion will be the year of sale. Based on this year portion, the Expiration Date can be one,
two, or five years from the year listed.
Registration Expiration Date
The vehicle's Expiration Date is the end of the registration cycle and consists of a month, day, and year
portion. MAX determines the Registration Expiration based off information (dates or permits) entered
during the Title service.

Month Portion
The month portion of the vehicle's expiration date will be the month of:
Purchase date
Temporary Registration (TRP) issue date
90-Day Resident Registration issue date
Date of application
Day Portion
The day portion of the expiration is based on when in the month the vehicle is being registered:
If the vehicle purchase date is the 1st through the 15th of the month, the expiration date will be
the 15th day of the month
If the vehicle purchase date is the 16th through the last day of the month, the expiration date will
be the last day of the month
If no permit was issued and there was a previous “Title Only”, the expiration date will be the 15th
or last day of the month of application
Year Portion
The year portion of the vehicle could be one, two, or five years from the year portion of the Effective
Date, depending on vehicle or customer requirements.
Policy
8.9.2 Regisration Cycles

## Arizona Registration Only (ARO)/ Special Interstate Registration (SPI)

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Registration_Only_(ARO).htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Arizona Registration Only (ARO)/ Special
Interstate Registration (SPI)
Arizona Registration Only (ARO)/ Special Interstate Registration
(SPI)
Arizona Registration Only (ARO) or a Special Interstate Registration (SPI) is for vehicles in Arizona that
are not eligible to be titled in Arizona. Both AROs and SPIs will be processed the same way in MAX.
A customer moves to Arizona from another state and their vehicle has a lien holder in that state,
the customer cannot title their vehicle in Arizona because they do not possess the title.
They are, however, required to register their vehicle in Arizona when they are a resident. This
ensures they are paying the appropriate vehicle license taxes and fees to support the
development and maintenance of Arizona roadways.
View the Residency Requirements topic for more information.
 
If the customer needs to know what state the vehicle is titled in, view the AAMVA Helpdesk -
Vehicle topic for steps on how to run the NM14 - Vehicle History Inquiry.

Names & AKAs on Titles
Depending on the situation, names on out-of-state titles or registrations, or title transfer with lienholder,
must stay the same and cannot be change during data entry in MAX.
Customer has their maiden name on out-of-state registration and their DL or ID has their
married name.
View the Title and Registration topic > Title and Registration or Choosing an AKA During a Title and Reg
Service sections for more information.

NATO/Military Member
If you have a NATO member asking for an ARO, view the Grey Market Vehicles topic > North Atlantic
Treaty Organization Agreement (NATO) section for more information on required documentation.

## Renew Registration

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Registration_Renewal.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Renew Registration
Renew Registration
After a vehicle has been initially titled and registered, the owner should receive a pre-printed Renewal
Invitation notice in the mail, or by email.
Vehicles expiring on the 15th and the last day of the month, the registration notices are emailed
or printed to be mailed the first weekend of the immediate month prior to the expiration of the
registration cycle.
It is the responsibility of the vehicle owner to renew the registration before the expiration date, even if
the Renewal Invitation (registration renewal) notice is not received.
Once the registration is renewed a new registration is printed.
If a customer just needs an idea of what the registration fees will be, use the Fee Estimation
service.

## Sold Notice

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Sold_Notice.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Vehicle Management > Sold Notice
Sold Notice
What is a Sold Notice?
It is an action taken by a vehicle owner to notify the Division that a vehicle or mobile home is sold. The
owner should submit this notice within 10 calendar days after sale of the vehicle. This notice is
protection for the vehicle owner from liability of tickets, accidents, etc. that may occur after the sale. A
Sold Notice is NOT a transfer of ownership.
How a Sold Notice is Submitted
A Sold Notice may be submitted with the Division, mail or via AZ MVD Now.
A Sold Notice can be written:
On a plain piece of paper, which is acceptable provided all required information is present
and the seller has signed it
On a Sold Notice, form #46-8502
A Sold Notice may be added to the vehicle record when told verbally by the customer without
completing the form.
MAX will generate a Sold Notice 'receipt' to the customer's record.
A completed Sold Notice will include:
VIN or Plate Number
Year of vehicle
Make of vehicle
Buyer's name and address, if known
Date the vehicle was sold
Seller's signature

For an owner with a vehicle with an IRP Apportioned Registration who is looking to get fees
refunded, DO NOT add a Sold Notice in an attempt to make the fees available for refund. Refer
them to the Motor Carrier Unit. When the vehicle is deleted from the IRP Fleet they will be given
the option for the refund of AZ fees or getting an account credit to be utilized toward a future
IRP registration (all fees).

Printing a Sold Notice
Adding a Sold Notice for an Export Vehicle
Removing a Sold Notice

## Personalized and Special Plates

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Special_Plates.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Plates > Personalized and Special Plates
Personalized and Special Plates
Personalized Plates
Many special plates may be personalized, along with the Standard plate.
A combination of numbers and/or letters
Combination must not conflict with any currently issued plates
Combination can be alphanumeric characters consisting of one to seven characters; which
is dependent on the type of plate being personalized or if a disability symbol is being
requested
Characters may be a:
Letter
Number
Space or a half space
Symbols (such as =, or any other special characters) are not available, with the
exception of the disability symbol
Disability-Deaf/Hard of Hearing Plate/Placard Application Individual, form #96-
0104 is required if a Disability plate or permanent placard has not been issued
Displayed on the back of the vehicle
Personalized plate fees are paid annually, along with a one-time initial postage/handling fee
The personalized plate fees for Standard plate do not go to any specific organization; they
are remitted back to the Division
Personalization choices must be approved by the Special Plates Unit
If denied, the Special Plates Unit will refund the customer's account
Personalized plates can be ordered during a T&R service, AZ MVD Now, or through the mail
A Special Plate Application, form #96-0143 is required to be completed and submitted only
when ordering a special plate through mail)
Special Plates
The Division offers special plates that represent, but is not limited to, colleges, the environment, and
organizations (view the Plate Selections Gallery on the ADOT website for plate background designs).

Some special plates can be personalized (view the Plates Available as Personalized or Standard
section for more information), have the disability symbol, and/or have eligibility requirements.
View the Eligibility Requirements for Special Plates section and click the plate name to view the specific
eligibility requirements for special plates.
When ordering a special plate, personalized standard or personalized special plate for an
alternative fuel vehicle, an Alternative Fuel sticker will be required for Tier 1 & Tier 2 vehicle to
keep their discounted VLT rate.
The Alternative Fuel sticker cannot be issued to a regular standard plate but may be issued to
a personalized standard plate.
See Fuel Types & Alternative Fuel Vehicles topic > Alternative Fuel Sticker section and the
Alternative Fuel Vehicles License Plate Scenarios Chart for additional information.
Once a service is completed, the special plate will be mailed to the customer, along with the registration
with tab.
View the Postage and Handling Fees chart for fees for different types of plate scenarios.

MAX generates and assigns license plate numbers. If a temporary plate generates that looks
blatantly derogatory, profane, or inappropriate, or if the customer points out that the plate
seems inappropriate (Offensive Standard Plate), the CSR would need to:
Complete anUpdate Registration service
SelectIssue New Plate for a new plate to be generated. Fees will need to be zeroed
out during the service
Have your upline approver complete the following to request for refund of incorrect
plate if the initial service was completed the same day:
Submit a Service Portal ticket
SelectSpecial Plates from the Area Impacted drop down
In the Detailed description field, begin the description by typing the appropriate
cancellation reason, and then typeany additional information needed
Cancellation Reasons:
Address Error
Burner Plate
Offensive Standard Plate
Ordered wrong background/Plate Type Error
Personalization Error
Spacing Error
*If the service is completed after 4pm on the same day as the initial service, the customer
must be notified that they MAY receive both plates AND an upline approver should still submit
the Service Portal ticket to the Special Plates Unit (SPU).
**ATPs Only:This process can only be done Monday thru Friday (not on weekends), as you
must submit the form the SAME day as processing the plate.
If a customer reports a plate they believe to be inappropriate, and they are not the owner of
the plate, please have the customer complete the License Plate Complaint, form #96-0577.
If there is a reason to cancel a plate request (e.g., personalization error, plate type error,
wrong address, etc.), view the Personalized and Special Plates topic > Plate Ordering Issues
section.
When the Special Plates Unit - Plate Support Request form is submitted for an Offensive
Standard Plate, this will inform the SPU to place the plate number on the 'no-no' list.
View the Personalized Plates: No-No List section for more information.

Customers with questions or concerns that wish to speak to the Special Plates Unit may call
the MVD Contact Center at: 602-255-0072. Do not give them the Special Plates Unit direct
number.
CSRs should see a Lead, Supervisor, or Manager with any issues pertaining to the Special
Plate Unit.

## Suspend Registration

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Suspend_Registration.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Suspend Registration
Suspend Registration
This function allows an authorized user to suspend a vehicle registration when there is no incident or
withdrawal associated to the situation warranting the suspension (e.g., as a part of an ongoing
investigation or at the request of OIG).
For most registration suspensions, an incident would trigger a Withdrawal action. Notification would be
made to the customer to let them know their vehicle registration will be suspended if the customer
doesn't meet specified criteria within the defined period of time.
Manual registration suspensions also trigger a notification to the customer. However, they will not
necessarily include Compliance Requirements because the suspension was not triggered as part of an
Incident Management process. The Suspend Registration service in MAX can be applied to vehicles
with an Active, Permanent, or Expired registration status.
Suspend Registration

## T and R Corrections

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/T_&_R_Corrections.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > T and R Corrections
T and R Corrections
The T&R Corrections service allows a CSR to correct an error on a title and/or registration due to data
that did not convert properly or other CSR errors that require supervisor approval, such as:
Odometer corrections
Ownership corrections
Make / Model / Body Style corrections (including length and width for travel trailers)
VIN - Only when there is an Arizona title on record
When a VIN needs to be corrected on an ARO or SPI, you cannot use T&R Corrections,
because the Title tab does not display any information (including the VIN). Have an upline
approver submit a Service Portal ticket in order for the VIN to be corrected.
It is important that all supporting documents must be scanned as a 'T and R Correction
Service'. Some of these documents include but not limited to: out-of-state title, level 1
inspection form, service history documents (ownership documents), etc.
This service is designed to make corrections related to data that didn't convert properly from TPX. There
may be scenarios where using T & R Corrections to fix a CSR error is permitted, but you will be required
to get a supervisor override to do so. An example would be to fix a T & R service for a vehicle that is
new in the state, where you forgot to add the second owner. There would be no previous title and/or
registration to "re-establish" so you would need to use this service. See Cancel Re-Establish Title and
Cancel Re-Establish Registration for more details about "resetting" a prior Title or Registration to
correct an error made by a CSR.
Any changes made on the Title tab of the service will result in a new title being sent to AAMVA.
This services does NOT charge or credit any fees.
Depending on changes made, an override may be requested.
All changes made in this service will be audited.
When correcting a Title or Registration record, the Customer selected to begin the
service must be listed on the ownership of the vehicle.
When to Correct a Title Record

A corrected title record is required when any of the following types of errors are discovered:
Vehicle description errors that change the original vehicle description, such as the vehicle make,
VIN, year, or body style
Verification of the correct information by archived documents (OnBase or MAX)
Ownership errors that change the name of the owner or lienholder
Registration (plate, insurance, emissions, exemptions)
There are very few restrictions on what can be changed, but it is important to remember to
use the service only to fix data errors.
Other T & R adjustments should be made using the applicable service in MAX (e.g. use the Update
Registration service to change a license plate or the declared gross weight**).
**Screens in MAX will read as Declared GVW, which is considered the declared gross weight
of the vehicle.
The declared gross weight will be displayed in the Declared Weight field under the
Registration section on the Vehicle Information Page.
Policy will use the term declared gross weight.
Titles & Corrections
With MAX, titles are electronic title records held within MAX. When corrections are required, proceed
with making any required changes to the title record, with or without the current Arizona title (if
applicable), as long as supporting documentation is available and scanned and uploaded into MAX.

A paper title exists, a T & R Correction needs to be done, technically certain scenarios used
to require the paper title to be submitted (say the VIN needs to be corrected), but the
customer lost the title, or they simply didn't bring the title with them. Just make the corrections
in MAX and scan and upload any supporting documentation in to the service to note the
correction.
A paper title exists, customer needs a service done that requires a CSR to use T&R
Corrections or Title & Reg service, and a new title number now exists and it's electronic. When
the customer goes to sell the vehicle the Title Transfer (Bill of Sale) form will be used to
transfer ownership.
If the customer does have the paper Arizona title with them at time of correction and
corrections were made, inform your customer the title is no longer valid and needs to be
destroyed.
It is no longer valid because a new title number was created.
Required Forms for Corrections
When the Division initiates the correction, a designated CSR will need to prepare and mail a letter to the
owner and send a T&R Application (Single), form #96-0236 along with the letter requesting a signature
and any other required documentation, if applicable.
If the correction involves a record with an ELT, the CSR's upline approver must contact the
MVD ELT Help Desk by phone. View the Electronic Lien and Title (ELT) topic for MVD ELT Help Desk
contact information.
If the lienholder initiates the correction, the lienholder will write 'on file' in the 'All Owners Sign Here' box
on the T&R Application (Single), form #96-0236 or Arizona Title Summary. If it is a ELT title, the
lienholder must contact their ELT Service Provider.
Starting a T and R Corrections Service

T and R Corrections - Owners Tab

## Title and Reg Recreate

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/T_&_R_Recreate.htm`

Title and Reg Recreate
T & R Recreate is a service designed to allow MAX users to recreate title records for an Arizona vehicle
when the existing data did not successfully convert from the previous legacy system (TPX) to MAX.
Use the T&R Recreate service only for title records with inactivity.
DO NOT USE to recreate the registration part of the title record.
This service should only be used when title data already exists, but did not fully populate the MAX
records.
To create new title and registration documents for a customer use the Title and Reg, Registration
Only, or Title Only services in MAX
To correct errors in title and registration documents use the T & R Corrections service
To update information on title and registration documents when details have changed, use the
Update Registration service in MAX
Before a T & R Recreate service is initiated, the existing title record should first be searched for in
Accomodative Data Search - Vehicle Snapshot - Accommodative Data. If the original record is found
they should be used to add/complete any missing data in MAX.
If the ownership is being transferred and the record has been purged, you will re-create the
record in the prior owner’s name first (exactly as the Arizona title states) and then transfer the
title to the new owner in MAX.
Older Purged Records

## Tab Replacement

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Tab_Replacement.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Tab Replacement
Tab Replacement
When a customer requests a new vehicle registration decal (tab) or duplicate registration (registration
was renewed in MAX after go-live) that has been lost, stolen or damaged, the request may be done in
office or through AZ MVD Now, using the Tab Replacement or Update Registration services.
If only replacing the decal (tab), it is recommended that you use the Tab Replacement service
in MAX.
Only use the Update Registration service if making other updates to the registration in addition
to replacing the decal (tab).
Once processed, the registration and decal will be mailed to the customer.
Requirements for Requesting a Tab Replacement in Office
The vehicle registration must be current
Can only be picked up by the registered owner, family member (with the same last name and
address as the registered owner), or by using a POA from the registered owner
Pays $5.00
This fee can be waived provided the owner has not moved, still maintains the mailing
address of record and claims non-delivery by the United States Postal Department (e.g.,
renewed by mail, or AZ MVD Now, etc.). The claim of non-delivery must be made 10 days
after the issue date, but within 45 days of the issue date to qualify for a no-fee
replacement
Processing a Tab Replacement

## Title and Registration

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Title_&_Registration.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Title and Registration
Title and Registration
The person establishes ownership when they purchase the vehicle, then shows the documentation to
the Division to prove ownership. During a Title and Registration service, ownership of the vehicle is
recorded. The Arizona vehicle title record and/or title is a recording of ownership.
Registration is required to operate a vehicle on public roads, and it indicates the customer has paid
required fees. As part of the registration process the customer will receive a license plate, a registration
document and tab to place on the plate. View the Title Document Overview topic for more specific
information on the physical documents.
MAX How To Video Series: Title and Registration
When additional documentation (e.g., POA, trust, court document, etc.) is used to give
authority to an individual, those documents must be scanned with other supporting
documentation.
Verify the signature with the submitted documentation (POA, trust paperwork, court
document, etc.).
If the vehicle being titled and/or registered is an recreational vehicle and an OHV decal is
requested, the OHV Education Course may be required.
For additional information, see OHV Education Course.
Electronic Titles and Records
Arizona Title Transfers
Electronic Title Transfer (ETT)
MCO or Factory Invoice Transfers

Lessee and Lessor
Trusts

Late Title Transfer Penalty Fees

The PEN fees are assessed when a title service is not completed in the allotted time period as required
per Arizona State Law. View the Fees (T and R) Overview topic > Late Title Transfer Penalty Fees
(PEN) section for more information on the PEN fees.
Issuing a Title and Registration
Advise the customer the title from this service will be 'electronic' and viewable in
AZ MVD Now > Title Viewer.
They will get an Arizona Title Summary at the end of this service, but not a title printed on
secure title paper.
If there is no lien on the vehicle and they would like a printed title, you will have to finalize this
service and then do a Print Title service. That title will be printed centrally and mailed to the
customer.
If processing a T&R service and Print Title service on the same day, the Title Application or
the Arizona Title Summary, will only need to be printed, signed, and scanned once for the
T&R service.  The day after, or later, will require the Title Application or Arizona Title Summary
to be printed, signed, and scanned.
The instructions below assume the vehicle and the customer records are already in MAX. Before getting
started, use the search engine to look for the vehicle and for the customer. If not found, add the vehicle
and/or the customer to MAX before you start the Title & Registration Service.
If the Customer Record is Restricted or Redacted, MAX will NOT stop you from completing
the Title & Registration Service. If you proceed without first calling to have the Restriction
lifted, all service artifacts will print with redacted address information.

## Title Document Overview

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Title_Document_Overview.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Title Documents > Title Document
Overview
Title Document Overview
Title documents and most supporting documentation is required to be scanned into MAX.
View the Document Management topic for more information on required T&R documents to
scan.
Arizona Titles
An Arizona Certificate of Title (title) is the ownership document of a vehicle that is titled in Arizona. The
title is valid for the life of the vehicle, provided the vehicle ownership remains the same.
When the owner of a vehicle, titled in Arizona, decides to sell their vehicle to an individual, company, or
dealership, the current Arizona paper title or Title Transfer (Bill of Sale), form #38-1306 must be
properly signed,
For additional information, see Company or Dealership: Completing the Buyer or Seller Section
on Title if it is a company or dealership that is buying or selling a vehicle for signature
requirements
For additional information, see the Forms Required for T & R topic > Title Transfer (Bill of Sale)
section for more information on the bill of sale
Once a title is properly signed, the title will represent acceptable proof of ownership and will be
surrendered to the buyer. The properly signed Arizona title will then be used to transfer ownership to the
new buyer.
A signature is acceptable if it is within the signature block, or if it is adjacent to (above, below,
left, right) the signature block if there are no other conflicting signatures which would call into
question the validity of that signature.
When a vehicle is sold on an 'open title' (the first purchaser of a vehicle, as shown on the sold notice
filed with Division (if applicable), sells the vehicle to another person without first obtaining title) the
Division will allow the owner in possession of the signed title to be entered on the title as the purchaser.
Current Arizona Title (December 2023 - Present)
Description of Front of Arizona Title (December 2023 - Present)

Description of Back of Arizona Title (December 2023 - Present)
Previous Arizona Titles
November 2023 - Present
September 2022 - October 2023
April 2020 - September 2022
1992 - April 2020
Additional Examples of Arizona Titles
Manufactured Certificate of Origin
The Manufactured Certificate of Origin (MCO) is the birth certificate document for any vehicle or trailer
issued by all vehicle manufacturers (Ford, GMC, Honda, etc.) and is sent the licensed dealership for
every vehicle the dealership purchases.
Description of Front of MCO
Description of Back of MCO
Out-of-State Title Documents
There are two different scenarios for out-of-state titles:
1. A customer has moved to the state of Arizona and is becoming a resident
2. An out-of-state person sells their vehicle to an Arizona resident
This scenario requires the title to be properly re-assigned and endorsed
An Arizona resident, who is to be the new owner of the vehicle, must obtain a title in their name before
selling or transferring ownership to another person, company, or dealer.

## Title Only

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Title_Only.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Title Only
Title Only
Title Only is to transfer ownership of a vehicle without registering the vehicle. The most common
reasons for a Title Only is when a dealer is titling the vehicle in their name with the intention of selling it,
or when a customer purchases a vehicle in Arizona and intends to take it to another state to title and
register it. View the Title Document Overview topic for more information on a title or view the Forms
Required for T & R topic > Title Transfer (Bill of Sale) section for more information.
A Title Only service is $4.00 and is subject to Vehicle Use Tax requirements and applicable late title
transfer penalty fees.
If the customer is requesting registration after a Title Only has been processed, view the Title
and Registration service.
MAX will recognize the title already exists for the customer and prompt for registration to be
completed.
Title Only Qualifications
The Division may issue a title without registration for any of the following reasons:
The vehicle is being titled in the name of one of the following:
New Arizona Licensed Motor Vehicle Dealer
Used Arizona Licensed Motor Vehicle Dealer
Wholesale Motor Vehicle Dealer
Public Consignment Auction Dealer
The vehicle is apportionally registered in any state under the International Registration Plan (IRP)
The customer must provide documentation (e.g., Apportioned Registration, Cab Card) that
shows the customer’s or company’s participation in the IRP as evidence that the trailing
equipment will be pulled by a vehicle that is apportionally registered, when titling any trailing
equipment
This includes any trailing equipment that would be pulled by a apportionally
registered vehicle in any state under (IRP)
If the vehicle never had apportioned registration issued in any state, the customer must
contact the Motor Carrier Licensing Unit by email at mvdmcs@azdot.gov or by phone at
602-712-6775

View the policy 8.10.3 Apportioned Registration for more information
The applicant is a nonresident who purchase a vehicle located in Arizona and is taking the vehicle
to the nonresident’s state for registration. The certificate of title, and documentation of non-
residency is required (e.g., an out-of-state driver license).
See Residency Requirements topic for information on what is considered an Arizona
resident
See 90-Day Non-Resident (Drive-Out) Permit topic > Proof of Non-Residency section for
acceptable documentation
The vehicle is exempt from registration view the policies 8.9.1 Registration Requirements and
8.13.4 Off Highway Vehicle for more information
The vehicle was acquired by repossession, forfeiture, or operation of law (court ordered title
transaction)
The applicant certifies with the Title Only Certification, form #48-0107 that the vehicle was
acquired for purposes other than highway use, and a Level I Vehicle Inspection is completed prior
to processing *. The Level I Inspection may be completed on the Title Only Certification, form #48-
0107 or on the Level I Vehicle Inspection, form #48-4503. Acceptable non-highway uses include:
To be placed on exhibition, or display
To be repaired, rebuilt, reconstructed, or dismantled
The vehicle has currently failed emissions within 15 days and needs repairs (proof required
to waive Level I Inspection requirement)
Failed emissions results include: Failed, Rejected, and Not Ready
The vehicle is currently in a tow yard or storage facility (proof required to waive Level
I Inspection)
The vehicle is currently impounded (proof required to waive Level I Inspection requirement)
If there are any questions whether the vehicle requires registration or is eligible for a Title Only, see an
upline approver for direction.
*An Arizona Driver License / Identification Card along with proof of Arizona residency may be
accepted to waive the Level I inspection.
This option is only allowed for individuals who are taking title in their name and is not
applicable for organizations
An Arizona Residency Affidavit, form #40-5143 is NOT an acceptable document for proof of
Arizona residency for a Title Only.
The address on the credential does not need to match the proof of Arizona residency being
provided.

ADOT MVD documents are accepted as proof of residency as long as the documents were
mailed to the customer's residential address that is listed on the application. Over-the-counter
documents such as temp credentials and vehicle permits are not acceptable, view Arizona
Travel DL/ID Documentation Requirements section for more information.
*The Level I Vehicle Inspection is waived for vehicles acquired by an Arizona education
institution to be repaired, rebuilt, reconstructed, or dismantled; or for vehicles acquired by an
Arizona professional driver training school conducting driver training on a private, closed
course.
Names and AKAs on Titles
Depending on the situation, names on out-of-state titles or registrations, or title transfer with lienholder,
must stay the same and cannot be changed during data entry in MAX.
Customer has their maiden name printed in the Buyer's Name field on the title.
A lienholder is printed in the New Lienholder field on the title (or added to the T&R Application
(Single), form #96-0236).
Their DL or ID has their married name.
The name to be added to the title/record must be their maiden name.
View the Title and Registration topic > Title and Registration or Choosing an AKA During a Title and Reg
Service sections for more information.
Back to Back Title Transfers
The Title Only Certification is not required when processing a back to back title transfer, view T and R
Scenarios > Title Transfer: Buyer to Buyer; Back to Back (In Office) section for more information.
Title Only for Out-of-State Dealers
Out-of-State Dealers who are requesting a title only in Arizona and do not have a valid Arizona Dealer
License must follow the Title Only process. This includes completing the Title Only Certification, form
#48-0107 and obtaining a Level I Inspection.
Title Only for Non-Probate and Probate
Non-Probate and Probate is not eligible for title only unless they follow standard title only procedures.
View the Titles Without Court Orders topic > Non-Probate section for more information
View the Titles With Court Orders topic > Probate > Documentation Required for a Probate
section for more information and exceptions

Issuing a Title Only in MAX
When the customer is ready to have registration issued after the Title Only has been
processed, use the Title & Reg service and a prompt will display asking 'Do you wish to add a
registration?'

## Titles With Court Orders

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Titles_With_Court_Orders.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Title Documents > Titles With Court
Orders
Titles With Court Orders
Any of these types of services may be done through Title and Registration or Title Only The Ownership
Reason in a Title service will need to be changed to match the type of documentation received.
Bankruptcy
Bankruptcy occurs when a person has more debt than income and files for relief from the creditors
through the court system. The court will issue an Appointment of Trustee or an Appointment of Referee
order for the distribution of property to the applicant and must be submitted at time of transfer.
Documentation Required for a Bankruptcy
Conservator or Guardianship
A court appointed conservator or guardian may sign ownership documents for the disposition of
property belonging to a protected person. The conservator or guardian is at your office to dispose of,
purchase vehicles or trailers.
Documentation Required for Conservator or Guardianship
Divorce Decree
When a vehicle(s) is jointly owned and is awarded to a specific person in a divorce settlement, the
certified court order of the Divorce Decree must be submitted. The Divorce Decree becomes the legal
document used for the transfer of ownership.
A Divorce Decree may specify the placement of a lien (Divorce Decree Lien) rather than a transfer of
ownership.
Documentation Required for a Divorce Decree
Judgment
Judgment paperwork is a court document indicating the vehicle is to undergo an involuntary transfer of
ownership.
The involuntary transfer of ownership is also known as an Operation of Law. This operation of Law
indicates that a person has filed a lawsuit for damages or due case, and the court's decision has ruled
in favor of the applicant (the person who filed).

The court will issue the judgment document to the claimant (the person that owns the items) and the
judgment document becomes the ownership document used to process the T&R transaction.
Documentation Required for a Judgment
Pending Order of Forfeiture
A Pending Order of Forfeiture is where a seizing agency may place a vehicle under constructive
seizure. The seizing agency will notify the Division that a Notice of Pending Forfeiture is issued and will
contain the description of the vehicle by the VIN and one additional item of information, (e.g., current or
prior license plate number, current title number, or name of the owner of record).
The Division will place a Forfeiture Lien incident on the vehicle and will require the lien to be satisfied or
overridden by an upline approver.
A lien release and odometer disclosure statement is not required.
Order of Forfeiture
An Order of Forfeiture is where the court has issued an order to seize a vehicle. The Order will award
ownership of either a vehicle or a major component part of a vehicle.
When a major component part of a vehicle is named in the Order, a Special Serial Number will be
assigned to the vehicle. View the Assign State VIN topic for more information.
Documentation Required for an Order of Forfeiture
Notification of an Order of Forfeiture on Title
Probate
Probate is the administration of the estate of a deceased person by the courts. A personal
representative will be appointed by the court to sign ownership documents for the disposition of property
belonging to a deceased person (administer the estate).
Documentation Required for a Probate
Until we are fully paperless, when you finish the T & R service for the customer, you will print
out the Title Summary (artifact), have the customer review and sign, and re-scan into MAX. This
will replace the process where you print the T&R Application for the customer to sign.

## Titles Without Court Orders

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Titles_Without_Court_Orders.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Title Documents > Titles Without Court
Orders
Titles Without Court Orders
Any of these types of services may be done through Title and Registration or Title Only The Ownership
Reason in a Title service will need to be changed to match the type of documentation received.
Internal Revenue Service (IRS) Transfer of Ownership (Form 2435)
The Division is authorized to issue a title for a vehicle seized and subsequently sold by the United
States Government. The IRS Form 2435 cancels all previously issued ownership documents (i.e., titles,
MCOs, etc.).
Documentation Required for an IRS Form 2435
Non-Probate
A Non-Probate is an affidavit available to an applicant for the purpose of making claim to a decedent’s
personal property and which will not require court paperwork. The affidavit contains a sworn oath and
requirements that the successor (referred to as the AFFIANT on the form) must sign.
When a Non-Probate can be Completed
Documentation Required for a Non-Probate
Customer Entitled to Plate and/or Registration Credit
Legal Status on a Non-Probate (One Owner is Still Living)
Lien Release with a Non-Probate Affidavit
Repossession
Repossession occurs when a lienholder takes possession of a vehicle after the owner of the vehicle
defaults on the terms of the security agreement.
When processing the repossession, the lien will need to be released.
However, if the lienholder has sold the vehicle to a new owner, there may be new lien to
record.
Repossession is limited to a vehicle in which a lien has been “properly filed with the Division.” However,
under certain circumstances, when an innocent third party is not involved, the Division will permit a lien

to be filed at the same time the application and documents for repossession are submitted. View the
Repossession When Lien Was Not Filed section for more information.
For a lien that has been 'properly filed with the Division', the lienholder on record must submit a
completed Repossession Affidavit, form #48-0902 for a vehicle that is physically located in Arizona.
A vehicle that is not currently titled in Arizona must first be titled in the state where the lien was
originally filed or where the vehicle is currently titled. There is an exception for Arizona
licensed dealer, view the Repossession for a Dealer section for more information.
The Division can only set the requirements for the Arizona Repossession Affidavits. Therefore,
the MVD cannot reject out-of-state repossession affidavits just because some fields required
by Arizona are missing.
Repossession for a Lienholder
Repossession for a Dealer
Repossession When Lien Was Not Filed
Out-of-State (OOS) Repossessions
Owner Affidavit, Form #46-4421
A Scrap Metal Dealer or an Automotive Recycler may use Owner Affidavit, form #46-4421 in place of a
title when all the following applies:
The owner does not have the title
The vehicle is at least 12 years old or older
The purchase price is not more than $1,200
The Scrap Metal Dealer or Automotive Recycler is putting the vehicle into their name
The vehicle will be branded as Non-Repairable and a title fee of $4.00 is collected.
View Policies 8.6.2 Title Transfer for more information on Owner Affidavits and the Brand Management
topic >Non-Repairable section for more information on the Non-Repairable brand.
When processing an Owner Affidavit the CSR will select Bill of Sale for the Ownership
Reason.
Sheriff's Sale
When a person is guilty of non-payment of personal property taxes, when the vehicle has been seized
for narcotics violations, or a court-issued writ, the county sheriff's department has the authority to sell
the vehicle.
Documentation Required for a Sheriff's Sale

Standard Form 97
A vehicle that is seized for narcotics, customs, or taxes, becomes the property of the Federal
Government. The United States (US) General Services Administration (GSA), issues the United States
Government Certificate to Obtain Title to a Vehicle (Standard Form 97) to the purchaser. This form
serves as an ownership document and cancels all previous titles and liens on the vehicle.
The United States Government can also use the Transfer Order Excess Personal Property (Form 122)
to transfer motor vehicles only for motor vehicles between federal government agencies and cannot be
used as an ownership document for issuance of a Certificate of Title.
The Arizona Department of Administration (ADOA) is authorized to accept and transfer federal
government surplus property motor vehicles to other state agencies and political subdivisions. A
Standard Form 97 assigned to the State of Arizona, together with a Title Transfer Certificate to the new
owner, is acceptable documentation for transfer.
Documentation Required for a Standard Form 97
Example of Standard Form 97
Until we are fully paperless, when you finish the T & R service for the customer, you will print
out the Title Summary (artifact), have the customer review and sign, and re-scan into MAX.
This will replace the process where you print the T&R Application for the customer to sign.

## Homemade Trailers

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Trailers/Homemade_Trailers.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Trailers > Homemade Trailers
Homemade Trailers
A homemade trailer has been configured, constructed together, and ‘made at home’ by private
individuals. They are made using various materials, and are not shop-made or mass-produced by a
manufacturer.
Title and Registration Requirements
Homemade trailers used for recreational or business purposes and moved on public highways must be
titled and registered.
Every homemade trailer MUST be inspected. The year of the inspection will become the year of
the trailer
The customer declares the weight and the intended usage, not the CSR
The factory list price of the trailer is declared by the customer and noted on the inspection
form in the Remarks area
Ownership documents are required when the declared value, as stated by the owner, is
$1,000 or more
Ownership documents do not have to equal the declared value
If the declared value is less than $1,000, no ownership documents or receipts are required
An Arizona Serial Number (AZ SNO) is assigned and becomes the Vehicle Identification Number
(VIN)
The $5.00 fee* will need to be collected after the service is completed using the Miscellaneous
Purchase service and selectingState Assigned  VIN Fee.
*ONLY for homemade vehicles/trailers, this does not include VINs with less than 17 characters
using the State Assigned VIN Type.
Homemade Trailer and Bonded Title
Inspections
The type of inspection depends on how the trailer was constructed, and if the trailer has an Arizona
Serial Number (AZ SNO), VIN manufacturer's serial or other identifying numbers assigned and intact.
View the Assign State VIN topic for more information on the AZ SNO.

View the Homemade Vehicle Permit service for more information on how to process a permit for a
vehicle or trailer that does not have an assigned VIN.
Level I inspection:
Homemade trailers titled for the first time for the purpose of assigning an AZ SNO
Level II or III inspection:
When a frame or bed from a pickup truck was used to make the homemade trailer
When any type of manufactured vehicle or essential part of a manufactured vehicle was
used to make the homemade trailer
When the trailer's AZ SNO, VIN, manufacturer's serial or other identifying numbers has
been altered, removed, obliterated, defaced, or omitted
If the Level II or III inspector determines a Level I inspection is sufficient for the trailer in
question, the Level II or III inspector will complete a no-fee Level I inspection.
When a vehicle or trailer is commercially built and the VIN plate is lost, a duplicate VIN can sometimes
be issued by ADOT Enforcement Compliance Division (ECD). Enforcement will determine if a duplicate
VIN or AZ Number may be assigned.
Arizona Serial Number (AZ SNO or AZ Number)
An Arizona Serial Number (AZ SNO), or AZ Number, is issued to homemade trailers (and other specially
constructed or reconstructed vehicles) when there is no VIN, or when the assigned AZ Number,
manufacturer's serial number or other identifying numbers have been altered, removed, obliterated,
defaced, or omitted.
View the Assign State VIN topic for more information.
Starting a Title and Registration Service

## Non-Commercial and Commercial Trailers

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Trailers/Noncommercial_and_Commercial_Trailers.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Trailers > Non-Commercial and
Commercial Trailers
Non-Commercial and Commercial Trailers
The Division is authorized to title and register both non-commercial and commercial trailers. Therefore,
depending on the declared gross weight and use of the trailer, customers are issued either a permanent
or annual registration.
Permanent Trailers
Non-commercial and commercial trailers qualify for permanent registration.
Non-commercial permanent trailers are used for personal use and will have a declared gross weight
between 1 to 10,000 lbs., while commercial permanent trailers are used for business use and will have
a declared gross weight of 10,001+ lbs.
Permanent Registration Requirements
The requirements for issuing a permanent registration to a trailer are as follows:
The customer must always declare the weight
Customers with non-commercial permanent trailers less than 10,000 lbs. must certify the trailer
will not be used for business or commercial enterprise either by checking the appropriate box on
the T&R Application (Single), form #96-0236 or by completing the Noncommercial Use Certificate,
form #96-0461
Initial Registration Fees for Permanent Trailers
Subsequent Registration Fees for Permanent Trailers
Trailers NOT Eligible for Permanent Registration
Additional Information on Commercial Permanent Trailers
Non-Permanent Trailers
Commercial non-permanent (annually) trailers are trailers the customer declares will be used for
business purposes or commercial enterprise but will be used at a lower declared gross weight than the
commercial permanent (heavyweight) trailers.
The declared gross weight may be between of 1 to 10,000 lbs. and are titled and registered the same
as a motor vehicle.
Title and Registration Requirements for a Commercial Non-Permanent Trailer

Fees for a Commercial Non-Permanent Trailer
Converting from Permanent Registration to Commercial Non-Permanent
Title and Register Non-Commercial and Commercial Trailers
View the Homemade Trailers topic > Starting a Title and Registration Service for more information on
how to process an title transfer for a trailer.
Until we are fully paperless, when you finish the T & R service for the customer, you will print
out the Title Summary (artifact), have the customer review and sign, and re-scan into MAX. This
will replace the process where you print the T&R Application for the customer to sign.

## Trailer Identification

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Trailers/Trailer_Identification.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Trailers > Trailer Identification
Trailer Identification
There are many different types of trailers the Division titles and/or registers. The type of trailer
determines the title and/or registration eligibility.
Travel or Tent Trailer
A non-powered recreational vehicle designed as temporary living quarters for recreational,
camping, or travel use
Annual registration (1, 2, or 5 years)
Non-Commercial - Permanent
For personal use only; owner must certify the trailer will not be used commercially
Trailer is not a travel or tent trailer
Customer declares the trailer's weight between 1 to 10,000 lbs.
Permanent registration
Commercial - Permanent
For commercial or business use
Customer declares the trailer can hold more than 10,001 lbs.
If this type is selected, the weight of the trailer, the trailer's load, the power unit, and
the power unit's load must be declared on the registration of the power unit
Permanent registration
Commercial - Non-Permanent
For commercial or business use
Customer declares the trailer's weight between of 1 to 10,000 lbs
Annual registration (1, 2, or 5 years)
Weight fees apply
Customer question: What is the weight of your trailer plus the maximum weight you
will load on the trailer?
View the Weight Chart Gross Weight Fees (WGT) for the appropriate Gross Weight
Fee
Homemade Trailer
A trailer that has been configured, constructed together, and ‘made at home’ by private
individuals

Based on use and declared gross weight, the trailer can be registered as any of the above
trailers
For the customer:
You may be cited by law enforcement and fined civil penalties for weight violations.
Additional information regarding civil penalties can be found in A.R.S. 28-5438.
There may be additional related fees for the registration of your commercial or non-commercal
trailer.
Trailer License Plates
All trailers are issued the same Trailer plate format, but have a choice in the size of plate, which
usually depends on the size of the trailer:
Regular trailer plate (6x12)
Small-size trailer plate (4x7) (like a Motorcycle plate)
Trailer plates can be personalized
Maximum number of characters allowed is seven for the full size; five for the small
size
Permanently registered trailers will be given a PERM tab to be displayed on the Trailer plate*
Non-permanently registered trailers will be given a month and year tab to be displayed on the
Trailer plate
*Any permanently registered trailer that has a special and/or personalized plate will receive a
year decal to show the PLATE expiration date.
View the Permanent Registration & Special Plates section for more information.
Permanent Registration & Special Plates
Trailer Fees
Registration fees for trailers will depend on the declared gross weight and the usage of the trailer.
View the Non-Commercial and Commercial Trailers topic or the Travel and Tent Trailers topic for more
detailed information on fees. View the Trailer Registration Reference Chart below for an overview of
trailer fees.
Trailer Registration Reference Chart
The declared gross weight provided by the owner is entered in the Declared GVW field on the
Title and Registration tab during a service and will be displayed in the Declared Weight field
under the Registration section on the Vehicle Information Page.

Data Entry for Trailer Models
When adding a trailer VIN to MAX, VINtelligience will not auto-populate the model for most trailers. View
the Add a Vehicle topic > Adding a Vehicle Type = Trailer section.

## Travel and Tent Trailers

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Trailers/Travel_&_Tent_Trailers.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Trailers > Travel and Tent Trailers
Travel and Tent Trailers
A travel trailer or a tent trailer is a non-powered recreational vehicle designed as temporary living
quarters for recreational, camping, or travel use.
It is not designed for commercial or permanent residence use
It is mounted on wheels
A recreational vehicle that is a combination of two units may not exceed 65 feet in length
Objects that can be removed or collapsed, such as awnings, are not measured when
determining vehicle size
The following criteria is used to establish recreational travel trailer use:
The owner verbally declares the travel trailer use at the time of application
The travel trailer is used only as a temporary dwelling place for recreational use
Title and Registration Requirements
Travel trailers used for recreational purposes and moved on public highways must be titled and
registered.
Customers can choose a 1, 2, or 5 year registration
Weight is NOT declared for travel or tent trailers, therefore no commercial fees will be collected
The trailer size (length and width) must be recorded, which may be done while adding the vehicle
to MAX or on the Vehicle tab during a service
Trailer size determines when an $8.00 Registration fee is collected
MAX automatically calculates the fees based on the trailer size information entered
The table below outlines when a Registration fee is collected

Trailer Size (in feet)
(length x width)
Is the registration fee
collected?
40' x 6'
Yes
39' x 7'
No
40' x 7'
Yes
32' x 8'
Yes
39' x 7' or less
No
Title and Register a Travel or Tent Trailer in MAX
View the Homemade Trailers topic > Starting a Title and Registration Service for more information on
how to process the trailer.
The Length and Width will need to be entered for these types of trailers. Based on this information
determines when the Registration fee will be collected.

Converting TV to a Mobile Home
When a travel trailer (TV) is being used as a mobile home (MH), the TV record may be changed to a
mobile home record. Or a MH record into a TV trailer record.
A centralized Mobile Home Processing Unit will process this request.
For mobile homes being converted to a travel trailer, view the Mobile Home Miscellaneous Information
topic > Mobile Homes & Travel Trailers section.
A 'Tiny Home' can be considered a non-powered recreational vehicle (travel trailer) or a
mobile home when the customer verbally declares that the tiny home meets eligible mobile
home requirements at the time of titling.
View the Mobile Homes Process topic for more information on mobile homes.

## Transfer Plate

`T_&_R/Title_&_Registration/Transfer_Plate.htm`

You are here: Title & Registration Services > Plates > Transfer Plate
Transfer Plate
Transfer plate allows customers to optionally transfer an existing plate, rather than keeping a current
plate or selecting a new plate, as part of a Title & Registration service. Plates can be transferred
between the customer's own vehicles or customers can transfer an existing plate owned by another
customer if it is current or expired for less than one year. When transferring an existing plate from one
customer to another customer a Plate Relinquishment form (a.k.a. Plate Notice, form #48-3302) will be
required as part of the service.
If the plate to transfer is a personalized plate that exists as multiple plate types (e.g. standard plate and
motorcycle plate), you must carefully select the correct plate type during the MAX plate transfer service.
View the Personalized and Special Plates topic > Transferring a Personalized Plate section for more
information on fees and requirements on transferring plates.
If the plate is part of a title service in which in the new owner is entitled to the plate and/or registration
credit, view the Titles Without Court Orders topic > Customer Entitled to Plate and/or Registration Credit
section for more information on how to handle the plate and/or credit.
When an owner is being added to an Arizona vehicle title record, this will begin a new
registration cycle. MAX is acting like adding an owner is a transfer of ownership (partial
transfer) and will require a $12 Plate Transfer fee to be charged on any applicable personalized
and/or special plates.
Transfer Plate function is available in any Registration service in MAX, including:
Title & Reg
Registration Only
Renew Registration
Update Registration
If transferring a plate from a vehicle that is actively registered with that plate number, that
registration will be automatically canceled once the plate is transferred to a different vehicle.
If a customer wants to switch plates on their active registrations (e.g. move plate A to vehicle B
and plate B to vehicle A), see Switching Plates Between Two Active Registrations below.

Completing a Plate Transfer as Part of a Registration Service
Switching Plates Between Two Active Registrations
Transferring a Plate to a Different Vehicle with the Same Owner
Transferring a Plate Between Two Vehicles with Different Owners
